# Turkey–United States relations

For relations before 1922, see [Ottoman Empire–United States relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottoman_Empire%E2%80%93United_States_relations).

Turkey and the United States established diplomatic relations in 1927. After [World War II](32927.md), relations evolved from the [Second Cairo Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Cairo_Conference) in December 1943 and Turkey's entry into World War II on the side of the [Allies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allies_of_World_War_II) in February 1945. Later that year, Turkey became a charter member of the [United Nations](31769.md). Since 1945, both countries have strengthened ties under the [liberal international order](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberal_international_order) advanced by the U.S., a set of global, rule-based, structured relationships grounded in political and economic liberalism. As a consequence, bilateral relations have advanced under the [G20](2206608.md), [OECD](33853117.md), [Council of Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_of_Europe), [OSCE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OSCE), [WTO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WTO), [IMF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IMF), the [World Bank](36752.md), the [Euro-Atlantic Partnership Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euro-Atlantic_Partnership_Council), and [NATO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkey_in_NATO).[^cite_note-Turkey_UN-1]

During the [interwar period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interwar_period) (1918–1939), Turkey and the United States laid the groundwork for cooperation without a defined strategic interest.[^cite_note-ant1-2] The U.S. sent a Congressional delegation to emphasize trade and business, along with non-missionary philanthropy and other cultural enterprises,[^cite_note-ant1-2] such as archaeological expeditions sponsored by American universities.[^cite_note-ant1-2]

During [World War II](32927.md) (1939–1945), oil was set to become increasingly important to American interests. President [Roosevelt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franklin_D._Roosevelt) had a geo-strategic plan in mind at the [Anglo-American Petroleum Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anglo-American_Petroleum_Agreement) in 1944. His successor, President [Truman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harry_S._Truman) stressed the importance of Turkey's geo-strategic location and its "proximity to the great natural resources".[^cite_note-ant1-2] In the following decades, as the United States has been engaged in [oil wars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil_wars) in the Middle East, Turkey has supported the U.S. in some capacity, directly or indirectly, by giving authorization for the use of the [Incirlik Air Base](1597543.md). After World War II, the [Mandate for Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandate_for_Palestine), which was established within the former [Ottoman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottoman_Syria) [vilayet of Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilayet_of_Syria) after the [dissolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dissolution_of_the_Ottoman_Empire) and [partition of the Ottoman Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Partition_of_the_Ottoman_Empire), was terminated by the result of the [Israeli Declaration of Independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_Declaration_of_Independence) on May 14, 1948. The United States recognized Israel on January 31, 1949, and Turkey followed suit on March 28, 1949. Four decades later, Turkey [recognized](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_recognition_of_the_State_of_Palestine) the [Palestinian Declaration of Independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_Declaration_of_Independence) on the same day it was announced – November 15, 1988. Even before the Cold War, relations between Turkey and the United States regarding the Middle East were set on a course based on two dimensions: The first dimension was [petroleum politics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petroleum_politics), and the second dimension was the fate of the [Palestinians](23267.md) ([Palestinian territories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_territories) and a [two-state solution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Two-state_solution)).

During the [Cold War](325329.md) (1945–1991), the [Turkish Straits crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_Straits_crisis) of 1945 developed over the request by [Joseph Stalin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_Stalin) for [Soviet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Union) military bases in the [Turkish Straits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_Straits) as a part of [Soviet territorial claims against Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_territorial_claims_against_Turkey), which prompted the United States to declare the [Truman Doctrine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Truman_Doctrine) in 1947.[^cite_note-Truman_Doctrine-3] In 1947, the [Office of Defense Cooperation Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Office_of_Defense_Cooperation_Turkey) was established as part of the [United States Security Assistance Organizations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Security_Assistance_Organizations) to assist in modernization programs, develop interoperability, promote cooperation, and integrate Turkey into the Western system. As a result of the integration, Turkey became part of the intelligence operations ([1960 U-2](#Soviet_Union_\(CENTO,_U-2_incident\))), and the missile systems ([1962 Missile Crisis](#Soviet_Union_\(Missile_Crisis\))). At the same time, the geopolitical strategy of [Containment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Containment) caused a response from the Soviet Union in the form of increased communist influence in Turkey. In 1964, President [Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lyndon_B._Johnson) disclosed the reluctance among Western powers to defend Turkey in what became known as the [Johnson letter](#Greece_\(Cyprus,_Johnson_letter\)). The U.S. [war on drugs](1181646.md) created a backlash when Turkish farm workers employed in [Turkey's poppy production](#War_on_drugs) lost their jobs. [Anti-Americanism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Americanism) developed among [left-wing extremists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Left-wing_extremists) in Turkey. In 1969, Amb. [Komer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Komer)'s car was set on fire by the [Marxist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marx)-[Leninist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lenin) [Dev-Genç](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dev-Gen%C3%A7) during his visit of [Kurdaş](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mustafa_Kemal_Kurda%C5%9F) at the [Middle East Technical University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_East_Technical_University), in which the nascent institution was developed using the aid given by the United States.[^cite_note-The_New_York_Times-4] In the coming years, Turkey became a front for the clashes between far-left and far-right militant groups, as the [political violence in Turkey (1976–1980)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_violence_in_Turkey_\(1976%E2%80%931980\)) developed as part of the worldwide incidents of [left-wing terrorism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Left-wing_terrorism) aimed at overthrowing the liberal and capitalist governments and replacing them with communist or socialist regimes. One of the organizations involved in the [terrorism in Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terrorism_in_Turkey) was the [Kurdistan Workers' Party](69680.md) (PKK). PKK underwent a significant transformation through its [history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Kurdistan_Workers'_Party), evolving from an opposition group (anti-imperialist, anti-American) to eventually serving as an intermediary for U.S. regional objectives. Another dimension was developed by [Carter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jimmy_Carter)'s [APNSA](44028.md) [Brzezinski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zbigniew_Brzezinski), a devout Roman Catholic whose father was posted to the Soviets during [persecution of Christians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persecution_of_Christians_in_the_Soviet_Union). Brzezinski viewed [political Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_Islam) primarily through a geopolitical lens and argued for the [Green Belt Theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_Belt_Theory) to prevent the spread (e.g., [Operation Cyclone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Cyclone)) using the [plight of Muslims](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deportation_of_the_Crimean_Tatars) as an ideological attack. President [Reagan](25433.md), in a speech to the [National Association of Evangelicals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Association_of_Evangelicals), labeled the Soviet Union an "[evil empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evil_Empire_speech)" based on suppressing religion and human freedom. In the U.S. State Department's [Country Reports on Human Rights Practices](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Country_Reports_on_Human_Rights_Practices), early on, Turkey's secular practices were criticized as being counter to the [freedom of religion in Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_of_religion_in_Turkey). The [Islamic preacher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ulama) [Hoca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hoca) [Fethullah Gülen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fethullah_G%C3%BClen) was imprisoned in 1971 for his religious subversivism by the [secular](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secularism_in_Turkey) [Kemalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kemalism) government. Despite the early pressure, the [Gülen movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G%C3%BClen_movement) continued to gain traction, not just locally but internationally, during [center-right](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Center-right) Presidents [Özal (1989 – 1993)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turgut_%C3%96zal) and [Demirel (1993 – 2000)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%BCleyman_Demirel).[^cite_note-5]. Allegations connecting this movement to the [CIA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CIA) were frequent claims, centering on Fethullah Gülen's [long-term U.S. residency](#Gülen_Movement_\(Ergenekon,_AKP_closure\)) and geopolitical tensions.[^cite_note-6] Both the PKK, based on [separationist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurdish_nationalism) idealogy, and the Gülen movement, based on [moderate Muslim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moderate_Muslim), continued to be an issue past the Cold War. According to the [U.S. State Department](31975.md), the United States has an interest in keeping Turkey anchored to the Euro-Atlantic community.[^cite_note-state.gov-7] During and after the Cold War, the U.S. supported the [accession of Turkey to the European Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Accession_of_Turkey_to_the_European_Union), as Turkey would be aligned with European norms and standards in many domains.[^cite_note-8]

[Post Cold War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Post%E2%80%93Cold_War_era), bilateral relations faced a [shifting dynamic](#Shifting_Dynamics_\(Clean_break,_memorandum\)): morphed into a complex, uneven partnership defined by strategic cooperation, regional divergence, and deep political friction. In 1996, the U.S. developed a [clean break](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A_Clean_Break:_A_New_Strategy_for_Securing_the_Realm) from the Cold War balance of power in the Middle East, in which Jordan and Turkey were to "contain, destabilize, and roll back" entities posing threats.[^cite_note-9] [September 11 attacks](5058690.md) pulled the U.S. into the Middle East. Turkey stood aligned with the U.S. through a [strategic partnership](#Strategic_partnership_during_War_on_Terror). Both countries shared goals in combating radical extremist networks, counterterrorism, and managing security dynamics in the Middle East during the War on Terror. The 1999 imprisonment of [Öcalan with the CIA's](#Syria_\(Terrorism,_Adana_Agreement\)) help was a turning point in Turkey-Syria relations. The 2008 Syria-Israel peace talks were achieved through [Turkey's active engagement](#Syria_\(normalization\)), with Erdogan literally serving as the go-between. Turkey provided full support to the U.S. Afghanistan mission through one of its best diplomats, [Hikmet Çetin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hikmet_%C3%87etin), as the first [NATO Senior Civilian Representative in Afghanistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NATO_Senior_Civilian_Representative_in_Afghanistan), the highest-level political representative of NATO in Kabul, where he had held the command of the [International Security Assistance Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Security_Assistance_Force) (ISAF) during that period. Turkey and the U.S. aligned 100% in Afghanistan. [Wesley Clark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wesley_Clark) cited a [Pentagon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Pentagon) policy and outline of a plan to "take out seven countries in five years," [great-power in the Middle East](#Great-Power_in_the_Middle_East_\(Israel,_Davos_incident\)). Turkey did not align 100% with U.S. goals in Iraq; in both the [1991 Iraq](#Iraq_\(Terrorism\)) and the [2003 Iraq](#Iraq_\(territorial_integrity\)) interventions, it was concerned about territorial integrity. U.S. did not align 100% with Turkey's goals to [peaceful nuclear energy](#Russia_\(Natural_Gas,_Nuclear_Energy\)). The [Turkish public opinion](#Opinion_Surveys) showed a decline in U.S. support after every intervention. Same sentiments were reflected in the Turkish media, and the Amb. [Edelman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eric_S._Edelman) publicly [criticized the depictions](#Valley_of_the_Wolves_\(2003_TV_series\)).

Over the 2010s, the *"[signs of deterioration](#Deterioration_of_the_partnership)"* were visible. Turkey evolved into a much more independent geopolitical player, but at the same time, it became less predictable. As instability in the region increased, Turkey was no longer an "island of stability" for the United States but a source of new, often unexpected impulses on a regional scale. The decade began with Turkey, entangled [with public](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_reactions_to_the_2010_Gaza_flotilla_raid#Turkey) and [legal issues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legal_assessments_of_the_2010_Gaza_flotilla_raid), was met with total honesty and zero softening to Obama \[solve this bloackage issue]: "[we don't need any other problems](#2013_Erdoğan_visit)" following the [nine activists killed](#Palestine_\(Gaza_flotilla_raid\)) during the flotilla raid. Relations in the 2010s looked like a soliloquy that began with the [Turkey migrant crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkey's_migrant_crisis) as [refugees were arriving](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrians_in_Turkey#Refugees_of_the_Syrian_civil_war) mainly from the [Syrian theater](#Syria_\(Civil_War,_territorial_integrity,_safe_zone,_Rat_Line,_YPG\)), in which Turkey's call for safe zone was rejected. Turkey's relations with the US were also affected by the [Iran Nuclear issues](#Iran_\(nuclear_deal,_arms_embargo,_oil_trading_controversy\)) in the early 2010s; the U.S. rejected the "Tehran Deal". Turkey was there when Libya [turned into a failed state](#Libya_\(Intervention\)), and [Gates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Gates) criticized Turkey for not "Holding up its end!" while Turkey criticized the mission goals. It is fair to say that in these conflicts, Turkey and the US did not see eye to eye. The [failed coup d'état](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Turkish_coup_d'%C3%A9tat_attempt#Reasons_for_failure) of 2016 was a major point in the relations; some strong words were [ exchanged between allies.](#Gülen_movement_\(coup_d'état_attempt\)) Turkey's decision-making centers examined how close the coup d'état brought them to fracturing their unity; the [Sèvres syndrome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A8vres_syndrome). When later asked: “Being strong both at the table and on the ground is not an option for us but an obligation," the U.S. "Blocs & Alliances" [were degrading](#Gülen_Movement_\(extradition_–_Flynn_–_Brunson_–_Visa_&_Tariff\)).[^cite_note-10] Turkey decoded [ByLock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ByLock) and quickly investigated the [social structure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_structure). In less than a year, Turkey began purges in the state apparatus. 87 army generals, 32 admirals, and 30 air force generals were sacked from [TAF](30205.md). After bleeding out its command staff, the TAF moved into the [Syrian theater](#Syria_\(Clashes_at_the_Turkish_Ambassador's_Residence,_Refugees,_Pull-out,_Barisha_raid\)) facing America's partner. SOTH [Pelosi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nancy_Pelosi) declared the withdrawal of U.S. forces from advancing TAF as an abandonment of Kurdish partners; she used the word "Betrayal of Allies."[^cite_note-11] During a pool spray, a little over a year ago, Erdogan off the cuff said to VP Biden; "Whether it be PYD, where it be PKK, whether it be YPG, whether it be the HKPC, or whether it be ISIS, ... They're all terrorist organizations at the end of the day, and terrorists will be terrorists regardless of our \[the U.S.] mentalities \[dress them]."[^cite_note-12] An unexpected outcome of the failed coup d'état was related to several rebel F-16s targeting the presidential jet and bombing the parliament, in which the NATO [IFF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Identification_friend_or_foe) system was a factor in any form of resistance to these agents. At the time, the modernization of the Turkish air defense system was an ongoing debate in which the U.S. rejected [C2](7092305.md) technology transfer for Patriot; in 2017, [the decision was made](#Air_Defense_\(Patriot_—_S-400,_F35,_CAATSA\)).

Over the 2020s, the *"[single to multipolar system](#Single_to_Multi-Polar_International_System)"*. A sanctions package, followed by the Armenian Genocide bill, passed on October 29, 2019, following Turkey's offensive into [northeastern Syria](#Deterioration_of_the_partnership). Ranking Rep [McCaul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_McCaul) said, "This will send another signal to Erdogan to stop ethnic cleansing, to stop this aggressive behavior, and I think these two bills together will tie in very well."[^cite_note-13] Second biggest military in NATO [cut off from NATO procurement](#Air_Defense_\(Patriot_—_S-400,_F35,_CAATSA\)). Other NATO members followed with separate export and defense-industry restrictions. Since the cut off neither the [Biden-Turkey](#Air_Defense_\(S400,_F-35,_F-16C\)) nor the [2nd Trump-Turkey](#Air_Defense_\(CAATSA\)) did reverse that decision. In 2021, not Trump but the succeeding [Biden recognized](#Genocide_\(Armenian,_Uygur,_Gaza\)) the [Armenian genocide](140376.md). The political deterioration was evident. Trump's SecState [Pompeo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Pompeo), an outspoken advocate of [Christian Zionism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_Zionism)[^cite_note-14] who considered Turkey a totalitarian Islamist dictatorship,[^cite_note-15] criticized Turkey's position on pre-Islamic [cultural heritage](#Palestine_\(Hamas,_World_Heritage\)). Biden called Erdogan an "Autocrat," and said [support those elements ... get more and embolden ... to take on and defeat](#Biden_administration_\(2021–2025\)) him. The foreign electoral intervention dispute was part of [2023 presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Turkish_presidential_election#Azerbaijani_and_Russian_interference_allegations), mimicking [2016 United States elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_interference_in_the_2016_United_States_elections). Biden, self-declared [Zionist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_Zionism)[^cite_note-16], criticized [Erdogan acting like](#Palestine_\(Hamas,_Peace_talks\)) an [anti-semite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-semite) through a statement from [DoS](31975.md).[^cite_note-17] Notes \[diplomatic] exchanged on [criticism of Israel](30074296.md), [antisemitism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three_Ds_of_antisemitism) and [weaponization of antisemitism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Weaponization_of_antisemitism). There was a short normalization in Israel-Turkey relations with the help of the U.S., [Erdogan-Netanyahu shaking hands](#Israel_\(Normalization,_Gaza_War,_NATO\)). However, the goodwill deteriorated between the two leaders very quickly due to [deteriorating conditions of Palestinians](#Palestine_\(Hamas,_Peace_talks\)). Erdogan called a U.S. ally "any less than what Hitler did," and Netanyahu responded in kind[^cite_note-18] while SecState [Blinken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antony_Blinken) performed multiple trips to the region facing a cold reception.[^cite_note-19]. Erdoğan criticized Biden and [United States support for Israel in the Gaza war](65284610.md) for what he viewed as complicity in Israeli actions in Gaza.[^cite_note-20] The [Fidan](39996069.md)'s [DOS](31975.md) visit and detention of Öztürk over [what she wrote](#Palestine_\(Öztürk\)) coincided. Turkey joined [South Africa's genocide case against Israel](75736183.md), on the other hand the U.S. declared they’re dismantling the ICC[^cite_note-21] On August 17, 2026, [Abu al-Duhur airport bombing](#Israel_\(Truce,_Gaza_Peace_Summit,_Abu_al-Duhur\)) targeted the Turkish forces during the period when Turkish court had an arrest warrant for Netanyahu. In the first quarter of the 21st century, Israel's strategic security needs [obliged Turkey to develop](#Alliances_\(Mecca,_NATO\)) a regional reordering in Mecca, "multi-alignment".

According to the [U.S. Department of State](31975.md), Turkey has been a key partner for U.S. policy in the surrounding region.[^cite_note-state.gov-7] Turkey has partnered with the U.S. for security in Afghanistan ([ISAF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Security_Assistance_Force)), and serves as NATO's vital eastern and southern anchor, controlling (per the [Montreux Convention of 1936](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montreux_Convention_Regarding_the_Regime_of_the_Straits)) the [Turkish Straits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_Straits). Turkey has contributed and continues to contribute to international security alongside U.S. forces in Europe (e.g., in [Bosnia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NATO_intervention_in_Bosnia_and_Herzegovina), [IFOR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Implementation_Force), [KFOR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kosovo_Force), [Baltic Air Policing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baltic_Air_Policing), etc.), and the seas bordering the [Horn of Africa](62142.md). Turkey borders Georgia ([Russo-Georgian War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russo-Georgian_War)), Azerbaijan ([Nagorno-Karabakh conflict](4695860.md)), Iraq ([Iraqi conflict](55579888.md)), Syria ([Syrian civil war](30741795.md)) and Iran ([hostile to the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran%E2%80%93United_States_relations_after_1979) since the [revolution in 1979](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_revolution)). Conflicts in these countries have affected relations between Turkey and the United States.

## Background

See also: [Ottoman Empire–United States relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottoman_Empire%E2%80%93United_States_relations) and [Barbary Wars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barbary_Wars)

After 1780, the United States began relations with North African countries and the [Ottoman Empire](22278.md).[^cite_note-22] In the early 1800s, the US fought the [Barbary Wars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barbary_Wars) against the Barbary states, which were under Ottoman suzerainty. The Ottomans severed diplomatic relations with the United States on April 20, 1917, after the United States declared war against Germany on April 4, 1917, due to the Ottoman–German alliance. The United States re-established normal diplomatic relations with the Ottoman Empire's successor state, Turkey, in 1927.[^cite_note-23]

The [strategic partnership](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strategic_partnership) characterized the exceptionally close economic and military relations between the two during the Cold War and the War on Terror.

### Strategic partnership during Cold War

From 1952 to 1991, the relationship was premised on a "mutuality of benefits".

| United States | Turkey |
| --- | --- |
| Security guarantees against the Soviet Union. | Stationed and based troops and equipment for defensive and intelligence-gathering purposes Grantee of Turkish control (Bosporus and Dardanelles straits) over Soviet access to the Mediterranean Turkish co-belligerency in case of an attack Contain Turkish-Greek tensions. |

Establishment of the [Central Treaty Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Treaty_Organization). [John Foster Dulles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Foster_Dulles) arrives in Ankara for signing of the Baghdad Pact

After participating with United Nations forces in the [Korean War](16772.md), Turkey joined the [North Atlantic Treaty Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Atlantic_Treaty_Organization) in 1952.[^cite_note-25] The second-largest army belongs to [Turkey in NATO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkey_in_NATO) and host of the [Allied Land Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allied_Land_Command) headquarters including the [Incirlik](1597543.md) (1955–present) and [Konya Airbases](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Konya_Air_Base) (2000–present).

Turkey was one of the founding members of the [Central Treaty Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Treaty_Organization) (CENTO), which promoted shared political, military, and economic goals similar to, and modeled after, NATO. The US pressured and promised military and economic aid in exchange for Turkey being a founding member. The US was not a member. The defensive organization never became functional partly due to the lack of leadership, as [John Foster Dulles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Foster_Dulles) ([United States Secretary of State](32293.md)) claimed that the administration could not obtain Congressional approval."[^cite_note-26]

The U.S. actively supported [Turkey's membership bid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Accession_of_Turkey_to_the_European_Union) to join the [European Union](9317.md). It frequently lobbied on behalf of [Ankara](802.md) through its diplomatic missions in the capitals of EU member states.

### Strategic partnership during War on Terror

In 2001, the relationship began with the premise that the United States would foster cooperation on counterterrorism, law enforcement, and military training and education.[^cite_note-27] Turkey remained a close ally of the United States and provided support in the War on Terror.

| Turkey | United States |
| --- | --- |
| Goal: Territorial Integrity | Goal: Achieve stability and a reduced threat of terrorism from Iraq and Afghanistan |
| ? | Joint counterterrorism efforts. Use of Turkish bases and territory for cargo transport. Control-distribution of the arms sales. Involvement of Turkish non-combat troops and trainers in Afghanistan and Iraq. |



*[Global Counterterrorism Forum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Counterterrorism_Forum)*: [SoS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secretary_of_state_\(U.S._state_government\)) John Kerry (left) ([Terrorism in the United States](18563588.md)) and FM [Feridun Sinirlioglu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Feridun_Sinirlioglu) (right) ([Terrorism in Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terrorism_in_Turkey))



[Gulen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fethullah_Gulen) a [moderate Muslim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moderate_Muslim), promoting [interfaith dialogue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interfaith_dialogue).

After the September 11 attacks, President George W. Bush coined the phrase "They hate our way of life (to disrupt and end a way of life).[^cite_note-28]" [Clash of Civilizations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clash_of_Civilizations) was the driving force (rejection of Western universal values), and [Moderate Muslim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moderate_Muslim) was the solution. The US and American foreign policy groups looked for "moderate Muslims" to counter violent extremism, promote democratic values, and build alliances in the Islamic world. [RAND Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RAND_Corporation) suggested funding and supporting secular, liberal, and Sufi Muslim networks to spread pluralistic ideas.[^cite_note-29] [Fethullah Gulen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fethullah_Gulen) and his liberal "Turkish Islam" movement were presented as moderate Islamists. [^cite_note-political-30] It was claimed that Gulen's movement seems to have no aspiration to establish political power.[^cite_note-political-30]

In 2005, [PM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Turkey) [Erdoğan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recep_Tayyip_Erdo%C4%9Fan) sponsored the [Alliance of Civilizations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alliance_of_Civilizations) (AOC) initiative. In 2013, the US and Turkey created a \$200 million fund to help stem extremism by undercutting the ideological and recruiting appeal of jihadists in places like Somalia, Yemen, and Pakistan.[^cite_note-31] The program was based on the role of education in preventing violent extremism and deradicalizing young people. The aim was to develop countering values to prevent violent extremism. It was the first global effort to support local, community-level initiatives aimed at strengthening democratic values and empathy towards the enemies. When asked, [John Kerry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Kerry) defined the strategic goal: "It's a different kind of challenge, and we believe we need to intensify our efforts to address the underlying factors that lead down the path of violence. It's about building foundational security, challenging the narrative of violence to refuse to justify the slaughtering of people."[^cite_note-32]

During the 2010s, the Turkish model was debated [during the Obama presidency](#Arab_Spring_\(Turkish_model,_Gazi_Park\)). The idea of a clash between Western and Islamic civilizations, and hence the Alliance of Civilizations, was criticized as overgeneralizing. Turkey's experience with secularism as envisioned under [Atatürk's reforms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atat%C3%BCrk's_reforms) created a bridge between the [Islamic world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_world) and the West ([Judeo-Christian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judeo-Christian)). However, these concepts, Islam-Christianity-Judaism, are complex and subject to shifting political realities.

### Deterioration of the partnership

|  | United States | Turkey |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [Iraq War](5043324.md) | The "[hood event](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hood_event)" was perceived as an act of U.S. hostility in Turkey. | Turkey denied the U.S. opening of the northern front. |
| [Syrian Civil War](30741795.md) | Openly communicated with the [People's Protection Units](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Protection_Units) (YPG).  Armed the YPG.  [Established an autonomous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Autonomous_Administration_of_North_and_East_Syria) region. | Turkey accepts YPG as the branch of [PKK](69680.md)  Performed [Operation Olive Branch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Olive_Branch) in 2018 and [Operation Peace Spring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Turkish_offensive_into_north-eastern_Syria) in 2019 against YPG.[^cite_note-auto-33]  Determined to destroy the 'Terror Corridor' (autonomous region)[^cite_note-34] |

U.S. [Congressional Research Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congressional_Research_Service) (CRS) stated that "Turkey's relative importance for U.S. policymakers declined in the immediate aftermath of the *Gulf War* and the collapse of the Soviet Union, but focus remained on several regional developments involving Turkey."[^cite_note-2023-crs-35] [CGS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_of_the_Turkish_General_Staff) General [Torumtay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Necip_Torumtay) resigned to prevent Turkey's active [engagement to Gulf War](#Iraq_\(Gulf_War,_Northern_Safe_Zone\)) (1990).[^cite_note-Michael_Robert_Hickok_2000-36] According to CRS, a "reassessment period" was established between 1991 and 2002. CRS timeline for 1991–2002 showed that the U.S. established a "[No-Fly Zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraqi_no-fly_zones_conflict)" in the north of Iraq. It later withheld military loans to Turkey over alleged human rights violations related to the PKK in 1994.[^cite_note-2023-crs-35] In 1997, the U.S. designated the PKK as a foreign terrorist organization, and the PKK's activities stopped following [its leader's capture](#Syria_\(Terrorism,_Adana_Agreement\)) with U.S. assistance in 1999.[^cite_note-2023-crs-35] In 2003, the Turkish parliament did not allow the U.S. invasion of [Iraq from Turkey](#Iraq_\(territorial_integrity\)), and the following year the PKK resumed insurgency and attacks from northern Iraq.[^cite_note-2023-crs-35] [^cite_note-Tr-US-PKK-40]



2014: [Peres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shimon_Peres) asked [Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama) for support in Kurdish statehood.

[^cite_note-41]



2014: “We should … support the Kurdish aspiration for independence,” Netanyahu to [INSS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institute_for_National_Security_Studies_\(Israel\))

[^cite_note-42]

A breakpoint arose on May 31, 2010, after [nine activists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Gaza_Freedom_Flotilla) were killed during the [Gaza flotilla raid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Gaza_flotilla_raid). Obama actively [engaged to repair relations](#Palestine_\(Gaza_flotilla_raid\)), saying, "use flotilla tragedy for Mideast peace". Erdoğan used the description "[this is open-air prison](#Great-Power_in_the_Middle_East_\(Israel,_Davos_incident\))[^cite_note-43]" in 2009 Davos, and Turkey wanted lifting of several restrictions on [Gazans](12047.md).[^cite_note-restrictions-44] AIPAC and J Street opposed relief on the [blockade](19224310.md), and Turkey faced a unified opposition \[bipartisan][^cite_note-45]. Initially, some neoconservatives have called for Turkey's expulsion from NATO.[^cite_note-neoconservatives-46] Later, Tom Rogan from [National Review](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Review) promoted expelling Turkey from NATO as part of his broader efforts to reform the alliance.[^cite_note-47] American libertarians followed them. A senior fellow in defense and foreign policy studies at the [Cato Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cato_Institute), Ted Galen Carpenter, proposed expelling Turkey from the Western alliance.[^cite_note-48] A breakpoint risen in 2015 related to [operations in Syria](#Syria_\(Civil_War,_territorial_integrity,_safe_zone,_Rat_Line,_YPG\)). While claiming Turkey turned a blind eye to ISIL, [Turkey–Islamic State conflict](47320825.md), and other jihadist networks on both sides of its border, the U.S. began openly arming the [People's Protection Units](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Protection_Units) (YPG)[^cite_note-Tr-US-PKK-40], which Turkey recognized as related to the [PKK](69680.md).[^cite_note-veconomist-49] The U.S.'s open support triggered the [2015 police raids in Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_police_raids_in_Turkey). The US-led [Operation Inherent Resolve](44120613.md) against ISIL began on June 15, 2014, five months before Turkey performed the [January 2014 Turkish airstrike in Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/January_2014_Turkish_airstrike_in_Syria). Another breakpoint arose in 2016. Caused by the belief among most Turkish citizens that [America had a hand](#Gülen_movement_\(coup_d'état_attempt\)) in the [2016 Turkish coup d'état attempt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Turkish_coup_d'%C3%A9tat_attempt). It was compounded by the fact that its suspected ringleader, the [Islamic preacher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ulama) [Hoca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hoca) [Fethullah Gülen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fethullah_G%C3%BClen), lives in [Saylorsburg, Pennsylvania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saylorsburg,_Pennsylvania).[^cite_note-veconomist-49] Shortly after the [FETÖ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fethullah%C3%A7%C4%B1_Ter%C3%B6r_%C3%96rg%C3%BCt%C3%BC)'s (the Gülen movement) purges and arrests ([2016–present purges in Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016%E2%80%93present_purges_in_Turkey)), on October 4, 2016, Turkey moved to arrest Turkish nationals employed at American consulates (Metin Topuz on espionage and conspiracy charges), followed on the October 7, 2016, arrest of [pastor](215153.md) and teaching elder [Evangelical Presbyterian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evangelical_Presbyterian_Church_\(United_States\)) [Andrew Brunson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrew_Brunson).[^cite_note-veconomist-49] On October 10, 2016, in regards to Ambs. [Bass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_R._Bass), Turkey declared: "We do not consider the ambassador a representative of the United States," which was a step short of being an [unwanted person](521241.md).[^cite_note-veconomist-49] The response came on May 16, 2017 [McCain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_McCain) and [McCaskill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Claire_McCaskill) called for the expulsion ([unwanted person](521241.md)) of the Turkish Amb. [Kılıç](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serdar_K%C4%B1l%C4%B1%C3%A7),[^cite_note-bbc.com-50] following the [2017 clashes at the Turkish Ambassador's Residence in Washington, D.C.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_clashes_at_the_Turkish_Ambassador's_Residence_in_Washington,_D.C.)[^cite_note-veconomist-49]

[Kurdish Nationalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurdish_Nationalism)

> With President Obama, we had a mutual agreement about the PKK[^cite_note-Tr-US-PKK-40] – but Obama deceived us. I don't believe the Trump administration will do the same.[^cite_note-51]

— [Recep Tayyip Erdoğan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recep_Tayyip_Erdo%C4%9Fan)

Another breakpoint arose in October 2017 following the September 25 [2017 Kurdistan Region independence referendum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Kurdistan_Region_independence_referendum). According to [The Economist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Economist), Turkish-American relations sank to their lowest level in over 40 years.[^cite_note-veconomist-49] Turkey moved to establish [safe zone (Syria)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Safe_zone_\(Syria\)). Turkey performed the [Operation Olive Branch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Olive_Branch) (March 25, 2018 – August 9, 2019) against the YPG[^cite_note-Tr-US-PKK-40]. Turkey established the [Northern Syria Buffer Zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Syria_Buffer_Zone). On August 10, 2018, [first Trump tariffs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Trump_tariffs), Trump tweeted that he would double tariffs on Turkish steel and aluminum under the "[Section 232](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trade_Expansion_Act)," which authorizes tariffs on imports that threaten national security. The [Economy of Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Turkey) fell into the [Turkish economic crisis (2018–current)](57486052.md), driven by both internal structural weaknesses and U.S. sanctions. The [Operation Peace Spring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Turkish_offensive_into_north-eastern_Syria) established the [Second Northern Syria Buffer Zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Northern_Syria_Buffer_Zone) from October 9 to 17, 2019. [Turkey cut off from NATO procurement](#Air_Defense_\(Patriot_—_S-400,_F35,_CAATSA\)) followed by the Armenian Genocide bill passed on October 29, 2019. U.S. lost trust in Turkey as the latter bombed its own military base at the Northern Syria Buffer Zone.[^cite_note-52] On February 5, 2020, the U.S. halted a secretive military intelligence cooperation program with Turkey against the PKK.[^cite_note-reu-drone-53] Turkey had [observation posts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_military_operation_in_Idlib_Governorate#List_of_observation_posts) in the [Idlib demilitarization (2018–2019)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Idlib_demilitarization_\(2018%E2%80%932019\)) zone which held more than 3,000,000 [internally displaced Syrians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internally_displaced_persons_in_Syria) (more than half of them children).[^cite_note-54] On February 27, 2020, [Syrian forces attacked Turkish forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Balyun_airstrikes) at the Idlib demilitarization zone, and military separation between the forces became public after a senior U.S. State Department official argued with the Pentagon over Turkey's request for two Patriot batteries on its southern border.[^cite_note-55] In 2016, Germany, Italy, and the U.S. already pulled out their units. The request was confirmed by Turkish Defense Minister Hulusi Akar.[^cite_note-56] On March 4, 2026, the [U.S. Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USS_Oscar_Austin_\(DDG-79\)) had to use the more expensive [RIM-161 Standard Missile 3](15787389.md) for the first of the [2026 interceptions of Iranian missiles in Turkey](82623663.md) over Iran's decision to attack [American interests and military bases](82751620.md). The use of only long-range precision unit stayed at [Operation Active Fence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Active_Fence), the Spanish Patriots, was engaged on March 8, 2026.



*&#x20;Vice President* [Mike Pence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Pence): "Turkey must choose. Does it want to remain a critical partner in the most successful military alliance in history, or does it want to risk the security of that partnership by making such reckless decisions that undermine our alliance?" \
*Vice President* [Fuat Oktay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fuat_Oktay): "The United States must choose. Does it want to remain Turkey's ally or risk our friendship by joining forces with terrorists to undermine its NATO ally's defense against its enemies?"

[^cite_note-57]

On October 12, 2023, President Biden declared, "particularly the actions by the Government of Turkey to conduct a military offensive into northeast Syria, undermines the campaign to defeat the Islamic State of Iraq and Syria, or ISIS, endangers civilians, and further threatens to undermine the peace, security, and stability in the region". Following this declaration President Biden reestablished Trump's Executive Order 13894 which stated Turkey is an unusual and extraordinary threat to the national security and foreign policy of the United States constituted by the situation in and in relation to Syria.[^cite_note-58] Following the October 1 [2023 Ankara bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Ankara_bombing), Turkish intelligence officials established that the assailants arrived from Syria by paragliders, where they had been trained.[^cite_note-59] Paragliders were because of the [Syria–Turkey barrier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syria%E2%80%93Turkey_barrier). Six days before the declaration, on October 5, Turkey began [bombing their facilities in Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/October_2023_Northern_Syria_clashes).[^cite_note-60] [CJTF–OIR](44618350.md) downed [a Turkish drone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Turkish_drone_shootdown), while doing airstrikes on PKK militants around Hassakeh, which came within 500 m of American troops.[^cite_note-61] A day before the executive order was made public, on October 11, Turkey declared to intensify strikes on the PKK in Iraq and Syria.[^cite_note-62] Turkey showed the same reaction in 2026. Four days before Biden's declaration, on October 7, the Palestinian militant group Hamas [used paragliders](74998317.md#Incursions_into_southern_Israel) for the [Gaza–Israel barrier](478337.md) to lead [an attack on Israel](74998317.md). Since October 7, the Netanyahu government took on the mission to create regional conflicts, which he called a campaign to weaken Iran and its network of armed groups. When the [2026 Iran war](82533582.md) began, Turkey once again moved to block a U.S.–Israeli-backed scheme to deploy the [Kurdistan Free Life Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurdistan_Free_Life_Party) PJAK to destabilize the Iranian government.[^cite_note-63] Turkey considers PJACK [^cite_note-Tr-US-PKK-40] a PKK-affiliated organization. "Kurds are not 'guns for hire, we're diplomats'" was stated by the Deputy Prime Minister of the Kurdistan Regional Government.[^cite_note-64]

### Single to Multi-Polar International System

> It is evident that the Turks do not want to be sidelined by the US or the West when it comes to their own national security concerns. Neither do they want to be hamstrung by easily severed logistics. Clearly, they want to be, and have been thinking about becoming, important players in regional politics, and their public national security policy says as much. They have become involved in multi-national military interventions at every opportunity. They have shown a willingness to defy the US. They have industrial and procurement plans aimed at strategic autonomy.[^cite_note-65]

The 2010s were a [period of deterioration](#Deterioration_of_the_partnership). In 2018, the [Council on Foreign Relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_on_Foreign_Relations) (CFR) recommended that "the U.S. needs to develop alternatives to [Incirlik Air Base](1597543.md), as the use of the base to advance U.S. interests is no longer assured."[^cite_note-Council_on_Foreign_Relations-66] CRS stated, "the U.S. needs to adjust its expectations, ask for less, and develop other options because, despite being a NATO ally, Turkey's interests do not fully align with the U.S.' on all regional or global issues.[^cite_note-Council_on_Foreign_Relations-66] In 2019, the second-biggest military in NATO was cut off [from NATO procurement](#Air_Defense_\(Patriot_—_S-400,_F35,_CAATSA\)). Other NATO members followed with separate export and defense-industry restrictions. Being on CATSAA prevented TAF from acquiring/developing parts, supplies, and NATO-aligned systems, as [debated in the early 2020s](#Air_Defense_\(S400,_F-35,_F-16C\)) over issues such as NATO interoperability and standardization (STANAGs). Seven years later, in 2026, Turkey was still asking NATO allies to lift restrictions.[^cite_note-67]

New U.S. regional alignment. Increase US-Greece cooperation [Trump administration](#Greece_\(Aegean_dispute\)). In 2019, the United States and Greece signed the "Revised Defense Cooperation Agreement". The agreement was described as critical to responding to new security challenges in the eastern Mediterranean Sea. In 2021, the "Greek-American Mutual Defense Cooperation Agreement" permitted the U.S. military to use more bases. In a few short years, Turkey saw NATO powers shift toward its western neighbor. [Anadolu Agency](43356543.md) reported growing US military presence in Greece can lead to undesired scenarios in the Aegean ‘Deploying more US troops to Greece would disrupt NATO's powers,’[^cite_note-aa.com.tr-68] In 2021, [Biden](145422.md), citing Turkey's dispensability, emphasized U.S. relations with Armenia.[^cite_note-69] In 2023, [American Enterprise Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Enterprise_Institute) scholar [Rubin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Rubin_\(historian\)) stated that Biden needs to act to block [Zangezur corridor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zangezur_corridor)[^cite_note-American_Enterprise_Institute-70] Biden started talks with Armenia to establish a military base in the Zangezur corridor.[^cite_note-Azer_news-71] It became [Trump Route for International Peace and Prosperity (2025)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TRIPP) through [Armenia–Azerbaijan peace agreement](79518584.md).

On August 17, 2026, [Abu al-Duhur airport bombing](#Israel_\(Truce,_Gaza_Peace_Summit,_Abu_al-Duhur\)) targeted Turkish forces during the period when a Turkish court had an arrest warrant for Netanyahu. In the first quarter of the 21st century, Israel's strategic security needs [obliged Turkey to develop](#Alliances_\(Mecca,_NATO\)) a regional reordering in Mecca, "multi-alignment".

## History



1943: [Roosevelt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franklin_D._Roosevelt), [İnönü](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C4%B0smet_%C4%B0n%C3%B6n%C3%BC) and [Churchill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Winston_Churchill) at the [Second Cairo Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Cairo_Conference) during [World War II](32927.md).



A Turkish stamp for the 150th anniversary of American Independence, with [İnönü](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C4%B0smet_%C4%B0n%C3%B6n%C3%BC), and [Roosevelt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franklin_D._Roosevelt).

### Truman administration (1945–1953)

One of Turkey's most important international relationships has been with the United States since the end of the Second World War and the beginning of the [Cold War](325329.md).&#x20;

#### Soviet Union (Straits crisis, Truman Doctrine, Korean War, NATO)

Honoring an Ambassador



1946: [USS *Missouri*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USS_Missouri_\(BB-63\)) "taking [Ertegün](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%C3%BCnir_Erteg%C3%BCn) back to his homeland" and [TCG *Yavuz*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SMS_Goeben) escorting

In 1945, the [Turkish Straits crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_Straits_crisis) developed over the request for Russian military bases in the [Turkish Straits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_Straits) as part of [Soviet territorial claims against Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_territorial_claims_against_Turkey). After World War II, the Soviet government pressured Turkey to allow Russian shipping to pass freely through the [Turkish Straits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_Straits). Tensions in the region prompted a display of naval force by the Soviets after Turkey refused to comply with the Soviet Union's requests.

In 1946, [President Truman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harry_S._Truman) returned the body of [Münir Ertegün](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%C3%BCnir_Erteg%C3%BCn), former Turkish Ambassador to Washington (2nd Ambassador of Turkey to the United States), back to Istanbul with the battleship [*Missouri*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USS_Missouri_\(BB-63\)). Missouri, with her 1,515 officers and enlisted men, honored the deceased between March 21, 1946, in Washington, and April 5, in Istanbul, until his body was given to his family. It was a gesture not only to [Turkish foreign service](10283896.md) but also to demonstrate that the US was defending Turkey against Soviet threats.[^cite_note-72]

[Truman Doctrine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Truman_Doctrine)



1947: Recommendation for assistance



1948: Situation Report

1949: Politics, economy & industry

1952: Politics, economy & industry

In 1947, British assistance to Turkey ended. The U.S. dispatched military aid to ensure that Turkey would retain chief control of the passage. Turkey began to associate with the United States in 1947 when the [United States Congress](31756.md) designated Turkey, under the provisions of the "Truman Doctrine", as the recipient of special economic and military assistance intended to help it resist threats from the Soviet Union.[^cite_note-FRD1996-73]

The [Truman Doctrine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Truman_Doctrine) was an American foreign policy that sought to counter Soviet geopolitical expansion during the Cold War. The Truman Doctrine prioritized adherence to democracy as a strategic goal. Truman used the advancement of liberal Democracy to secure congressional aid for Turkey. Truman's strategic imperatives and democratic rhetoric against communist ideology held that, because of US assistance against the Soviets, Turkey moved away from a single-party system towards a multi-party system. There is no causal link between Turkey's democratization and either the Truman Doctrine or Turkey's admission to the North Atlantic Treaty Organization (NATO).[^cite_note-Kayaoğlu-74] In [Atatürk's Reforms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atat%C3%BCrk's_Reforms), [Mustafa Kemal Atatürk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mustafa_Kemal_Atat%C3%BCrk) envisioned a multi-party-based system. However, Turkey was a "de facto single-party state" (the correct term is [one-party period of the Republic of Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/One-party_period_of_the_Republic_of_Turkey)) as the [Republican People's Party](404748.md) effectively prevented the opposition from winning elections. Turkey held the first multi-party elections in 1946. [Multi-party period of the Republic of Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multi-party_period_of_the_Republic_of_Turkey) began with the [Democratic Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democrat_Party_\(Turkey,_historical\)) government of [Adnan Menderes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adnan_Menderes).[^cite_note-Kayaoğlu-74]

The U.S. sought the [containment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Containment) strategy at the beginning of the Korean War to defend [Republic of Korea (ROK)](27019.md) from a [communist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist) invasion by [Democratic People's Republic of Korea (DPRK)](21255.md). In support of the US' overall Cold War strategy, Turkey contributed to the United Nations forces in the [Korean War](16772.md) (1950–53).[^cite_note-FRD1996-73] On October 19, 1950, with more than 4,500 troops [Turkish Brigade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_Brigade) was attached to the [U.S. 25th Infantry Division](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/25th_Infantry_Division_\(United_States\)). The Turkish Brigade was the only foreign unit of its size permanently attached to a U.S. division (Brothers in Arms). Turkey replaced/replenished this Brigade each year (15,000 combat soldiers and a total of 21,212) until mid-summer of 1954. A mutual interest in containing Soviet expansion provided the foundation of US–Turkish relations for the next four decades.[^cite_note-FRD1996-73]

[Containment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Containment): US and Turkey at [Korean War](16772.md)

Dec 1–2, 1950: Row footage. [Turkish Brigade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_Brigade) supported by [1st Cavalry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1st_Cavalry_Division_\(United_States\)) at [Sunchon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunchon)



December 15, 1950: Brigade General [Yazıcı](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tahsin_Yaz%C4%B1c%C4%B1) receiving [Silver Star](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silver_Star) from Lieutenant General [Walker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walton_Walker)

After extensive study and debate on extending the alliance to the eastern Mediterranean, the admission of Greece and Turkey to NATO on February 18, 1952, was finalized. The membership of these two countries created NATO's new southern flank. Changes were needed to the treaty's wording to expand its territorial reach. Turkey's admission to NATO gave NATO a much longer land border with the [Warsaw Pact](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warsaw_Pact). The 1936 [Montreux Convention Regarding the Regime of the Straits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montreux_Convention_Regarding_the_Regime_of_the_Straits) gave Turkey control over the [Turkish Straits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_Straits), through which the [Soviet Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Navy)'s [Black Sea Fleet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_Sea_Fleet) had access to the Mediterranean. At the same time, Turkey brought to the alliance its second-largest military workforce after the United States, in addition to access to sites for forward deployment and intelligence gathering.[^cite_note-FRD1996-73]

#### Marshall Plan (economic recovery & industrial modernization)

[Marshall Plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marshall_Plan)



Informational panel about the [Marshall Plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marshall_Plan) displayed at [Haydarpaşa Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haydarpa%C5%9Fa_Station), 1951



Agricultural support to Turkey

Since [Atatürk's Reforms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atat%C3%BCrk's_Reforms), Turkey has wanted to be part of the Western alliance. Turkey also intended to enter into the Western economic structure. The Truman Doctrine aimed to prevent the spread of communism in Turkey. It aimed to change Turkey's isolated economy (statist structure) to move into a liberal system. Marshall subsidies created economic added value in Turkey. The Marshal Plan also directed Turkey toward higher military spending (the second-largest military in NATO) because it was seen mainly as a buffer state against the Soviet Union and the Middle East.[^cite_note-75]

Turkey received \$111 million in economic and military aid, and the U.S. sent the [aircraft carrier *Franklin D. Roosevelt*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USS_Franklin_D._Roosevelt).  The Marshall Plan intended to direct Turkey towards developments in the agriculture, mining, and tourism sectors. Tractors and other agricultural equipment entered the country, sparking a production boom. The aid to the Turkish military went back to the US military-industrial complex. .[^cite_note-76]

### Eisenhower administration (1953–1961)



1954\:President [Celal Bayar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Celal_Bayar) receives a standing ovation after his speech before a joint session of Congress. Behind him are Vice-President [Nixon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Nixon) and Speaker of the House [Rayburn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sam_Rayburn)

1959: President Eisenhower meeting PM Menderes

#### Soviet Union (NATO, CENTO, U-2 incident)

Turkey was a founding member of the [Central Treaty Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Treaty_Organization) (CENTO) collective defense pact established in 1955, and endorsed the principles of the 1957 [Eisenhower Doctrine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eisenhower_Doctrine).[^cite_note-FRD1996-73] In the 1950s and 1960s, Turkey generally cooperated with other United States allies in the Middle East ([Iran](14653.md), [Israel](9282173.md), and [Jordan](7515964.md)) to contain the influence of countries ([Egypt](8087628.md), [Iraq](7515928.md), and [Syria](7515849.md)) regarded as Soviet clients.[^cite_note-FRD1996-73] Throughout the Cold War, Turkey was the bulwark of [NATO](21133.md)'s southeastern flank and directly bordered [Warsaw Pact](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warsaw_Pact) countries.

[1960 U-2 incident](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1960_U-2_incident) was a [U-2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lockheed_U-2) [spy plane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reconnaissance_aircraft) shot down by the [Soviet Air Defence Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Air_Defence_Forces) while performing photographic [aerial reconnaissance](20338498.md) deep into Soviet territory. On April 28, 1960, a U.S. [Lockheed U-2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lockheed_U-2)C spy plane, Article 358, was ferried from [Incirlik Air Base](1597543.md) in Turkey to the US base at Peshawar airport by pilot Glen Dunaway. Fuel for the aircraft had been ferried to [Peshawar](66230.md) the previous day in a US Air Force [C-124](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Douglas_C-124_Globemaster_II) transport. A [US Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_Air_Force) [C-130](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C-130) followed, which carried the ground crew, mission pilot [Francis Powers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francis_Gary_Powers), and backup pilot Bob Ericson. On the morning of April 29, the crew in Badaber was informed that the mission had been delayed by one day. As a result, Bob Ericson flew Article 358 back to Incirlik, and John Shinn ferried another U-2C, Article 360, from Incirlik to Peshawar. On April 30, the mission was delayed one more day due to bad weather over the Soviet Union.[^cite_note-PocockU2-77] On May 1, Captain Powers left the base in Peshawar on a mission with the operations code word GRAND SLAM. Four days after Powers' disappearance, [NASA](18426568.md) issued a very detailed press release noting that an aircraft had "gone missing" north of Turkey.[^cite_note-Russian_officer-78]

On May 13, the Soviet Union sent complaints to Turkey, which in turn protested to the United States. Turkey received assurances that no U.S. aircraft would be allowed to be used for unauthorized purposes.

### Kennedy and Johnson administrations (1961–1969)

#### Soviet Union (Missile Crisis)

[Nuclear umbrella](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_umbrella)



1961- IRBMs to Turkey



1962: [VP](32759.md) [Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lyndon_Johnson) and [EB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Board_of_directors) of the [Justice Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Justice_Party_\(Turkey\)) [Demirel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%BCleyman_Demirel)



1963: President [Kennedy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_F._Kennedy) with [TM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Treasury_and_Finance) [Ferit Melen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ferit_Melen) & Deputy PM [Ekrem Alican](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ekrem_Alican)

Turkey risked nuclear war on its soil during the Cuban Missile Crisis. It was a 13-day confrontation between the United States and the Soviet Union initiated by the American discovery of Soviet [ballistic missile](38025.md) deployment in [Cuba](5042481.md). In response to the failed [Bay of Pigs Invasion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bay_of_Pigs_Invasion) of 1961 and the presence of American [Jupiter ballistic missiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PGM-19_Jupiter) in Italy and Turkey, Soviet leader [Nikita Khrushchev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nikita_Khrushchev) agreed to Cuba's request to place nuclear missiles on the island to deter a future invasion. [John F. Kennedy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_F._Kennedy) and Khrushchev reached an agreement. Publicly, the Soviets would dismantle their offensive weapons in Cuba, in exchange for a US public declaration and agreement to avoid invading Cuba again. Secretly, the United States agreed to dismantle all US-built [Jupiter MRBMs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PGM-19_Jupiter) that had been deployed in Turkey and Italy against the Soviet Union.[^cite_note-79]

In 2017, *[The Putin Interviews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Putin_Interviews)* claimed that the placement of Russian missiles in Cuba was a Russian reaction to the earlier stationing of American missiles in Turkey in 1961–62; Khrushchev attempted to achieve a balance of power.[^cite_note-80]

#### Greece (Cyprus, Johnson letter)

1963: President [Kennedy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_F._Kennedy) addressing the Turkish people on [Atatürk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mustafa_Kemal_Atat%C3%BCrk) on the [Republic Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_Day_\(Turkey\))

The [Cyprus Emergency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyprus_Emergency) was a [conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conflict_\(process\)) fought in [British Cyprus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Cyprus) between 1955 and 1959. The [National Organization of Cypriot Fighters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EOKA) (EOKA), a [Greek Cypriot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greek_Cypriot) right-wing nationalist [guerrilla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guerrilla) organization, began an armed campaign in support of ending [British colonial rule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Cyprus) and enabling the [unification of Cyprus and Greece](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enosis) (*Enosis*) in 1955. Opposition to *Enosis* among [Turkish Cypriots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_Cypriots) led to the formation of the [Turkish Resistance Organisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_Resistance_Organisation) (TMT) in support of the [partition of Cyprus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taksim_\(politics\)) and in the mid-1960s relations worsened between Greek and Turkish communities on Cyprus. Britain wanted to hand the crisis and a peacekeeping role to either NATO or UN forces. [US President Lyndon B. Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_of_Lyndon_B._Johnson) tried to prevent a Turkish invasion of Cyprus and war between them. American diplomat [George Ball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Ball_\(diplomat\)) found [Archbishop Makarios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Makarios_III), president of Cyprus, difficult to deal with, as he commonly rejected the advice.&#x20;

The Americans secretly talked to General [Georgios Grivas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Georgios_Grivas), leader of the [EOKA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EOKA) guerrilla organization. While invasion and war did not occur, the U.S. alienated both the Greek and Turkish governments and drove Makarios closer to the Russians and Egyptians.[^cite_note-81][^cite_note-82] The Cyprus Emergency ended in 1959 with the signing of the [London-Zürich Agreements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/London-Z%C3%BCrich_Agreements), establishing the [Republic of Cyprus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_Cyprus) as a non-partitioned independent state separate from Greece.

On June 5, 1964, President LBJ sent a letter to Turkish Premier [İsmet İnönü](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C4%B0smet_%C4%B0n%C3%B6n%C3%BC), known as the "Johnson letter." The most important point was NATO would be reluctant to defend Turkey.[^cite_note-83]

> Washington, June 5, 1964, 12\:15 a.m.
>
> Dear Mr Prime Minister:\
> &#x20;I am gravely concerned by the information which I have had through Ambassador Hare from you and your Foreign Minister that the Turkish Government is contemplating a decision to intervene by military force to occupy a portion of Cyprus. I wish to emphasize, in the fullest friendship and frankness, that I do not consider that such a course of action by Turkey, fraught with such far-reaching consequences, is consistent with the commitment of your Government to consult fully in advance with us. ... (1)**NATO Allies have not had a chance to consider whether they have an obligation to protect Turkey.** ... (2) your Government is required to obtain United States consent for the use of military assistance for purposes other than those for which such assistance was furnished. ... I do feel that you and I carry a very heavy responsibility for the general peace and for the possibilities of a sane and peaceful resolution of the Cyprus problem. I ask you, therefore, to delay any decisions which you and your colleagues might have in mind until you and I have had the fullest and frankest consultation.
>
> &#x20;Sincerely, Lyndon B. Johnson

— [Lyndon B. Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lyndon_B._Johnson), President Johnson's letter to Prime Minister Inonu, The letter was released by the White House in January 1966 and printed in Middle East Journal 20 (1966), pp. 386–393.

Item 1 was the critical American position toward Turkey. Item 2 was the wake-up call. Johnson revealed to Turkey's political elite that the TAF overly depended on the US.[^cite_note-jfb-84] Inonu knew that he would receive a strong message. The letter leaked to the press before the messenger left the building. Inonu created a reference point for the nature and intention of American policy on Turkey.[^cite_note-jfb-84] In the following decades, Johnson's Letter became the first-level reference when an American policy regarding Turkey was analyzed.[^cite_note-jfb-84] The unintended consequence of the Johnson letter showed in the public debates. What would American assistance look like if the US wanted to defend Turkey?

The US ordered the Sixth Fleet to Istanbul in August 1968. The Turkish public perceived the visit as a follow-up to the Johnson letter. The labor federations and the other left-wing groups protested the fleet. Those were the same groups that presented a heartfelt welcome in the late 1950s.[^cite_note-jfb-84]

### Nixon and Ford administrations (1969–1977)



[Richard Nixon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Nixon) and [Nihat Erim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nihat_Erim)

#### War on drugs

The [war on drugs](1181646.md) is the policy of a global campaign to reduce the illegal drug trade in the United States. In controlling the [illegal drug trade in Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illegal_drug_trade_in_Turkey), the government signed the [Single Convention on Narcotic Drugs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Single_Convention_on_Narcotic_Drugs) in 1961, and the parliament ratified it in 1967. In 1971, Turkey imposed a ban on opium production as a result of considerable U.S. government pressure. The U.S. provided a total of \$35.7 million in support for the transition of 70,000 farm workers to cultivating other crops. The Turkish government implemented the program under pressure from farmers who claimed lost revenue. Nationalists claimed the program was imposed under foreign pressure. During the [1973 Turkish general election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1973_Turkish_general_election), all political parties expressed dissatisfaction with the campaign. In 1974, Turkey moved to a system for licensing poppy production in which the state purchased all crops (no poppy market) and used them for the legal production of [pharmaceuticals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pharmaceuticals) by state-owned institutions (no sale to private industry).[^cite_note-85]

#### Greece (Cyprus, Arms embargo)

Turkey and Greece



1975: Ford & Prime Minister Demirel



1976: Ford & Foreign Minister Caglayangil



1976: Ford & Prime Minister [Ecevit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B%C3%BClent_Ecevit)



1976 Ford on the New York assassination attempt on Ecevit. Ecevit on the Cyprus problem.

After the [1974 Cypriot coup d'état](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1974_Cypriot_coup_d'%C3%A9tat) (backed by the [Cypriot National Guard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cypriot_National_Guard) and the [Greek military junta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greek_military_junta_of_1967%E2%80%931974)), on July 20, 1974, Turkey invaded [Cyprus](5593.md), claiming it was protecting the safety of Turkish Cypriots following the [Treaty of Guarantee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Guarantee_\(1960\)). The Turkish military occupied the northern third of Cyprus ([Turkish invasion of Cyprus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_invasion_of_Cyprus)), dividing the island along what became known as the [Green Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_Line_\(Cyprus\)) monitored by the United Nations, defying the ceasefire.

Turkey repeatedly claimed, for decades before the invasion and frequently afterward, that Cyprus was of vital strategic importance to it. Ankara defied a host of UN resolutions demanding the withdrawal of its occupying troops from the island. About 142,000 Greek Cypriots living in the north and 65,000 Turkish Cypriots living in the south were forcibly expelled and were forbidden to return to their homes and properties. 109 Turkish villages were destroyed, and 700 Turks were kept as hostages. Daily Telegraph described events as an anti-Turkish pogrom.[^cite_note-86]

The United States imposed an arms embargo on Turkey in response, and relations between the two countries suffered significantly.[^cite_note-87] The US could not achieve its desired goal by imposing an embargo on Turkey. However, it caused Turkey to invest in its defense industry.[^cite_note-usarmsembargo-88] Soviets saw the expansion of [Warsaw Pact](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warsaw_Pact) could have a chance in the region.[^cite_note-ant5-89]

#### Soviet Union (Cold War)

In 1969, Ambs. [Komer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Komer)'s car was set on fire by the [Marxist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marx)-[Leninist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lenin) [Dev-Genç](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dev-Gen%C3%A7)[^cite_note-ant5-89] during his visit of [Mustafa Kemal Kurdaş](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mustafa_Kemal_Kurda%C5%9F) at the [Middle East Technical University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/METU). The nascent institution developed using the aid given by the United States.[^cite_note-The_New_York_Times-4] Former [CIA](5183633.md) [counterintelligence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Counterintelligence) officer [Aldrich Ames](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aldrich_Ames) decoded the organization as a part of the [CIA activities in Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CIA_activities_in_Turkey).[^cite_note-ant5-89]

In the coming years, Turkey became a front for the clashes between far-left and far-right militant groups, as the [political violence in Turkey (1976–1980)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_violence_in_Turkey_\(1976%E2%80%931980\)) took place simultaneously with the worldwide incidents of [left-wing terrorism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Left-wing_terrorism) aimed at overthrowing the liberal and capitalist governments and replacing them with communist or socialist regimes.

NATO support manifested in the establishment of a clandestine [stay-behind](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stay-behind) army, called the "[Counter-Guerrilla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Counter-Guerrilla)", under [Operation Gladio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Gladio#Turkey).

#### Armenia (Terrorist attacks)

The [Armenian Secret Army for the Liberation of Armenia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenian_Secret_Army_for_the_Liberation_of_Armenia) (ASALA), listed as a [terrorist organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terrorist_organization)[^cite_note-asala1-90][^cite_note-asala2-91][^cite_note-asala3-92][^cite_note-asala4-93][^cite_note-asala5-94] by the [United States Department of State](31975.md),[^cite_note-DeptState1989-95] carried out attacks against Turkish diplomats and [Turkish Airlines](167925.md) offices between 1975 and 1991. The precursor to the foundation of ASALA was the assassination of Turkish Consul General [Mehmet Baydar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mehmet_Baydar) and Turkish Consul [Bahadır Demir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bahad%C4%B1r_Demir) by Armenian American [Kourken Yanigian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kourken_Yanigian), who invited them to a luncheon on January 27, 1973, in [Santa Barbara, California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Barbara,_California), United States. Yanigian was sentenced to life imprisonment,[^cite_note-96] but many [Armenian Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenian_Americans) expressed support for his views. [Numerous attacks by ASALA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_attacks_by_ASALA) were carried out in the United States, Europe, and the Middle East.

### Carter administration (1977–1981)

The geopolitical changes in the Middle East, like the [Iranian Revolution](347268.md), force the arms embargo was silently removed a few years later with the contribution of the National Security Advisor Zbigniew Brzezinski discussed with his staff a possible American invasion of Iran by using Turkish bases and territory if the Soviets decided to repeat the Afghanistan scenario in Iran, although this plan did not materialize.
