During the 1980s, relations between Turkey and the United States gradually recovered.[^cite_note-DeptState1989-95] In March 1980, Turkey and the US signed the Defense and Economic Cooperation Agreement (DECA), in which the United States was granted access to 26 military facilities in return for Turkey's ability to buy modern military hardware and \$450 million.[^cite_note-97]

#### Syria (Terrorism)

See also: [Syria–United States relations](15996874.md) and [Syria–Turkey relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syria%E2%80%93Turkey_relations)

[Terrorism in Ba'athist Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terrorism_in_Ba'athist_Syria). Syria has been on the [U.S. list of state sponsors of terrorism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Sponsors_of_Terrorism_\(U.S._list\)) since the list's inception in 1979 and is deemed a "safe haven" for terrorists.

The [1983 Orly Airport attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1983_Orly_Airport_attack) by ASALA in [Paris](22989.md), [France](5843419.md), resulted in the deaths of 8 people and injuries to 55 others, which caused widespread international condemnation. The United States, France, and numerous other countries began listing ASALA as a terrorist organization.[^cite_note-asala1-90][^cite_note-asala2-91][^cite_note-asala3-92][^cite_note-asala4-93][^cite_note-asala5-94] |After the 1983 Orly Airport attack, ASALA began to reduce its activities and eventually went underground. During [PKK-Syria (1980–1984)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Kurdistan_Workers'_Party#Syria_\(1980%E2%80%931984\)), between 1982 and 1984, the PKK had received armed combat training by ASALA at the [Beqaa Valley](2162385.md) in [Lebanon](17771.md) and by the [Assad regime](57134800.md) in [Syria](7515849.md).[^cite_note-98][^cite_note-Simon-99] In 1984, the [Kurdistan Workers' Party](69680.md) (PKK), designated as a [Foreign Terrorist Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_State_Department_list_of_Foreign_Terrorist_Organizations) by the [U.S. State Department](31975.md),[^cite_note-100] declared itself a paramilitary group and began staging attacks in the villages and towns of southeastern and eastern Turkey. In 1986, the U.S. withdrew Amb. [Scotes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_J._Scotes) in response to evidence of direct Syrian involvement in an attempt to blow up an Israeli airplane. After Syria expelled the [Abu Nidal Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abu_Nidal_Organization) and helped free an American hostage earlier, the U.S. assigned [Richard W. Murphy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_W._Murphy) to [Damascus](8914.md) in 1987.[^cite_note-101] At the same period, Turkey openly threatened Syria over its support for the PKK.[^cite_note-102] Turkey claimed that Syria employed former [Schutzstaffel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Schutzstaffel) officer [Alois Brunner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alois_Brunner) to train militants.[^cite_note-103]

PKK leader [Abdullah Öcalan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdullah_%C3%96calan) was hosted in [Damascus](8914.md) until 1998.

### George H. W. Bush administration (1989–1993)



President [George H. W. Bush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_H._W._Bush) and President [Turgut Özal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turgut_%C3%96zal) take a cruise on the [Bosporus](3705.md) strait in [Istanbul](3391396.md) (July 21, 1991)

President [Turgut Ozal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turgut_Ozal) believed Turkey's future security depended on the continuation of a strong relationship with the United States.[^cite_note-FRD1996-73] Although Ankara resented continued attempts by the United States Congress to restrict military assistance to Turkey because of Cyprus and the introduction of congressional resolutions condemning the [Armenian genocide](140376.md), the [Özal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turgut_%C3%96zal) government generally perceived the administration of President [George H. W. Bush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_H._W._Bush) as sympathetic to Turkish interests.[^cite_note-FRD1996-73] At this time, [Turkish Aerospace Industries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_Aerospace_Industries) (TAI) was established and it started to build [F-16 Fighting Falcon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F-16_Fighting_Falcon) jets under license in Turkey. Washington demonstrated its support of Özal's market-oriented economic policies and efforts to open the Turkish economy to international trade by pushing for acceptance of an [International Monetary Fund](15251.md) program to provide economic assistance to Turkey.[^cite_note-FRD1996-73] Furthermore, the United States, unlike European countries, did not persistently and publicly criticize Turkey over allegations of [human rights violations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_rights_violations),[^cite_note-FRD1996-73] nor did it pressure Özal on the [Kurdish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurdish_people) problem. By 1989 the United States had recovered a generally positive image among the Turkish political elite.[^cite_note-FRD1996-73]

#### Post–Cold War (Dual containment, Turn to east)

The [post–Cold War era](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Post%E2%80%93Cold_War_era) refers to the period following the [dissolution of the Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dissolution_of_the_Soviet_Union) in December 1991. The end of the [Cold War](325329.md) forced Turkey to reassess its international position. The disappearance of the [Soviet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Union) threat and the perception of being excluded from Europe created a sense of vulnerability with respect to Turkey's position in the fast-changing global political environment.[^cite_note-FRD1996-73] Turkey was an [associate member](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ankara_Agreement) of the [EEC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Economic_Community). During the Cold War, the U.S. maintained a balance-of-power policy between Iran and Iraq, which later shifted to [Dual containment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dual_containment), containing these countries to prevent negative influence on the international community. The previous balance-of-power policy effectively ended, as the Soviet Union was no longer around to serve as a security benefactor for either country. Like the U.S., Turkey also adapted to the changing security concerns in the region. President Özal modified the main principles of Turkish foreign policy towards the Middle East since the foundation of the republic, namely non-interference in intra-Arab disputes and wider Middle Eastern affairs. Turkey became proactive about its role in resolving regional conflicts. Turkey offered ideas and advice on regional issues of common concern to both countries.[^cite_note-FRD1996-73]

The role Turkey played during the Gulf War demonstrated to the public that it was one of the key actors in the region.[^cite_note-104] Turkey expanded ties with the Central Asian members of the [CIS](36870.md).[^cite_note-FRD1996-73]

#### Palestine

Turkey supported the [Arab–Israeli peace process](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli-Arab_cooperation_projects).

#### Iraq (Gulf War, Northern Safe Zone)



The [Kirkuk–Ceyhan Oil Pipeline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kirkuk%E2%80%93Ceyhan_Oil_Pipeline), [Ramadan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taha_Yassin_Ramadan) and Özal

[Iraq–United States relations](17681177.md), [Iraq–Turkey relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraq%E2%80%93Turkey_relations)

The Iraq's [Taha Yassin Ramadan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taha_Yassin_Ramadan), said he come to an opinion that Turkey would remain neutral in the conflict after meeting with President Turgut Ozal.[^cite_note-105] President Özal supported the United States' position during the [Gulf War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_War) (August 2, 1990 – January 17, 1991). Turkey's economic ties to Iraq were extensive and their disruption hurt the country.[^cite_note-FRD1996-73] Turkey lost approximately \$60 billion by closing the [Kirkuk–Ceyhan Oil Pipeline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kirkuk%E2%80%93Ceyhan_Oil_Pipeline) during the conflict. Just before the war, Chief of the Turkish General Staff General [Necip Torumtay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Necip_Torumtay) resigned in disagreement with the involvement of Turkish ground forces in the conflict,[^cite_note-Michael_Robert_Hickok_2000-36] which prevented Turkey's active military engagement.

Turkey allowed United Nations forces ([UN SC Resolution 665](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_665)) to fly missions from its air bases; by doing so Turkey remained a platform for the US attacks against Iraq for the rest of the conflict. Turkey played a role in the war by restraining a sizeable proportion of the Iraqi army on the Turkey–Iraq border.

After the war, Turkey continued to support major United States initiatives in the region, including the creation of a safe zone for Iraqi Kurds over northern Iraq. [Turkey received a large number of Iraqi Kurdish refugees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkey's_migrant_crisis#Influx_from_the_Gulf_War) following the [1991 uprisings in Iraq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1991_uprisings_in_Iraq) (March 1 – April 5, 1991). The [Iraqi no-fly zones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraqi_no-fly_zones) were two no-fly zones (NFZs) proclaimed by the United States, the United Kingdom, and France to create safe zones for internally displaced people after the war. The US and the UK claimed authorization for the NFZ under [United Nations Security Council Resolution 688](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_688), though not in its text. The US stated that the NFZs were intended to protect the ethnic Kurdish minority in northern Iraq and Shiite Muslims in the south. Turkey opened its Incirlik and Diyarbakır air bases and became involved in the ground support and intelligence operations for the northern NFZ, which was initially part of [Operation Provide Comfort](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Provide_Comfort)'s relief operations before being succeeded by [Operation Northern Watch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Northern_Watch).

The United States' use of Turkish military installations during the bombing of Iraq in 1991 led to anti-war demonstrations in several Turkish cities, and sporadic attacks on United States facilities in 1992 and 1993.[^cite_note-FRD1996-73] NFZs also enabled a safe haven for PKK. Turkey performed cross-border operations into northern Iraq:

- [Operation Northern Iraq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Northern_Iraq): October 12 – November 1, 1992

### Clinton administration (1993–2001)

In January 1995, Turkey's political elite had reached a consensus that the country's security depended on remaining a [strategic ally](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strategic_alliance) of the United States. For this reason, both the [Demirel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%BCleyman_Demirel) ([49](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/49th_government_of_Turkey)) and [Çiller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tansu_%C3%87iller) ([50](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/50th_government_of_Turkey), [51](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/51st_government_of_Turkey), [52](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/52nd_government_of_Turkey)) governments made efforts to cultivate relations with the administrations of presidents [George H. W. Bush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_H._W._Bush) and [Bill Clinton](3356.md).[^cite_note-FRD1996-73]

#### Shifting Dynamics (Clean break, memorandum)

Shifting Dynamics



Implications



Model Partnership

In 1996, a study group inside the U.S. government led by [Richard Perle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Perle) constructed an approach to solving security issues in the Middle East with an emphasis on "Western values," which was titled "[A Clean Break: A New Strategy for Securing the Realm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A_Clean_Break:_A_New_Strategy_for_Securing_the_Realm)." This new approach to peace included Turkey and Jordan's cooperation with Israel, and weakening, containing, and even rolling back Syria.[^cite_note-106] On May 18, 1996, President [Demirel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%BCleyman_Demirel) survived an assassination attempt by a sympathizer of the National Youth's Foundation, which was affiliated with the Welfare Party, and was carried out in protest of [military cooperation agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Turkey_relations) with Israel.[^cite_note-assasin-107] The military agreement was a source of anger for a segment who advocated a closer alignment with "conservative values".[^cite_note-assasin-107] [20th Parliament of Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/20th_Parliament_of_Turkey) was 28.7% from Welfare Party. On June 28, 1996, the political ideology and movement founded by [Erbakan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Necmettin_Erbakan), [Millî Görüş](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mill%C3%AE_G%C3%B6r%C3%BC%C5%9F), assumed the position of authority in Turkey's government ([54th](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/54th_government_of_Turkey)).

The [1997 Turkish military memorandum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1997_Turkish_military_memorandum), reflecting concerns about the potential weakening of "Western values," was issued by the military leadership at a National Security Council meeting and resulted in the resignation of Islamist prime minister [Necmettin Erbakan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Necmettin_Erbakan) of the [Welfare Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Welfare_Party). In December 1997, member of the welfare party [Recep Tayyip Erdoğan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recep_Tayyip_Erdo%C4%9Fan) recited a modified version of the "[Soldier's prayer](https://tr.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asker%20Duas%C4%B1)" poem written by [Ziya Gökalp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ziya_G%C3%B6kalp). Erdoğan's modified version included: "The mosques are our barracks, the domes our helmets / The minarets our bayonets and the faithful our soldiers...."[^cite_note-108] Erdoğan was given a ten-month prison sentence for incitement to violence and his mayoral position dropped due to conviction.[^cite_note-109] In 1999, the influential Islamic preacher and Sunni cleric (mufti) based in Izmir [Fethullah Gülen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fethullah_G%C3%BClen), who was imprisoned in 1971 for subversion, was facing another subversion trial.[^cite_note-110]

Rise of [Conservatism in Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservatism_in_Turkey)



[Millî Görüş](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mill%C3%AE_G%C3%B6r%C3%BC%C5%9F)



[Erdoğanism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erdo%C4%9Fanism)



[Gülen movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G%C3%BClen_movement)

In the [1999 Turkish general election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1999_Turkish_general_election), [Bülent Ecevit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B%C3%BClent_Ecevit)'s [Democratic Left Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Left_Party_\(Turkey\)) (DSP) emerged as the biggest party and swept the board in most of Turkey's western provinces after the capture of Kurdistan Workers' Party leader Abdullah Öcalan, who was ousted from Syria. [57th government of Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/57th_government_of_Turkey) was a win for Ecevit and [Ecevitism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecevitism).

#### Iraq (Terrorism)

PKK camps in northern Iraq

1995\:PM Ciller to President Clinton 5\:30: "they \[U.S.] know about this. This land became a "No Man's Land". Without authority. It is not our making. It is not only our responsibility either."

Turkey saw the U.S. failure to halt PKK activities in northern Iraq ([Northern Safe Zone](#Iraq_\(Gulf_War,_Northern_Safe_Zone\))) as evidence of a double standard with respect to terrorism and Turkey's security more broadly. Turkey performed:

- [Operation Steel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Steel): March 20 – May 4, 1995

- [Operation Hammer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Hammer_\(1997\)): May 12 – July 7, 1997

- [Operation Dawn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Dawn_\(1997\)): September 25 – October 15, 1997

In September 1998, [Masoud Barzani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Masoud_Barzani) and [Jalal Talabani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jalal_Talabani) signed the US-mediated Washington Agreement and established a formal peace treaty. In the agreement, the parties agreed to share revenue, share power, and deny the use of northern Iraq to the PKK.[^cite_note-111] President [Bill Clinton](3356.md) signed the [Iraq Liberation Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraq_Liberation_Act) into law, providing for military assistance to Iraqi opposition groups, which included the PUK and KDP. PKK insurgency waned following the [Second Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Army_\(Turkey\)) moving to the border of Syria, which was followed by the PKK leader's expulsion from Syria and the U.S.-assisted 1999 capture and subsequent trial and conviction.

#### Syria (Terrorism, Adana Agreement)

Turkey condemned Syria for supporting the PKK[^cite_note-Tr-US-PKK-40]. Turkey and Syria nearly engaged in war (a narrowly averted conflict) when Turkey threatened military action if Syria continued to shelter [Abdullah Öcalan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdullah_%C3%96calan) in [Damascus](8914.md), his long-time haven. Öcalan was the leader and a founding member of the PKK. As a result, the Syrian government forced Öcalan to leave the country, who was captured in [Kenya](188171.md) on February 15, 1999, while being transferred from the Greek embassy to [Jomo Kenyatta International Airport](142547.md) in [Nairobi](21482.md), in an operation by the [National Intelligence Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Intelligence_Organization_\(Turkey\)) (MIT) with the help of the [CIA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CIA).[^cite_note-112]

The [Adana Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adana_Agreement) was signed between Turkey and Syria regarding terrorism originating (mainly [PKK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PKK)) from Syria into Turkey. This agreement would become a contentious issue between the U.S. and Ankara later, as Turkey would not use the powers in the agreement to move into Syria militarily against ISIS. The Syrian government would also declare that it feels no longer bound by the agreement.

#### Iran (Natural gas)

Sanctions



[Pipeline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tabriz%E2%80%93Ankara_pipeline) and [Sanctions](20905961.md).



[Uğur Mumcu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U%C4%9Fur_Mumcu) memorial

On the morning of January 24, 1993, [Uğur Mumcu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U%C4%9Fur_Mumcu) was killed by a [C-4 plastic bomb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C-4_\(explosive\)). The investigation initially connected the assassination to [Iran](14653.md). It was claimed that Iran's [Ministry of Intelligence](501536.md) employed the [Hezbollah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hezbollah_\(Turkey\)) to carry out the assassination.[^cite_note-113] Against the negative public opinion on Iran, the [Tabriz–Ankara pipeline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tabriz%E2%80%93Ankara_pipeline) was a priority to the [54th government of Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/54th_government_of_Turkey). In 1996, PM Erbakan visited Tehran for the pipeline project, and Iran's cooperation against the PKK was part of the negotiations. This visit was perceived as a snub to the [Iran and Libya Sanctions Act](20905961.md). [R. Nicholas Burns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R._Nicholas_Burns) said: 'We have made very clear to the Turkish government that Iran is a state that should be isolated. We think that business-as-usual relations with Iran are not wise.'[^cite_note-114]

### George W. Bush administration (2001–2009)

State of Affairs

According to [leaked diplomatic cables](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_diplomatic_cables_leak) originating from 2004, then Prime Minister Erdoğan was described by U.S. diplomats as a "perfectionist workaholic who sincerely cares for the well-being of those around him".[^cite_note-CNN3-115] He was also described as having "little understanding of politics beyond Ankara" and as surrounding himself with an "iron ring of sycophantic (but contemptuous) advisors". He is said to be "isolated", and that his MPs and Ministers feel "fearful of Erdogan's wrath". Diplomats state that "he relies on his charisma, instincts, and the filterings of advisors who pull conspiracy theories off the Web or are lost in neo-Ottoman Islamist fantasies".[^cite_note-guard5-116][^cite_note-117][^cite_note-118]

#### Great-Power in the Middle East (Israel, Davos incident)

The [Project for the New American Century](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Project_for_the_New_American_Century) (PNAC) was established "to promote American global leadership," as its members viewed American leadership as beneficial to both America and the world, and sought to build support for "a Reaganite policy of military strength and moral clarity." The organization's influence originated with its members in the [administration of Bush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_of_George_W._Bush), including [Dick Cheney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dick_Cheney), [Donald Rumsfeld](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Rumsfeld), [Paul Wolfowitz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_Wolfowitz), and seven others in decision-making positions.[^cite_note-119] [Foreign policy of the George W. Bush administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_policy_of_the_George_W._Bush_administration). This group challenged regimes hostile to American interests and values, which supported unilateral military action to advance U.S. interests, particularly in the Middle East. Shortly after the Sep 11 attacks, [Wesley Clark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wesley_Clark) cited a [Pentagon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Pentagon) policy and outline of a plan to "take out seven countries in five years."[^cite_note-120] PNAC's push for U.S. [regime change in Iraq](37258993.md#2003%E2%80%932021:_Iraq) strained Turkey. Turkey feared cross-border instability ([refugees from Iraq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_migrant_crisis#Influx_from_the_Gulf_War)), economic fallout, and the rise of entities aligned with the PKK. Turkey's nightmare. The [October 2007 clashes in Hakkâri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/October_2007_clashes_in_Hakk%C3%A2ri) and simultaneous introduction of the H.Res.106 Armenian Genocide Resolution by Rep. [Schiff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adam_Schiff)[^cite_note-121] made the streets of Turkey erupt with anger. The Bush administration's response to [north Iraq PKK camps](#Iraq_\(territorial_integrity\)).

What did PNAC want from Turkey? It can be found in the [neoconservative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neoconservative) doctrine (manifesto), [A Clean Break: A New Strategy for Securing the Realm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A_Clean_Break:_A_New_Strategy_for_Securing_the_Realm). "A Clean Break" was "a kind of US-Israeli position paper, and it proposed "a mini-cold war" in the Middle East, advocating the use of proxy armies for regime changes, destabilization, and containment."[^cite_note-122] It said, "Rather than pursuing a 'comprehensive peace' with the entire Arab world, Israel should work jointly with Jordan and Turkey to 'contain, destabilize, and roll back ' those entities that are threats to all three," and continued with supporting facts: "Syria repeatedly breaks its word. It violated numerous [agreements with the Turks](#Syria_\(Terrorism\)), and has betrayed the United States by continuing to occupy Lebanon in violation of the Taef agreement in 1989."[^cite_note-CleanBreak-123] The document, in the operations, states: "Israel can shape its strategic environment, in cooperation with Turkey and Jordan, by weakening, containing, and even rolling back Syria."[^cite_note-CleanBreak-123] The 2008 Syria-Israel peace talks progressed through [Erdoğan literally serving](#Syria_\(normalization\)) as the go-between. Erdoğan invested massive political capital. The negotiations fell apart during the fourth round of indirect talks in Ankara while Israeli PM Ehud Olmert was expressing the progress. The news of Israel's military launching [Operation Cast Lead](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Cast_Lead) arrived. Erdoğan publicly condemned the military offensive. Israel had a different vision for its strategic security needs.

| External videos |
| --- |
| YouTube logoWorld Economic Forum: Davos Annual Meeting |
| video icon [Gaza: The Case for Middle East Peace](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cR4zRbPy2kY\&si=LuohAOfm-f4GqPEh\&t=3664) |

On February 1, the [2009 Davos incident](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_Davos_incident) at the [WEF](172185.md) session "Gaza: The Case for Middle East Peace" coincided with a radical domestic transformation that led Turkey to revise its international alliance preferences as a member of a Western alliance (NATO). Erdoğan's outburst at the Davos Summit marked a critical juncture, signaling a shift in Turkey's national agenda and alignment policies. The fallout came on May 31, 2010, after [nine activists were killed](#Palestine_\(Gaza_flotilla_raid\)) over the [Gaza flotilla raid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Gaza_flotilla_raid) after Netanyahu cleared the operation; the [signs of changing position](#Deterioration_of_the_partnership) were visible. "A Clean break" position regarding "...in cooperation with Turkey..." lost its viability. As the 2000s ended, Turkish political parties could not ignore the [domestic political shifts](#Opinion_Surveys). As the U.S. prioritized mainly Israel's security needs, Turkey pursued more independent strategies and moved away from U.S.-Israel security designs. At this juncture, [Davutoğlu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmet_Davuto%C4%9Flu) (Minister of Foreign Affairs, May 1, 2009 – August 29, 2014) aimed to establish Turkey as an influential power within the Balkans, Caucasus, and the Middle East by resolving regional disputes and building peaceful, cooperative economic and political ties to achieve economic development. He called his doctrine the "Zero Problems with Neighbors," but the West called it "[Neo-Ottomanism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neo-Ottomanism)."[^cite_note-124] Why did the West call it Neo-Ottomanism? "[Ottomanism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottomanism)" was primarily conceived as a reform and renewal project for the [declining Ottoman Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Decline_and_modernization_of_the_Ottoman_Empire) in the 19th century. Ottomanism did not prevent the [dissolution of the Ottoman Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dissolution_of_the_Ottoman_Empire).

#### War on terror (Rendition)

Turkey had remained a close ally of the United States in the [war on terror](13425800.md) after the [September 11 attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/September_11,_2001_attacks). Turkish President [Ahmet Necdet Sezer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmet_Necdet_Sezer) and Prime Minister [Bülent Ecevit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B%C3%BClent_Ecevit) condemned the attacks, and the Turkish government then ordered all of its flags at half-mast for one day of mourning. Turkey participated in the [International Security Assistance Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Security_Assistance_Force).

War on terror



[Gül](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdullah_G%C3%BCl) with [Rumsfeld](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Rumsfeld) on July 24, 2003



Administrative Review of [Murat Kurnaz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Murat_Kurnaz)



[Kurnaz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Murat_Kurnaz) v George W. Bush

[Turkish detainees at Guantanamo Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_detainees_at_Guantanamo_Bay)

According to a report by the [Open Society Foundations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open_Society_Foundations), Turkey participated at one point or another in the CIA's [extraordinary rendition](1031191.md) program.[^cite_note-125] U.S. ambassador [Ross Wilson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ross_Wilson_\(ambassador\)) revealed the involvement of the Incirlik airbase in a [diplomatic cable](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diplomatic_cable) dated June 8, 2006, which described Turkey as a crucial ally in the "global war on terror" and an important logistical base for the US-led war in Iraq. On June 14, 2006, Turkish foreign ministry officials told reporters: "The Turkish government and state never played a part \[in the secret transfers] ... and never will." According to evidence, the US base was a transit stop in taking detainees to secret prisons. The cable also stated: "We recommend that you do not raise this issue with TGS \[Turkish general staff] pending clarification from Washington on what approach state/OSD/JCS/NSC \[national security council] wish to take."[^cite_note-126]

#### Syria (normalization)



Israel-Syria talks, and Iran



Accepting Turkey in the Israel-US economic zone.



Erdogan and Olmert, Israel-Syria

During the February 2007 visit to Turkey, Israeli Prime Minister Ehud Olmert discussed the possibility of Turkish mediation between his country and Syria. Turkey tried to be involved in the negotiations with the ongoing Syrian-Israeli disputes.

Turkey offered peaceful political and economic cooperation with Syria.[^cite_note-syriavisit-127] In April 2008, during Erdogan's visit to Damascus, Turkey urged Syrians to "liberalize" in line with Turkey’s recent economic/social reform model, noting that their national income had increased from \$230 billion to \$659 billion since 2003.[^cite_note-syriavisit-127] Erdogan stated, “This can easily be done in Syria…We are willing to put our hand in yours!”[^cite_note-syriavisit-127] On May 21, 2008, after considerable dialogue by Davutoglu, Israel, Syria, and Turkey simultaneously announced that Israel and Syria had launched proximity (indirect) peace talks in Istanbul. Turkey helped lay the groundwork for a peace agreement without getting credit for achieving it.

Because of Syria’s destabilizing influence in Lebanon, support for Hezbollah and Hamas, and ties with Iran, Bush administration policy was isolating Syria, not peace negotiations. Dana Perino stated that the Administration was not surprised by the May 21 announcement and did not object to it. Condoleezza Rice, “We would welcome any steps that might lead to a comprehensive peace in the Middle East.... We are working very hard on the Palestinian track. It doesn’t mean that the U.S. would not support other tracks.” [^cite_note-128] [^cite_note-129]

Later, the U.S. characterized this effort as an opportunity to convey appropriate messages to Damascus; the Istanbul negotiations were a forum rather than real peace negotiations.[^cite_note-130] The U.S. shifted its policy development to [Iran Syria Policy and Operations Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran_Syria_Policy_and_Operations_Group). Perino stated, “What we hope is that this is a forum to address various concerns that we all share about Syria—U.S., Israel, and many others—in regard to Syria’s support for Hamas and Hezbollah (and) the training and funding of terrorists that belong to these organizations.... We believe it could help us to further isolate Iran....”[^cite_note-131] After successful agreements between Israel and Syria, parties were transitioning from indirect to direct negotiations. What did indirect talks agree on besides the pullout? Israel got full peace, open borders, diplomatic and commercial relations with Syria, and Syria to halt military ties with Iran and its regional proxies, Hezbollah and Hamas.[^cite_note-132] Corruption investigations surrounding Israeli Prime Minister Ehud Olmert limited both sides' capacity to finalize the accord.[^cite_note-133] The talks stopped after the [Operation Cast Lead](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Cast_Lead) in December 2008. [Ehud Olmert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ehud_Olmert) succeeded by [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) on 31 March 2009. Netanyahu passed legislation that made it tougher to withdraw from the Golan. Syria denounced Netanyahu's law as "proof" that Netanyahu's doesn't want peace. Erdoğan criticized Israel and severely strained Turkish-Israeli relations. In October 2009, Turkey banned Israel's participation in the [Anatolian Eagle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anatolian_Eagle) military exercise, and Netanyahu stated "Turkey can't be \[an] honest broker", between Syria and Israel.[^cite_note-134] The peace negotiations ended.

#### Iraq (territorial integrity)

Iraq War



Turkey, deployment of forces



Effects of Turkey's 2003 refusal of the U.S.



On March 5, 2003, Gen. [Hilmi Özkök](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hilmi_%C3%96zk%C3%B6k), [Chief of the Turkish General Staff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_of_the_Turkish_General_Staff), stated that he believed it was possible to shorten the duration of the war in Iraq by opening a second front near the Turkey-Iraq border.

[^cite_note-135]

Turkey is particularly cautious about a Kurdish state arising from a destabilized Iraq. Turkey has fought an insurgent war against the PKK, which is listed as a terrorist organization internationally by [a number of states and organizations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurdistan_Workers_Party/States_Listed_as_Terrorist). Iraq was a safe haven for PKK. The Iraqi Kurds were organized under the PUK and KDP, who later cooperated with American forces during the [2003 invasion of Iraq](201936.md).

In 2002 [Morton I. Abramowitz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morton_I._Abramowitz) (1989–1991 US Ambassador) said, in regards to Turkey's involvement in an upcoming war: "It is hard to believe that in the end the Turks would not cooperate with the United States if war takes place, with or without UN blessing".[^cite_note-136] Vice President [Dick Cheney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dick_Cheney)'s only trip abroad in his first three years at the office was a four-day trip to Ankara. Prime Minister [Bülent Ecevit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B%C3%BClent_Ecevit) welcomed Cheney to a working dinner on March 19, who offered \$228 million to aid in military efforts provided that international military operations took command of the Afghanistan peacekeeping force.[^cite_note-137] Turkey's position on Iraq was presented to Cheney. In December 2002, Turkey moved approximately 15,000 soldiers to its border with Iraq.[^cite_note-138] The [2003 invasion of Iraq](201936.md) faced strong [domestic opposition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opposition_to_the_Iraq_War) in Turkey: opinion polls showed that 80% of Turks were opposed to the war. The Turkish Parliament's position reflected the public's. The March 1, 2003, motion at the [Turkish Parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_Parliament) could not reach the absolute majority of 276 votes needed to allow US troops to attack Iraq from Turkey (62,000 troops and more than 250 planes), the final tally being 264 votes for and 250 against.[^cite_note-upsetsUS-139] BBC's Jonny Dymond said the knife-edge vote is a massive blow to the government which has a majority in parliament. On March 11, Abdullah Gül resigned as Turkey's Prime Minister.[^cite_note-140] [Chief of the General Staff of Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_of_the_General_Staff_of_Turkey) [Hilmi Özkök](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hilmi_%C3%96zk%C3%B6k) said "Turkey would suffer the effects of the war \[motion also included twice as many Turkish troops to be deployed to northern Iraq]." The US did not immediately re-deploy the forces intended for staging in Turkey and the State Department asked for "clarification" of the Turkish vote.[^cite_note-upsetsUS-139] In the end, the US pulled the offer of \$6 billion in grants and up to \$24 billion in loan guarantees, which caused Turkey's stock market to plunge by 12%.[^cite_note-141]

In connection with its invasion of Iraq, the United States requested that Turkey allow 62,000 soldiers to deploy from its territory and that 9 Turkish air bases allow United States bombers to deploy.[^cite_note-\:3-142]<sup>: 37 </sup> Tension developed when the Turkish government agreed to the request, but parliament rejected it on March 1, 2003.[^cite_note-\:3-142]<sup>: 37 </sup> On March 20, 2003, following a visit by United States Secretary of State Colin Powell to Ankara, an agreement was consummated to allow the United States Air Force to use Turkish airspace.[^cite_note-\:3-142]<sup>: 37 </sup> Relations improved in 2009 following United States President Barack Obama's visit to Turkey.[^cite_note-\:3-142]<sup>: 37 </sup>

[^cite_note-Tr-US-PKK-40]



Countering the PKK

[^cite_note-Tr-US-PKK-40]

"Iraq’s territorial integrity is maintained and the quality of life in Iraq is improved visibly." stated goal at "US and Coalition Objectives" by [USDP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Under_Secretary_of_Defense_for_Policy) [Feith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Douglas_Feith) to Bush and the NSC.

[^cite_note-143]

On March 20, the [2003 invasion of Iraq](201936.md) began. On July 4, 2003, Turkish military personnel who were stationed in northern Iraq were captured from their station, led away with hoods over their heads, and interrogated; this later came to be known as the "[hood event](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hood_event)". Turkish military personnel had stationed military observers in the "northern safe zone" after the 1991 Gulf War. The specific unit was stationed at [Sulaimaniya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sulaimaniya) after the civil war broke out in 1996 to monitor a ceasefire between the PUK and KDP.[^cite_note-Economist_2003-144] The unit station was a historical Ottoman Empire facility (dwelling), which held the historical archives of the Ottoman Empire. Among the destroyed documents were the deed records of the region.[^cite_note-145] The hood event was strongly condemned by the Turkey's newspapers and referred to Americans as "[Rambos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Rambo)" and "[Ugly Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ugly_American_\(epithet\))".[^cite_note-Guardian-146] [Chief of the General Staff of Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_of_the_General_Staff_\(Turkey\)) [Hilmi Özkök](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hilmi_%C3%96zk%C3%B6k) declared the incident as the sign of "crisis of confidence" between the US and Turkey.[^cite_note-Guardian-146]

During the conflict, Ankara pressured the U.S. into subduing PKK training camps in northern Iraq. The U.S. remained reluctant because northern Iraq was relatively stable compared to the rest of the country. The [October 2007 clashes in Hakkâri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/October_2007_clashes_in_Hakk%C3%A2ri) and simultaneous introduction of the H.Res.106 Armenian Genocide Resolution by Rep. [Schiff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adam_Schiff)[^cite_note-147] made the streets of Turkey erupt with anger. On October 17, 2007, the [Turkish Parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_Parliament) voted in favor of allowing the Turkish Armed Forces to take military action against the PKK based in northern Iraq.[^cite_note-148] In response, Bush stated that he did not believe it was in Turkey's interests to send troops into Iraq. Besides Bush and Rice [on the phone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/October_2007_clashes_in_Hakk%C3%A2ri#Reaction), Multinational Force commander General [Petraeus](3223944.md) and Vice Chairman of the Joint Chiefs of Staff General [Cartwright](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cartwright) rushed to Turkey to calm the situation and deconflict U.S. and Turkish operations as TAF moved into [2008 Turkish incursion into northern Iraq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Turkish_incursion_into_northern_Iraq).[^cite_note-149]

#### Russia (Natural Gas, Nuclear Energy)

Turkey's fourth attempt to build a nuclear reactor in 2002 was driven by concerns about dependence on Russian gas for electricity generation. US companies did not produce any bids. In fact, Turkey received only bid—from [Rosatom](1641369.md).[^cite_note-thebulletin.org-150] The bid was rejected partly due to defeating the dependency problem. Turkey's build-own-transfer approach failed.[^cite_note-thebulletin.org-150]

In June 2008, The United States and Turkey began to cooperate on peaceful uses of nuclear energy with a pact that aims for the transfer of technology, material, reactors, and components for nuclear research and nuclear power production in Turkey for an initial 15-year period followed by automatic renewals in five-year increments that provides a comprehensive framework for peaceful nuclear cooperation between the two nations under the agreed non-proliferation conditions and controls. A parallel U.S. bipartisan resolution highlighted the importance of the Turkish Republic's key role in safeguarding Eurasian energy security for its Western and regional allies.

The Center for Strategic and International Studies launched a one-year initiative to evaluate and enhance the Turkish Republic–United States strategic partnership, aiming to develop an implementation plan for the concluded framework by the end of this phase.

After 8 years of failed attempts, due to being perceived as a possible nuclear proliferation front in the West (US), Turkey altered its strategy. Instead of owning a nuclear power plant, Turkey enabled vendors to own the plant. In 2010, Turkey and Russia signed an agreement for nuclear cooperation.[^cite_note-thebulletin.org-150] From a Eurasian energy security perspective, the US lost a NATO partner (in nuclear cooperation), as this nuclear deal did not solve Turkey's dependence on Russia's natural gas but added a second dependence on nuclear technology.[^cite_note-thebulletin.org-150]

#### Gülen Movement (Ergenekon, AKP closure)

[Fethullah Gülen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fethullah_G%C3%BClen) ([Pasinler, Erzurum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pasinler,_Erzurum) 1941 – [Saylorsburg, Pennsylvania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saylorsburg,_Pennsylvania) 2024) was a Turkish Islamic preacher and Sunni cleric ([mufti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mufti)) who was based in the U.S. since he left Turkey in March 1999, just before tried *in absentia* and found guilty of subverting the [government of Turkey](3680197.md) (occupying senior bureaucratic and judicial positions to create a [parallel state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parallel_state)) in 2000. In 2002, Gülen applied for "[green card](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Permanent_residence_\(United_States\))." The [HS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secretary_of_Homeland_Security) argued for the rejection, while the CIA National Intelligence Council's former vice chairman [Graham E. Fuller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Graham_E._Fuller), former CIA official [George Fidas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Fidas?action=edit\&redlink=1), and former Ambassador to Turkey [Morton Abramowitz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morton_Abramowitz) sided with Gülen and presented affidavits. The court granted Gülen permission to reside in the U.S. Later in 2008, the AKP, which was facing the [2008 Justice and Development Party closure trial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Justice_and_Development_Party_closure_trial), reversed Gülen's conviction in Turkey. AKP's case against the closure trial and Gülen's subversion trial were finalized for the defendants. Prime Minister Erdogan has suggested that the closure case was a response to the government's earlier pursuit of Ergenekon, and/or that Ergenekon was not retribution for the closure. Six years after the reversed conviction, in 2014, Erdogan said that Sunni cleric Gülen should return to Turkey.[^cite_note-151] After the 2016 failed coup, [during Obama administration](#Gülen_movement_\(coup_d'état_attempt\)), it was speculated that Gülen planned to return to Turkey like [exiled Khomeini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruhollah_Khomeini's_life_in_exile).[^cite_note-152]

The [Gülen movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G%C3%BClen_movement), followers of Gülen, is a [sub-sect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_schools_and_branches) of [Sunni Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunni_Islam) based on a [Nursian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Said_Nurs%C3%AE) theological perspective as reflected in Fethullah Gülen's religious teachings. Gülen movement's possible involvement in the [Ergenekon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ergenekon_\(organization\)) plot (trials) is controversial.[^cite_note-153] The investigation claimed to study an organization compared to a [Counter-Guerrilla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Counter-Guerrilla). The accused were declared to be the "[deep state in Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deep_state_in_Turkey)." The [Ergenekon trials](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ergenekon_trials) were a series of high-profile trials that began on October 20, 2008, in which 275 people, including military officers, journalists, and opposition lawmakers, all alleged members of Ergenekon, were accused of plotting against the Erdogan government. The trials resulted in lengthy prison sentences for most of the accused.

[Ergenekon trials](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ergenekon_trials)



[CRS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congressional_Research_Service): Crisis on Identity and Power



[Rice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Condoleezza_Rice):“But ultimately, these questions about politics and religion and different social values are going to have to be ones that are resolved within the context of Turkish law, politics, and their Constitution.”



The arrest of U.S. partner in the [war on terror](13425800.md) [Chief of the General Staff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_of_the_General_Staff_\(Turkey\)) [Başbuğ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C4%B0lker_Ba%C5%9Fbu%C4%9F) on terrorist charges regarded as [Gülen movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G%C3%BClen_movement) has the power to imprison whoever it likes.

[^cite_note-154]

The US Secretary of State reported on the Turkish investigation into the Ergenekon network and concluded that "the details of the case were murky, however, and Ergenekon's status as a terrorist organization remained under debate at year's end."[^cite_note-TZ090502b-155]

### Obama administration (2009–2017)

Erdoğan and Obama at the 2015 G20 Antalya summit in Turkey

September 25, 2009, U.S. President Barack Obama and Turkish Prime Minister Recep Tayyip Erdoğan following the G-20 Summit afternoon session in Pittsburgh, Pa.,;

February 13, 2012, Turkish Foreign Minister Ahmet Davutoğlu and U.S. Secretary of State Hillary Clinton in Washington, D.C.;

September 5, 2014, Turkish President Recep Tayyip Erdoğan with U.S. President Barack Obama and U.S. Secretary of State John Kerry during the NATO Summit in Newport;

November 1, 2011, Turkish Defense Minister İsmet Yılmaz with U.S. Secretary of Defense Leon Panetta

November 18, 2013, Turkish Foreign Minister Ahmet Davutoğlu and U.S. Secretary of State John Kerry in Washington, D.C.

December 6, 2016, Turkish Foreign Minister Mevlüt Çavuşoğlu with U.S. Secretary of State John Kerry

At the beginning of the Obama administration, on May 30, 2009, a [U.S. Democratic Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Democratic_Party) delegation group including U.S. Senators [Casey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bob_Casey_Jr.), [Kaufman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edward_E._Kaufman), [Lautenberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frank_Lautenberg) and U.S. Congressman [Walz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timothy_Walz) met with Turkish officials in Ankara to confirm that "Turkey can always depend on the US, while the US can always rely on its close friendship with Turkey".[^cite_note-TZ090602a-156] Obama gave impassioned and eloquent speeches in Turkey ([Obama visit](#2009_Obama_visit)) and Cairo ([A New Beginning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A_New_Beginning_\(speech\))). [Middle Eastern foreign policy of the Obama administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_Eastern_foreign_policy_of_the_Obama_administration) was a strategic "rebalancing" away from extensive military commitments as a shift from the post-9/11 policies of George W. Bush. In this period, Turkey and President Obama faced the Middle Eastern dysfunctional state systems and fraying civil societies, as well as blowback from George W. Bush's invasions.

President Obama's speeches were seen as the advent of a new era. However, [Foreign Minister](10283896.md) [Ahmet Davutoğlu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmet_Davuto%C4%9Flu) said that *2013* was the year that would shape relations between Turkey and the US over the coming decades. According to Davutoglu, Obama's responses to the breach of his red lines ([Ghouta chemical attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghouta_chemical_attack)) and acceptance of the coup in [Egypt](8087628.md) ([2013 Egyptian coup d'état](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_Egyptian_coup_d'%C3%A9tat)) worked for the narratives of extremists, encouraged atrocities, also signaled to our (NATO) adversaries that they can count on the US's inaction at places like [Crimea](163045.md) ([Russian occupation of Crimea](71944314.md)).[^cite_note-Al_Jazeera-157]

At the end of the Obama administration, signs of strain emerged when Obama refused to hold a formal meeting with Erdoğan during the latter's visit to the United States in March 2016.[^cite_note-158]

#### War on terror

The 2009 [U.S. Secretary of State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Secretary_of_State)'s *Country Report on Terrorism* confirmed that cooperation against terrorism is a key element in America's strategic partnership with Turkey, before going on to praise Turkish contributions to stabilize Iraq and Afghanistan and highlighting the strategic importance of the [İncirlik Air Base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C4%B0ncirlik_Air_Base) used by both U.S. and NATO forces for operations in the region.[^cite_note-TZ090502b-155] Turkey had opposed the appointment of [Anders Fogh Rasmussen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anders_Fogh_Rasmussen) as NATO secretary general due to his approach to the [Muhammed carricaturs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jyllands-Posten_Muhammad_cartoons_controversy) in Denmark until Obama assured a Turk would be one of Rasmussen deputies.[^cite_note-159]

The U.S. Secretary of State's report also contained information on the [PKK](69680.md) and other terrorist groups operating in Turkey, whom the U.S. and Turkish authorities share intelligence on, highlighting the September 12, 2006, attack on [Diyarbakır](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diyarbak%C4%B1r) and the July 27, 2008, attack on Güngören.[^cite_note-TZ090502b-155] In 2016, Vice President [Joe Biden](145422.md) called the PKK a terrorist group "plain and simple" and compared it to the [ISIL](9087364.md).[^cite_note-160]
