#### Libya (Intervention)

Turkey was involved in the [2011 military intervention in Libya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_military_intervention_in_Libya) following the leadership of the Obama administration, [American involvement in the 2011 Libyan Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_involvement_in_the_2011_Libyan_Civil_War). However, Turkey opposed the Obama-Sarkozy position. Turkey stated, "\[This is] French interests over the Libyan people!"[^cite_note-161] Another breakpoint with the Obama administration, Turkey cut ties with Gaddafi only after extending a hand to him and asking him to be exiled in Turkey in which he refused.[^cite_note-162] [Killing of Muammar Gaddafi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Killing_of_Muammar_Gaddafi) occurred on October 20, 2011, after the Battle of Sirte.



Turkey and [Operation Odyssey Dawn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Odyssey_Dawn)



[Davutoğlu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmet_Davuto%C4%9Flu), [London Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/London_Conference_on_Libya), [Unified Protector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Unified_Protector)

Libya showed Ankara's new policy toward the Arab Middle East: leading the world in dropping dictators in favor of the pro-democracy movements, from Egypt to Libya to Syria.[^cite_note-163] [Ankara moves closer to Washington.](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=File\:Orienting_our_sights_on_the_future_-_opportunities_and_challenges_of_the_Arab_revolts_\(IA_orientingoursigh00amin\).pdf\&page=23)

#### Iran (nuclear deal, arms embargo, oil trading controversy)

Further information: [United States sanctions against Iran](9941859.md) and [Nuclear program of Iran](721807.md)

In May 2009, after parliamentary debates in Belgium and Germany called for the removal of the nuclear weapons stationed in their military bases, questions were subsequently raised over the reportedly continuing presence of [B61 nuclear bombs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B61_nuclear_bomb) stationed at the [Incirlik Air Base](1597543.md) as part of [NATO](21133.md)'s [nuclear sharing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_sharing) program. [Bilkent University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bilkent_University) Professor Mustafa Kibaroğlu speculated that if the Obama administration had pressed for the withdrawal of these weapons, which Turkey wished to maintain, then Turkey-U.S. relations could have been strained.[^cite_note-TZ090504-164] In October 2009, President Obama sent a proposal (nuclear deal framework) to Erdoğan during the heated discussions in the U.S. on nuclear weapons in Turkey. However, before the completion of the framework, Turkish and Iranian diplomats were discussing a preliminary nuclear deal, which would be named the Tehran Declaration.[^cite_note-gurzel-165] In April 2010, while Turkish and Iranian diplomats were working on a deal, Washington stepped up its efforts to impose a new round of sanctions on Iran over its nuclear program ([Comprehensive Iran Sanctions, Accountability, and Divestment Act of 2010](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comprehensive_Iran_Sanctions,_Accountability,_and_Divestment_Act_of_2010)). Iran agreed to the "Tehran Declaration" after China and Russia agreed to the prepared UN sanctions on Iran (described as a 'tactical move'). In May 2010, non-permanent members of the Security Council, Turkey and Brazil, announced the "Tehran Declaration."[^cite_note-gurzel-165] The Tehran Declaration was a fuel-swap deal. It stipulated that medical isotopes (a humanitarian need of Iran) could be produced by 20-percent-enriched nuclear fuel, which was to be provided to only one research reactor. The research reactor in question was the Tehran Research Reactor, which was supplied by the U.S. under the Atoms for Peace program. In exchange, Tehran would transfer 1,200 kilograms of low-enriched uranium to Turkey.[^cite_note-gurzel-165] On May 17, 2010, Brazil, Iran and Turkey signed a tripartite Joint Declaration asserting that a nuclear fuel exchange could lead to peace.[^cite_note-166] The U.S. discarded the agreement because "it did not address the continued production of uranium enriched to 20 percent inside Iranian territory."[^cite_note-167]



2010: Tehran Declaration, Turkey and Brazil.



2015: the signing of [INDF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran_nuclear_deal_framework), 5 years after the "Tehran Declaration."



2018: Ground-breaking of the [Akkuyu Nuclear Power Plant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Akkuyu_Nuclear_Power_Plant).



[Atilla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_v._Atilla) writs of certiorari [Halk Bankasi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkiye_Halk_Bankasi_A.S._v._United_States)

Turkey, India, and China opposed the adoption of a new round of sanctions, claiming the "Tehran Declaration" could be improved, rather than discarded.[^cite_note-168] Opposition in the U.S. condemned the move, with claims that Turkey is not in the Western camp.[^cite_note-gul-169] Turkey's objection to the U.S. sanctions resulted in the U.S. Congress delaying arms sales sought by the Turkish military.[^cite_note-170] On August 17, 2010, a separate report presented to Obama by the [U.S. Commission on International Religious Freedom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Commission_on_International_Religious_Freedom), which had previously urged him to raise the subject of religious freedom during his 2009 presidential visit to Turkey, concluded that Turkey's interpretation of secularism "resulted in violations of religious freedoms for many of the country's citizens, including members of the majority and, especially, minority religious communities".[^cite_note-TZ090502c-171] Obama said that future arms sales would depend on Turkish policies.[^cite_note-172]

Following the discredited Tehran Declaration and sanctions on Turkey, progress on the issue stopped until the change of government in Iran. Iranian President [Hassan Rouhani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hassan_Rouhani) signaled a change and opened the way for nuclear talks.[^cite_note-gul-169] On April 2, 2015, the [Iran nuclear deal framework](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran_nuclear_deal_framework) was signed five years after the Tehran Deal. Instead of the Turkey-Brazil initiative for the "Tehran Deal", the pact was signed between [Iran](14653.md) and the [P5+1 countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/P5+1).

> We urge both sides to continue their constructive approach, which aims to carry the process forward. Our hope is that both sides will carry out the mutually agreed steps on the agreement, therefore resolving this issue permanently in such a way that will satisfy all the sides involved through diplomacy.[^cite_note-gul-169]

— [Turkish Foreign Ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_Foreign_Ministry)

Why were Turkey and Brazil involved in the Iranian Nuclear issues? The [Turkish Energy, Nuclear and Mineral Research Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_Energy,_Nuclear_and_Mineral_Research_Agency) managed [nuclear power in Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_power_in_Turkey). In putting together the fuel-swap deal, Turkey was trying to defend the autonomy of non-nuclear-weapon states (NNWS) to enrich uranium for producing electricity and strengthen the right of NNWS to develop peaceful nuclear activities.[^cite_note-173] The Obama administration sanctioned Turkey, albeit for different reasons than Iran. In 2013, Russian nuclear construction company [Atomstroyexport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atomstroyexport) and Turkey signed a construction agreement for the "[Akkuyu Nuclear Power Plant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Akkuyu_Nuclear_Power_Plant)". Turkey did not fall into the Iranian situation. The whole operation is Russian built, owned, and operated ([build–own–operate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Build%E2%80%93own%E2%80%93operate)) such that even the spent fuel ([high-level waste](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High-level_waste)) returned to Russia.[^cite_note-174] Instead of the US partnering with Turkey, leaving Turkey to the Russia's help for the NNWS will affect [Russia–Turkey relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russia%E2%80%93Turkey_relations#1990%E2%80%93present) by prolonging Turkey's dependence on Russian energy, beyond [natural gas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil_and_gas_in_Turkey). It was a critical disconnect among NATO allies.

Before the November 2013 US-led nuclear sanctions were imposed, [Halkbank](16045143.md) purchased gold on the open market between March 2012 and July 2013. November 2013 sanctions prevented Iran from being paid in dollars, but gold was never mentioned in the sanctions regime. Halkbank exchanged gold for Iranian oil (oil trading controversy). October 18, 2015, the [Joint Comprehensive Plan of Action](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint_Comprehensive_Plan_of_Action) canceled the sanctions in question. In March 2017, the deputy head [Halkbank](16045143.md#U.S._conviction_of_executive), Mehmet Hakan Atilla, was arrested by the US government for conspiring to evade sanctions against Iran by helping [Reza Zarrab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reza_Zarrab), an Iranian-Azeri businessman who had taken Turkish citizenship, "use U.S. financial institutions to engage in prohibited financial transactions that illegally funneled millions of dollars to Iran".[^cite_note-175] Zarrab was in [Miami, Florida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miami,_Florida), in March 2016.[^cite_note-176] Atilla's trial commenced in New York City federal court in November 2017, with Zarrab agreeing to testify after reaching a plea deal with prosecutors.[^cite_note-177] In early 2018, Atilla was convicted on five of six counts against him, including bank fraud and conspiracies and acquitted on one count after four days of jury deliberation.

#### Syria (Civil War, territorial integrity, safe zone, Rat Line, YPG)

Turkey was particularly cautious about a Kurdish state arising from a destabilized Syria. Turkey has fought an insurgent war against the PKK, which is listed as a terrorist organization internationally by a number of states and organizations. Until 2011, Turkey's policy was to try to preserve a neutral but constructive position because civil war and sectarian conflicts would threaten Turkey's security.[^cite_note-178] Eventually war broke and Syria ([refugees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_refugees_in_Turkey), [spillover](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spillover_of_the_Syrian_Civil_War#Turkey)) impacted Turkey more directly than other [actors in the conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_involvement_in_the_Syrian_Civil_War).



2011: [Hillary Clinton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hillary_Clinton) meets with [Selahattin Demirtaş](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Selahattin_Demirta%C5%9F)

Between 2011 and 2012, Turkey absorbed 120,000 [Syrian refugees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_refugees_in_Turkey), 90,000 of whom are in camps. In September 2012, Turkey asked the US to establish "[safe zones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Safe_Zone_\(Syria\))" with Turkey in northern Syria to accommodate refugees and reduce the number of civilian casualties.[^cite_note-179] Gen. [Philip Breedlove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philip_Breedlove) to the [United States Senate Committee on Armed Services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Senate_Committee_on_Armed_Services) said Syria was [weaponizing migration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Refugees_as_weapons).[^cite_note-180] In 2015, U.S. senators called for humanitarian safe zones in Syria; the Obama administration declined.[^cite_note-181] In 2016, during the [European migrant crisis](46415102.md), Merkel asked the same question. Obama said Syrian safe zones won't work.[^cite_note-182]

> If we set up a no-fly zone or [safe zones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Safe_Zone_\(Syria\)) on the ground, we were buying a costly, dangerous, lengthy, and uncertain military commitment on top of Afghanistan and Iraq that put significant numbers of U.S. forces in harm's way. Could we have protected civilians in safe zones? Yes, had we deployed thousands of U.S. troops to take and hold the ground and committed roughly 100 planes to provide air coverage.[^cite_note-183]

— [Susan Rice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Susan_Rice) ([United States National Security Advisor](44028.md)), In Syria, America Had No Good Options, [The Atlantic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Atlantic) (October 7, 2019)



[USS *George H. W. Bush*](156573.md) anchored during a port visit to [Antalya](361527.md) in the [Turkish Riviera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_Riviera), March 11, 2013.

Beginning in 2012, Turkey and the United States supported the "[Syrian opposition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_opposition)" which held the idea of replacing the [Assad regime](57134800.md). In early 2012, [Seymour Hersh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seymour_Hersh) reported that the [CIA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CIA_activities_in_Syria) cooperated with Turkey in a covert operation named "the Rat Line", which obtained and transported armaments from Libya to rebel groups (later known as the [Free Syrian Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_Syrian_Army) (FSA)) in Syria via proxies and [front organizations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Front_organization) in southern Turkey. The CIA's involvement reportedly ended after the mass evacuation of CIA operatives from the American consulate in Benghazi, Libya, after the [2012 Benghazi attack](37018641.md).[^cite_note-184][^cite_note-185] In January 2014, the [House Permanent Select Committee on Intelligence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/House_Permanent_Select_Committee_on_Intelligence) reported specifically on "the CIA annex at Benghazi", that "all CIA activities in Benghazi were legal and authorized. On-the-record testimony establishes that the CIA was not sending weapons ... from Libya to Syria, or facilitating other organizations or states that were transferring weapons from Libya to Syria."[^cite_note-186] While the Obama administration [investigated the Benghazi attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Investigation_into_the_2012_Benghazi_attack) in January 2014, the [National Intelligence Organisation scandal in Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_National_Intelligence_Organisation_scandal_in_Turkey) broke out. In May 2014, the [editor-in-chief](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Editor-in-chief) of the [Cumhuriyet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cumhuriyet) [Can Dündar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Can_D%C3%BCndar) published pictures of agents and trucks, and was later sentenced for ″leaking secret information of the state″.[^cite_note-187]

[Opposition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_Opposition): [Free Syrian Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_Syrian_Army)



[Davutoğlu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmet_Davuto%C4%9Flu), [Kerry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Kerry), [Moaz al-Khatib](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moaz_al-Khatib)



[CRS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congressional_Research_Service): Train and Equip for Syria, Funding, and Issues

In October 2014, Vice President [Joe Biden](145422.md) accused Turkey of funding [al-Nusra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Nusra_Front) and [al Qaeda](1921.md) (FSA-identified groups),[^cite_note-188] to which Erdoğan angrily responded, "Biden has to apologize for his statements" adding that if no apology is made, Biden would become "history to \[him]". Biden subsequently apologized.[^cite_note-189] In 2015, the *[International Business Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Business_Times)* wrote that the US sent weapons shipments to FSA-identified groups through a CIA program for years.[^cite_note-190] [Timber Sycamore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timber_Sycamore) was a classified weapons supply and training program run by the CIA and supported by some Arab intelligence services, such as the security service in [Saudi Arabia](349303.md). It launched in 2012 or 2013 and supplied money, weaponry and training to rebel forces. According to US officials, the program has trained thousands of rebels.[^cite_note-barrile-191] In July 2017, [H. R. McMaster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/H._R._McMaster), National Security Adviser to President [Donald Trump](4848272.md) and CIA Director [Mike Pompeo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Pompeo), decided to terminate the program.[^cite_note-192]

> Early on, Obama saw Recep Tayyip Erdoğan, the president of Turkey, as the sort of moderate Muslim leader who would bridge the divide between East and West—but Obama now considers him a failure and an authoritarian, one who refuses to use his enormous army to bring stability to Syria.[^cite_note-193]

— [Jeffrey Goldberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Goldberg), "The Obama Doctrine: The U.S. president talks through his hardest decisions about America's role in the world.", [The Atlantic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Atlantic) (April 2016)

On August 20, 2012, Obama used the "[Red line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_line_\(phrase\))" in relation to [chemical weapons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chemical_weapons).[^cite_note-The_Spectator_magazine_2013-194] On the one-year anniversary of Obama's red line speech, the [Ghouta chemical attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_Ghouta_attacks) occurred. [John McCain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_McCain) said the red line was "apparently written in disappearing ink", due to the perception the red line had been crossed with no action.[^cite_note-195] At the same time, [United States Central Command](338482.md) (CENTCOMM) approached the [YPG](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Protection_Units).

> In other words, this [illegitimate regime](364813.md) (reference to President Obama's statement[^cite_note-196]) was the source of these challenges and threats for Turkey, yet the Obama administration was unwilling to recognize it as such and act upon it accordingly. On the contrary, at the end of the day, the Obama administration allowed Assad's brutality to continue while supporting "his ally" Turkey's foes, on the ground.[^cite_note-Al_Jazeera-157]

— [Ahmet Davutoğlu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmet_Davuto%C4%9Flu) ([Minister of Foreign Affairs](10283896.md)), "From Obama to Trump: Lessons and challenges", [Al Jazeera English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Jazeera_English) (February 3, 2017)

[American intervention in the Syrian civil war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_intervention_in_the_Syrian_civil_war)



YPG-SDF [Mazloum Abdi](59386296.md)



1914 Obama, Special Presidential Envoy for the Global Coalition to Counter ISIL Ret. Gen. [Allen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_R._Allen), and Deputy Special Presidential Envoy [McGurk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brett_McGurk)

2015 November, Obama in Antalya on countering ISIL

December 1, 2015: President Obama delivers remarks on Syrian Civil War

Turkey-US relations began showing signs of deterioration, particularly over the handling of the YPG.[^cite_note-197] Some groups held the idea of "Syrian Balkanization" ("division of the country") in which they promoted [federalizing Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federalization_of_Syria) on ethnic and religious-sectarian lines, [Constitution of the Autonomous Administration of North and East Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_the_Autonomous_Administration_of_North_and_East_Syria). The American forces in the Syrian Civil War openly allied with the Kurdish YPG fighters and supported them militarily. The YPG was criticized by Turkey for its alleged support to the PKK, especially since a [rebellion in southern Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurdish%E2%80%93Turkish_conflict_\(2015%E2%80%93present\)) began in 2015.[^cite_note-auto-33] By early 2015, voices in the US foreign policy establishment pushed to abandon the rebels.[^cite_note-198] In early October 2015, shortly after the start of the [Russian military intervention in Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_military_intervention_in_the_Syrian_Civil_War), Obama was reported to have authorized the resupply of 25,000 YPG militia.[^cite_note-199] Erdoğan stated that he had asked Obama not to intervene on the side of the YPG: "I told Mr. Obama, 'Don't drop those bombs \[meaning weapons and other supplies]. You will be making a mistake.' Unfortunately, despite our conversation, they dropped whatever was needed with three C-130's and half of it landed in \[IS'] hands. So who is supplying \[ISIL], then?" Erdogan also opposed any arrangements in Syria that would mirror the Iraqi Kurds' *de facto* state in northern Syria. He told reporters on January 26, 2015: "What is this? Northern Iraq? Now \[they want] Northern Syria to be born. It is impossible for us to accept this. ... Such entities will cause great problems in the future."[^cite_note-200]

[Turkish involvement in the Syrian civil war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_involvement_in_the_Syrian_civil_war)



Actors involved in the Syrian civil war



[Syrian opposition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_opposition)

According to General [Raymond A. Thomas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raymond_A._Thomas) (at the time head of the [United States Special Operations Command](45204749.md) (USSOCOM)) at the [Aspen Security Forum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aspen_Security_Forum) in July 2017, the SDF (established October 10, 2015) is a [PR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_relations)-friendly name for the YPG, which Thomas personally suggested because the YPG is considered an arm of the PKK.[^cite_note-201] On February 1, 2016, Brett McGurk officially visited SDF commander [Ferhat Abdi Şahin](59386296.md) (also known as General [Mazloum Kobani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mazlum_Kobani)), after the [Siege of Kobanî](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Koban%C3%AE). In response, Erdoğan said: "How can we trust you? Is it me that is your partner or is it the terrorists in Kobani?"[^cite_note-202] After Kobani, General Allen and Brett McGurk worked on Tal Abyad. Turkey did not permit takeoff from a Turkish airbase. McGurk said: "So the picture that developed while General Allen and I were spending most of these months in Ankara is that something was not on the level \[the U.S. allied with Turkey's enemy]."[^cite_note-203] Turkey overtly defied American orders of ceasing Turkey's military bombardment of the YPG fighters in their bid to take the town of [Azaz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azaz) in northern Syria.[^cite_note-204]

In summary, during the Obama years, Turkey developed its policy towards Syria in two stages. The first stage was by itself. The second stage was with the US, which was unsuccessful. However, during obama years, Turkey was unwilling to act unilaterally toward Syria.[^cite_note-phillips-205]

1. Tried to persuade Assad to reform. Cut the diplomatic ties. Supported regional and international political solutions. Support and aid Syria's political and armed opposition.

2. Asked Obama to support direct military intervention, such as a no-fly zone or humanitarian corridor for the refugees. Obama administration rejected.

In summary, during the Obama years, the Obama administration did not respond to the idea that the [Rojava conflict](48303468.md), which the Obama administration associated itself with, provided a launch pad for Turkish Kurdish separatists toward Turkey and might raise questions about Turkey's territorial integrity.[^cite_note-phillips-205]

#### Palestine (Gaza flotilla raid)

[2014 Gaza War](43243199.md)



2013 Munich: [McCain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_McCain) and Davutoğlu at [Security Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/49th_Munich_Security_Conference) debating the future of the Middle East



2014 Paris: Davutoğlu, [John Kerry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Kerry) and [Khalid al-Attiyah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khalid_bin_Mohammad_Al_Attiyah) discussing [Israel](9282173.md)–[Hamas](13913.md) ceasefire deal

The Gaza flotilla raid was a military operation by Israel against six civilian ships of the "[Gaza Freedom Flotilla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Gaza_Freedom_Flotilla)" on May 31, 2010, in international waters in the Mediterranean Sea. [Israel–Turkey relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Turkey_relations) reached a low point after the incident. Turkey recalled its ambassador, canceled joint military exercises, and called for an emergency UN Security Council meeting. Erdoğan harshly referred to the raid as a "bloody massacre" and "state terrorism", and criticized Israel in a speech before the [Grand National Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_National_Assembly_of_Turkey). After Israel's military action, Turkish-Israeli relations have reached a low. Turkish Prime Minister Erdoğan accused Israel of 'state terrorism' and predicted in a speech to party members: 'From today on, nothing is anymore as it used to be.' He distanced himself clearly from antisemitic tones.[^cite_note-206]

On March 22, 2013, Netanyahu apologized for the incident in a 30-minute telephone call with Erdoğan, saying the results were unintended; the Turkish prime minister agreed to enter discussions to resolve the compensation issue. White House stated Erdoğan accepted the apology. The call took place at the airport as Obama was taking off, and at one point Obama jumped in to facilitate the peace.[^cite_note-restrictions-44] The issue again came up during [2013 Erdoğan visit](#2013_Erdoğan_visit). Obama's position was one of regret over the killing but not condemnation of Israel. It was stated that Obama had a "cautious, diplomatic response".[^cite_note-207]

#### Armenia (Genocide, Normalization)

During his Ankara visit, Obama urged Turkey to come to terms with its past and resolve its Armenian issues. During the [2008 US presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_United_States_presidential_election), he had criticized former US President George W. Bush for his failure to take a stance and stated that the "Armenian genocide is not an allegation, a personal opinion, or a point of view, but rather a widely documented fact supported by an overwhelming body of historical evidence".[^cite_note-BBC290409-208] He responded positively to an announcement from sources in Ankara and [Yerevan](34352.md) that a deal to reopen the border between the two states and exchange diplomatic personnel would happen, and indicated that although his own personal views on the subject remained unchanged, to avoid derailing this diplomatic progress, he would refrain from using the word "genocide" in his upcoming April 24 speech on the question.[^cite_note-LAT030409-209]

On April 22, 2009, shortly after Obama's visit, Turkish and Armenian authorities formally announced a [provisional roadmap](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenia%E2%80%93Turkey_relations#Announcement_of_provisional_roadmap_and_reactions) for the normalization of diplomatic ties between the two states.[^cite_note-TZ250409NYT-210] The U.S. responded positively with a statement from the office of [U.S. Vice President](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Vice_President) [Joe Biden](145422.md) following a phone conversation with [Armenian President](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenian_President) [Serzh Sargsyan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serzh_Sargsyan), which stated that "the Vice President applauded President Sargsyan's leadership, and underscored the administration's support for both Armenia and Turkey in this process".[^cite_note-TZ250409a-211] Turkish columnists, however, criticized the timing of the announcement, and believed it to have been made to placate Obama in advance of his April 24 speech, with Fikret Bila writing in the *[Milliyet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milliyet)* that "the Turkish Foreign Ministry made this statement regarding the roadmap before midnight", as it would allow Obama to go back on his campaign promise to refer to the incident as genocide, which the Turkish government profusely denied, by pointing out to the Armenian diaspora that "Turkey reached a consensus with Armenia and set a roadmap" and "there is no need now to damage this process".[^cite_note-TZ250409FD-212][^cite_note-TZ250409KS-213]

#### Arab Spring (Turkish model, Gazi Park)

[Turkish model](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_model)



[Arab Spring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Spring)



Gezi Park and the Turkish model



[Gezi Park protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gezi_Park_protests)

[Turkish model](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_model) refers to the idea of Turkey as a potential model for other Muslim-majority countries, particularly in the Middle East, combining a democratic government ([Secularism in Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secularism_in_Turkey)) with an Islamic identity ([Conservatism in Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservatism_in_Turkey)). Respectively represented by [Atatürk's reforms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atat%C3%BCrk's_reforms) and Erdogan's [Justice and Development Party (Turkey)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Justice_and_Development_Party_\(Turkey\)). Particularly early on [Obama administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_of_Barack_Obama), there was some discussion and hope that Turkey could serve as a positive example of a modern, democratic, and predominantly Muslim nation.[^cite_note-T-model-214]

Hugh Pope—The Wall Street Journal — wrote that “crowns the rediscovery of the strategic value of Turkey” after Prime Minister [Bülent Ecevit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B%C3%BClent_Ecevit)'s 2002 Washington visite which showcased Turkey's laicism as an effective way to combat Islamic radicalism.[^cite_note-T-model-214] In the Obama period, opposing side argued that the independence of the media (does Turkish media represent all groups?), judiciary (does Turkey have an independent judiciary?), and army (does Turkey have a professional army?). However, despite these questions, the Egyptian Muslim Brotherhood's rejection of the Turkish model revealed the limits of secularism's regional influence.

The [Arab Spring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Spring) was a series of anti-government protests, uprisings, and armed rebellions that spread across much of the Arab world in the early 2010s, resulting in the [Arab Winter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Winter). [Amb.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Ambassador_to_Turkey) [Jeffrey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Franklin_Jeffrey) stated that Turkey at a crossroads after the [Gezi Park Protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gezi_Park_Protests) (2013), and asked what do the Gezi Park Protests mean for democracy in the region?[^cite_note-215] Failure of the Turkish model in the American point of view during Obama years, as [Center for American Progress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Center_for_American_Progress) summary points out, was Turkey's relationship with the American-ally Kurdish militia, YPG, (Turkey's nomenclature PKK subsidiary group), or "Lack thereof."[^cite_note-T-model-214]

#### Gülen movement (coup d'état attempt)

The [AKP–Gülen movement conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AKP%E2%80%93G%C3%BClen_movement_conflict) is a major Turkey–United States relations. In the evening of July 15, 2016, a fraction attempted a [coup d'état](25914154.md), [2016 Turkish coup attempt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Turkish_coup_attempt), in Turkey.

Campbell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_F._Campbell_\(general\)), was the "mastermind" behind the coup attempt in Turkey.[^cite_note-222]

Due to perceptions that former US Secretary of State and Democratic Party presidential nominee [Hillary Clinton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hillary_Clinton) is friendly towards the [Gülen movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G%C3%BClen_movement), many Erdoğan supporters reportedly favored Republican Party presidential nominee [Donald Trump](4848272.md) in the United States' 2016 presidential election.[^cite_note-223]

The Turkish government had a generally warm relationship with the Trump administration,[^cite_note-224] backing the Trump administration's stance against [Antifa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antifa_\(United_States\)) groups during the [George Floyd protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Floyd_protests),[^cite_note-225] and condemning restrictions placed on Trump's social media accounts as "digital fascism".[^cite_note-226]

#### Saudi Arabia (Khashoggi)

The [assassination of Jamal Khashoggi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assassination_of_Jamal_Khashoggi), a [Saudi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi_people) [dissident](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dissident), journalist for *[The Washington Post](102226.md)*, and former general manager and editor-in-chief of [Al-Arab News Channel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Arab_News_Channel), occurred on October 2, 2018, at the Saudi [consulate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Consul_\(representative\)) in [Istanbul](3391396.md), Turkey, and was perpetrated by agents of the Saudi Arabian government.[^cite_note-nytasassination-227] Government officials of Turkey believe Khashoggi was murdered with premeditation. Anonymous Saudi officials have admitted that agents affiliated with the Saudi government killed him.[^cite_note-Guardian22OctGiveus-228]

CIA Director [Gina Haspel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gina_Haspel) traveled to Turkey to address the investigation. Haspel's visit came before a planned speech by Erdoğan. She listened to audio purportedly capturing the sound of saw on a bone. On November 20, US President [Donald Trump](4848272.md) rejected the CIA's conclusion that Crown Prince [Mohammed bin Salman](38689358.md) had ordered the killing. He issued a statement saying "it could very well be that the Crown Prince had knowledge of this tragic event — maybe he did and maybe he didn't" and that "in any case, \[their] relationship is with the Kingdom of Saudi Arabia".[^cite_note-229]

#### Qatar (diplomatic crisis)

On June 5, 2017, Turkey ([Qatar–Turkey relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qatar%E2%80%93Turkey_relations)) supported Qatar in its [diplomatic confrontation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Qatari_diplomatic_crisis) with a [Saudi](349303.md) and [Emirati](69328.md)-led bloc of countries that severed ties with and imposed sanctions on Qatar. US ([Qatar–United States relations](15994161.md)) was on the Saudi side, which argued on the basis of [Qatar and state-sponsored terrorism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qatar_and_state-sponsored_terrorism). Turkey criticized the list of demands released by the Saudi and Emirati-led bloc on June 22, stating that they undermine Qatar's sovereignty. In December 2017, for defense of US position, US national security advisor General H.R. McMaster said that Turkey had joined Qatar as a prime source of funding that contributes to the spread of extremist ideology of [Islamism](15012.md): "We're seeing great involvement by Turkey from everywhere from western Africa to Southeast Asia, funding groups that help create the conditions that allow terrorism to flourish."[^cite_note-Voice_of_America-230]

> A lot of Islamist groups have learned from Erdogan and his Justice and Development Party. It is a "model of really operating through civil society, then the education sector, then the police and judiciary, and then the military to consolidate power in the hands of a particular party (institution that is state-like in its organization, [parallel state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parallel_state)), which is something we'd prefer not to see and is sadly contributing to the drift of Turkey away from the West."[^cite_note-Voice_of_America-230]

— [National Security Adviser](44028.md), General [H.R. McMaster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/H.R._McMaster), US Official Accuses Turkey of Pushing Extreme Islamist Ideology, Voice of America

> We expect the United States, which we continue to recognize as our friend and ally, to display the same stance to our country, to cease all forms of cooperation with terrorist groups such as YPG and provide more concrete and effective support in our ongoing determined fight against terrorism and radicalism.

— [Ministry of Foreign Affairs](10283896.md), secretary to Turkish Foreign Minister., US Official Accuses Turkey of Pushing Extreme Islamist Ideology, Voice of America

On June 26, 2024, H.R. McMaster and [Ahmet Üzümcü](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmet_%C3%9Cz%C3%BCmc%C3%BC) (Turkish diplomat, former permanent representative to NATO) rehash the same issues at "Turkey: A Strained & Critical Alliance"[^cite_note-231] discussion for the Turkey's work in advancing peace and prosperity relation to fight against ISIS.[^cite_note-232]

#### Syria (Clashes at the Turkish Ambassador's Residence, Refugees, Pull-out, Barisha raid)







U.S. and Turkish forces conduct joint patrols on the outskirts of Manbij, Syria, November 8, 2018

Turkish and American soldiers in Gaziantep train for joint patrols in Manbij, Syria during the Syrian civil war, October 9, 2018

The [clashes at the Turkish Ambassador's Residence in Washington, D.C.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clashes_at_the_Turkish_Ambassador's_Residence_in_Washington,_D.C.) broke out on May 16, 2017, between Turkey's [Police Counter Attack Team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Police_Counter_Attack_Team) and a crowd of protesters, some of whom carried flags of the [Democratic Union Party (Syria)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Union_Party_\(Syria\)) (a left-wing Syrian branch of the [Kurdistan Workers Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurdistan_Workers_Party)).[^cite_note-233] On May 16, 2017 [John McCain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_McCain) and [Claire McCaskill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Claire_McCaskill) called for the expulsion ([unwanted person](521241.md)) of the Turkish ambassador [Serdar Kılıç](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serdar_K%C4%B1l%C4%B1%C3%A7) for the embassy brawl.[^cite_note-bbc.com-50]

In the first half of the 2010s, the [Turkey migrant crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkey's_migrant_crisis) developed, according to UNHCR in 2018, as Turkey hosted 63.4% of all refugees in the world. As of 2019, [refugees of the Syrian Civil War in Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Refugees_of_the_Syrian_Civil_War_in_Turkey) numbered the highest among "registered" refugees, with 3.6 million. Turkey approached the problem in two ways. First, it focused on managing their presence in Turkish society by addressing their legal status, basic needs, employment, education, and impact on local communities.[^cite_note-234] Second, Turkey encouraged the U.S. to establish safe zones; the Obama administration was concerned about the potential for pulling the U.S. into a war with Russia.[^cite_note-235] The [Trump travel ban](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trump_travel_ban) actions include two [executive orders](210541.md) for restrictions on citizens of seven (first executive order) or six (second executive order) [Muslim-majority countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_by_country).[^cite_note-236] A third action, done by [presidential proclamation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidential_proclamation), restricts entry to the U.S. by citizens from eight countries, six of which are predominantly Muslim. During and after his election campaign, Trump proposed establishing safe zones in Syria as an alternative to Syrian refugees' immigration to the US. In the past, "safe zones" have been interpreted as establishing, among other things, no-fly zones over Syria. In the first few weeks of Trump's presidency, Turkey renewed its call for safe zones and proposed a new plan. The Trump administration spoke with several other Sunni Arab States regarding safe zones, and Russia has asked for clarification regarding any Trump administration plan regarding safe zones.

In December 2018, Trump announced "We have won against ISIS", and ordered the withdrawal of all troops from Syria.[^cite_note-won-237] The next day, Mattis resigned in protest, calling his decision an abandonment of the U.S.'s [YPG](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/YPG) allies. In November 2018, Trump said he would not approve any extension of the American deployment in Syria.[^cite_note-238] House of Representatives condemned Trump's decision.[^cite_note-239] ISIS send a message to Trump with [2019 Manbij bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Manbij_bombing). The attack killed the wife of future Trump's [NCTC](3231506.md) director [Joe Kent](68079795.md).

On October 23, 2019, President Trump ordered the withdrawal of U.S. troops from the Syrian-Turkish border to southeastern Syria for enabling Turkey to set up [a new "Buffer Zone"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Northern_Syria_Buffer_Zone).[^cite_note-240]

Trump: "I order the withdrawal of all troops from Syria"

[^cite_note-won-237]

Defense Secretary James Mattis and the Chairman of the Joint Chiefs of Staff, Marine Gen. Joseph F. Dunford Jr., update the media on the defeat of the Islamic State of Iraq and Syria during a joint press conference

[James Mattis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Mattis) Resigned

[^cite_note-241]

#### Gülen Movement (extradition – Flynn – Brunson – Visa & Tariff)

In July 2016, after the [failed coup attempt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Turkish_coup_d'%C3%A9tat_attempt), Turkey demanded that the United States government extradite [Fethullah Gülen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fethullah_G%C3%BClen), a cleric and Turkish national living in the U.S. state of [Pennsylvania](23332.md). However, the US government demanded that Turkey had to produce evidence of Gulen's connection with the coup attempt. On July 19, an official request was sent to the US for the extradition of Fethullah Gülen.[^cite_note-auto2-254] Senior U.S. officials said this evidence pertained to certain pre-coup alleged subversive activities.[^cite_note-255]

On November 8, 2016, [The Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Hill_\(newspaper\)) published an op-ed by Flynn stating that US ally Turkey was in crisis and needed US support on the day of the [2016 United States presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_United_States_presidential_election). Flynn called for the US to back Erdoğan's government and alleged that the regime's opponent [Fethullah Gülen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fethullah_G%C3%BClen), who is a Pennsylvania-based opposition cleric and the leader of the [gulen movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulen_movement) whose members were [purged in Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016%E2%80%93present_purges_in_Turkey) because of the [2016 Turkish coup d'état attempt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Turkish_coup_d'%C3%A9tat_attempt), headed a "vast global network" that fit "the description of a dangerous [sleeper terror network](14558092.md)".[^cite_note-FlynnHillEd-256] Flynn, who was specialized in US counterterrorism strategy and dismantling insurgent networks, argued Gulen falls into radical Islamist groups aligned with Seyed Qutb and Hasan al Bana and he finished his article stating "running a scam". On March 8, 2017, four months after the publication, General Flynn filed documents with the Federal government indicating consulting work that might have aided the government of Turkey. The [Crossfire Hurricane (FBI investigation)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crossfire_Hurricane_\(FBI_investigation\)) ("Crossfire Razor"[^cite_note-257] – a counterintelligence investigation on Flynn) was taken over by the [Mueller special counsel investigation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mueller_special_counsel_investigation) (May 17, 2017). Flynn was part of Mueller's special counsel investigation.

(alleged plot) [Flynn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Flynn) delivering [Gulen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fethullah_Gulen) to Turkey

\[^cite_note-[www.cbsnews.com-258](http://www.cbsnews.com-258/)]



[Robert Mueller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Mueller). [Reza Zarrab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reza_Zarrab), a witness at [Halkbank](16045143.md) exchange gold for Iranian oil (Turkey oil trading controversy), offered evidence and became Mueller's key for Flynn in the [investigation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Special_Counsel_investigation).

[^cite_note-259]



*[Mueller report](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mueller_report)'*&#x73; conclusion on the issue ("concerned a country other than Russia (i.e., Turkey)") on page 183.



[Bijan Kian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bijan_Kian). Trial testimony indicated foreign customer was interested in classified government information on [Gülen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fethullah_G%C3%BClen), surveillance of [supporters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G%C3%BClen_movement), and likely terrorist links that might be turned up by Turkey's investigations of the cleric.

[^cite_note-260]



"Turkey Project:" Flynn's materially false statements and omissions tried at *[United States v. Flynn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_v._Flynn)*.

In 2017, the special counsel, [Robert Mueller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Mueller), began probing whether Michael Flynn was part of an alleged plot to kidnap the cleric for Turkey.\[^cite_note-[www.cbsnews.com-258](http://www.cbsnews.com-258/)] [Michael Flynn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Flynn)'s consulting company was hired by Inovo BV, a company owned by Kamil Ekim Alptekin. Alptekin also chairs the Turkish-American Business Council, an arm of the Foreign Economic Relations Board of Turkey (DEIK).[^cite_note-261] Trump transition aide [Bijan Kian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bijan_Kian) played key role in Flynn's connection to the case.[^cite_note-262] Federal investigators probing the lobbying work of national security adviser Michael Flynn are focused in part on the role of Bijan Kian.[^cite_note-263] In 2017, FBI investigated whether Fethullah Gulen skimmed money from [charter schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charter_schools) in the US.[^cite_note-264] [Gülen movement schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G%C3%BClen_movement_schools) is a network of more than 150 U.S. charter schools (as of 2017). Gulen-linked educational institutions (schools in varying categories, and classifications) are sizable with 300 in Turkey and over 1,000 worldwide.[^cite_note-265]

[Serkan Golge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serkan_Golge), a naturalized US citizen, was jailed in Turkey for three years on charges of participating in terrorism and conspiring against the government as a member of the Gülen movement. Metin Topuz, a US consulate employee, was charged with having links to Gülen and was arrested under "terror charges" by an Istanbul court. Topuz was the second US government employee in Turkey to be arrested in 2017. Pastor [Andrew Brunson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrew_Brunson) was charged with terrorism and espionage during the [purges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016%E2%80%93present_purges_in_Turkey) that followed the [2016 Turkish coup d'état attempt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Turkish_coup_d'%C3%A9tat_attempt).[^cite_note-267] The U.S-based Christian group [Voice of the Persecuted](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voice_of_the_Martyrs) took up the Brunsons’ cause, as had opposition Parliament members in Turkey and other Protestant pastors in the largely Muslim nation.[^cite_note-VOA-c-268] Brunson's fate was determined at the [Directorate General of Migration Management](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_of_Migration_Management) which gave the instruction for detention and deportation of Brunson."[^cite_note-VOA-c-268] [Selina Dogan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Selina_%C3%96zuzun_Do%C4%9Fan), a member from the [Republican People's Party](404748.md), attempted to free Brunson.[^cite_note-VOA-c-268] The Turkish government said the post-coup crackdown was not just on Gulen movement but also ([subversive activities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subversion)) links to Kurdish militants and terror coming from Islamic State.[^cite_note-VOA-c-268]

The United States suspended all non-immigrant visas for Turkey "indefinitely" following Topuz's arrest. Turkey retaliated against the US with suspensions of all US visas, including tourist visas, shortly after the US State Department made their announcement.[^cite_note-269] On August 1, 2018, the US Department of Treasury imposed sanctions on Justice minister [Abdulhamit Gül](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdulhamit_G%C3%BCl) and interior minister [Suleyman Soylu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suleyman_Soylu), who were involved in the detention of Brunson. [Daniel Glaser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniel_Glaser), the former Treasury official under Obama, said: "It's certainly the first time I can think of" the U.S. sanctioning a [NATO](21133.md) ally.[^cite_note-270] On August 10, 2018, Trump imposed punitive tariffs against Turkey after an impasse over Brunson's imprisonment and other issues.[^cite_note-271] On August 10, 2018, Trump tweeted that he would double tariffs ([first Trump tariffs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Trump_tariffs)) on Turkish steel and aluminum using the "[Section 232](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trade_Expansion_Act)" which was imposed on countries whose exports threaten to impair national security. Markets responded and the lira plunged. Depending on the calculation nearly 40% year to date or 20% just after the tweet.[^cite_note-272] [Michael Klein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Klein_\(World_Bank_official\)) said "It's not like the United States caused this, but there's probably some element of the trade spat \[signaling to] investors and Turkish residents that things were not going to get better."[^cite_note-273] With both internal structural weaknesses and US sanctions Turkey's economy fall into [Turkish economic crisis (2018–current)](57486052.md). Later in December 2022, the WTO ruled against the United States saying that there was no national security emergency that justified US invocation of the exception[^cite_note-274] The move prompted Erdoğan to say that the United States was "\[ex]changing a strategic NATO partner for a pastor" and that the US' behavior would force Turkey to look for new friends and allies.[^cite_note-hurriyetdailynews.com-275]

The presidential spokesperson, [İbrahim Kalın](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C4%B0brahim_Kal%C4%B1n), tweeted that the US is losing Turkey, and that the entire Turkish public is against U.S. policies.[^cite_note-hurriyetdailynews.com-275] In addition, the [Uşak Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U%C5%9Fak_Province) decided to stop running digital advertisement on United States-based social media platforms like [Facebook](7529378.md), [Google](1092923.md), [Instagram](31591547.md), [Twitter](9988187.md), and [YouTube](3524766.md), canceling all of their ads as a response to US sanctions on Turkey.[^cite_note-276] Turkey went on to say that it would retaliate against the raising of steel and aluminium tariffs by the U.S. administration[^cite_note-277] (The US had already imposed 10 percent and 25 percent additional tariffs on aluminum and steel imports respectively from all countries on March 23, 2018, but on August 13, 2018, it added additional tariffs on steel imports from Turkey).[^cite_note-278] Erdoğan said that Turkey will boycott electronic products from the US, using [iPhones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IPhone) as an example.[^cite_note-279] The [Keçiören](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ke%C3%A7i%C3%B6ren) Municipality in the [Ankara](802.md) decided not to issue business licenses to American brands including [McDonald's](2480627.md), [Starbucks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Starbucks) and [Burger King](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burger_King).[^cite_note-280] In addition, Turkey decided to increase tariffs on imports of a range of US products,[^cite_note-281]

> The Turkish people expected the United States to unequivocally condemn the attack and express solidarity with Turkey's elected leadership. It did not. The United States' reaction was far from satisfactory. Instead of siding with Turkish democracy, United States officials cautiously called for "stability and peace and continuity within Turkey." To make matters worse, there has been no progress regarding Turkey's request for the extradition of Fethullah Gulen under a bilateral treaty.[^cite_note-282]

— [Recep Tayyip Erdoğan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recep_Tayyip_Erdo%C4%9Fan), Erdogan: How Turkey Sees the Crisis With the U.S. (Aug. 10, 2018), The New York Times

On August 20, 2018, there were gunshots at the USA Embassy in Ankara. No casualties were reported and Turkish authorities detained two men as suspects.[^cite_note-283]

#### Greece (Aegean dispute)



October 5, 2019: Greece Mutual Defense Agreement



Aegean Issues

In 2018, the [CFR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_on_Foreign_Relations) recommended "the U.S. needs to develop alternatives to [Incirlik Air Base](1597543.md). The use of the base to advance U.S. interests is no longer assured."[^cite_note-Council_on_Foreign_Relations-66] The US and Greece signed a "Revised Defense Cooperation Agreement". The agreement was described as critical to responding to new security challenges in the eastern Mediterranean Sea. Pompeo said "We have told the Turks that illegal drilling is unacceptable, and we'll continue to take diplomatic action to make sure that we do as we do always: ensure that the lawful activity takes place in every space where international law governs. ... We're working to get ... everyone to de-escalate and find a set of outcomes that are mutually agreeable,"[^cite_note-284] The [Aegean dispute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aegean_dispute) is a set of interrelated controversies over sovereignty and related rights in the region of the Aegean Sea. The United States ambassador Geoffrey Pyatt stated that all the islands have the same rights to EEZ and continental shelf as the mainlands do, which is disputed.

In 2021, a new agreement, the "Greek-American Mutual Defense Cooperation Agreement" permitted the US military to use Georgula Barracks in Greece's central province of Volos, Litochoro Training Ground, and army barracks in the northeastern port city of Alexandroupoli apart from the naval base in [Souda Bay in Crete](49049283.md) which the US has been operating since 1969.[^cite_note-defensenews.com-285] In short couple years, Turkey saw shifting NATO powers to its western neighbor. [Anadolu Agency](43356543.md) reported growing US military presence in Greece can lead to undesired scenarios in the Aegean ‘Deploying more US troops to Greece would disrupt NATO's powers,’[^cite_note-aa.com.tr-68]

#### Armenia (Genocide Recognition, Nagorno-Karabakh War)

Further information: [Armenian genocide recognition](12001341.md) and [2020 Nagorno-Karabakh conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Nagorno-Karabakh_conflict)

In 2019, the [United States Congress](31756.md), with sponsors from Saudi Arabia, issued official recognition of the [Armenian genocide](140376.md), which was the first time the United States has officially acknowledged the genocide, having previously only unofficially or partially recognized the genocide.[^cite_note-290][^cite_note-291] Turkey, which has traditionally denied that such genocide existed, blasted the United States for inflaming tensions. Donald Trump has rejected the congressional resolution, stating that his administration's stance on the issue had not changed.[^cite_note-292]

[Eliot Engel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eliot_Engel), chairman of the House Foreign Affairs Committee, called the influence of third party actors like Turkey "troubling" at the [2020 Nagorno-Karabakh conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Nagorno-Karabakh_conflict).[^cite_note-293] In a letter to Secretary of State Pompeo, Senate Foreign Relations Committee ranking member [Bob Menendez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bob_Menendez), Senate Minority Leader [Chuck Schumer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chuck_Schumer), and several other lawmakers called for the Trump administration to "immediately suspend all sales and transfers of military equipment to Ankara."[^cite_note-The_Armenian_Weekly-294] As for the result, relations between the United States to Turkey and Azerbaijan further worsened, with Turkey accused the United States of sending weapons and supplies to Armenia, which Washington denied.[^cite_note-295]

On October 15, 2020, Secretary of State [Mike Pompeo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Pompeo) urged both sides to respect the humanitarian ceasefire and stated, "We now have the Turks, who have stepped in and provided resources to Azerbaijan, increasing the risk, increasing the firepower that's taking place in this historic fight."[^cite_note-296]

#### Air Defense (Patriot — S-400, F35, CAATSA)

Air Defense System

US Service members stand by a patriot missile battery in Gaziantep, Turkey

February 4, 2013: US [Patriot](52024.md) in Gaziantep.



[2015 Russian Sukhoi Su-24 shootdown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Russian_Sukhoi_Su-24_shootdown).

The US had leverage over Turkey. Turkey depended on the U.S. to control its airspace. Turkey has long pursued air-control radars and missile-defense systems. [^cite_note-297] Turkey got serious about acquiring a missile defense system early in the Obama administration. Turkey put in a bid that included the [MIM-104 Patriot](52024.md), which the US had already [given to 18 other](52024.md#Operators) militaries. In 2013, the US did not part with the Patriots. China's air defense system won in 2013.[^cite_note-298] In 2015, Turkey reversed its position to acquire [China's FD-2000](5341441.md) long-range air defense missile system. The Chinese reportedly refused Ankara's demands for technology transfer.

U.S. [Patriots](52024.md) that were allocated to the defense of Turkey were removed on August 16, 2015[^cite_note-Reuters-299] On November 24, 2015, three months after the Patriots pulled out, Turkey's lack of an air defense system became an issue when [2015 Russian Sukhoi Su-24 shootdown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Russian_Sukhoi_Su-24_shootdown) occurred by a patrolling Turkish F-16. Turkey stated it has the right to defend its airspace.[^cite_note-BBC-300] Russia claimed the US knew the flight path of the Sukhoi Su-24, and it was an issue between two NATO partners; two US officials claimed there was no such information.[^cite_note-301]

Primary motivation. Turkey had no way, other than patrolling the border with F-16s, to defend it. Secondary motivation. The Turkish Speaker of the House gave the response: Starting around 2015-2016, the U.S. began arming the YPG \[PKK's Syrian arm][^cite_note-Tr-US-PKK-40] in Syria. Turkish parliament interpreted the U.S. as seeking to establish a new state, a "terror corridor," along Turkey's southern border. Turkey had a due diligence in response to this U.S. stance. We got what we can.[^cite_note-Anger400-302] Tertiary motivation. Turkey wanted the S-400 for the exact same reason that the U.S. did not approve it. The [2016 Turkish coup d'état attempt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Turkish_coup_d'%C3%A9tat_attempt) added another major point of tension. An unexpected outcome was that several rebel F-16s targeted the presidential jet and bombed the parliament. Turkey wanted to protect its parliament, even by shooting down aircraft operated by the Turkish Air Force, if necessary.[^cite_note-Anger400-302][^cite_note-303]

In 2017, Erdogan confronted, "They give tanks, cannons and armored vehicles to the terror organization, but we can't procure some of our needs, although we want to pay the price."[^cite_note-304] Turkey, a NATO member since 1952, reportedly saw that Russian technology transfer was available. Turkey transferred the money for the [S-400 missile system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S-400_missile_system) on September 12, 2017.[^cite_note-305] Bloomberg reported "permission" of the sale of Patriot systems to Turkey as a breakthrough on December 18, 2018.[^cite_note-306] Just short of six months after the US breakthrough, Turkey received its first installment of the Russian S-400 missile defense system on July 12, 2019.[^cite_note-307] The patriots were offered to replace Russian ordinance was US\$3.5 billion while Turkey paid US\$2.5 billion for S-400m.&#x20;

Turkey cut from NATO procurement



[Hollen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Van_Hollen): Turkey has drifted as an ally&#x20;

[Graham](363279.md): Suspension from NATO

[Cheny](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liz_Cheney): We consider the costs of having to go back into situations \[Syria] where we’ve withdrawn from.

H.R.4692

&#x20;and S.2644

[^cite_note-312]

On July 22, 2019, Turkey claimed to retaliate against the "unacceptable" threat of US sanctions over Turkey's purchase of Russian S-400 missile defenses.[^cite_note-313]

On July 31, 2019, the United States decided to end the [F-35](11812.md) deal. Acting [Defense Secretary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defense_Secretary) [Patrick M. Shanahan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patrick_M._Shanahan) had warned Turkey that such a deal with Russia risks undermining its ties to [NATO](21133.md).[^cite_note-314] Turkey was a level-3 partner, building 900 parts for the F-35. Turkey had over 100 jets on advance order. Turkey also had 4 jets already paid for and produced for Turkish pilot training. Undersecretary of Defense for Acquisition [Ellen Lord](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ellen_Lord) and Deputy Undersecretary of Defense for Policy [David Trachtenberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Trachtenberg) were in a very rare public discussion: “Turkey cannot field a Russian intelligence collection platform in proximity to where the F-35 program makes repairs and houses the F-35.” Turkey, on multiple occasions, said, "We are not going to link or work these systems together.[^cite_note-315] Turkey said Turkish personnel will man the S-400s. Various Russian advisors and technicians for training and maintenance would be based in Turkey. Finally, the US said: "It has the power to keep the planes from moving to Turkish soil." Turkish pilots and maintenance personnel training in the U.S. were sent home the same day. 1914 dispute. Turks were reminded of a historical parallel: the [Reşadiye-class battleships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Re%C5%9Fadiye-class_battleship) were paid for, but shortly after World War I began, the British Royal Navy seized the ships. Germany replaced them with [two new cruisers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pursuit_of_Goeben_and_Breslau). Germany gave ships to the Ottomans to distance them from Britain. It became an important factor for the [Ottoman entry into World War I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottoman_entry_into_World_War_I).[^cite_note-316] Israel lobbied to drop Turkey from F-35 program. Israel used its behind-the-scenes influence to push the decision forward because excluding Turkey downgraded its military edge.[^cite_note-317]

The US threatened Turkey with [Countering America's Adversaries Through Sanctions Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Countering_America's_Adversaries_Through_Sanctions_Act) (CAATSA) over Turkey's decision to buy th S-400 missile defense system from Russia.[^cite_note-318][^cite_note-319]

On February 5, 2020, the US halted a secretive military intelligence cooperation program with Turkey against the PKK[^cite_note-Tr-US-PKK-40].[^cite_note-reu-drone-53] On December 8, 2020, the House of Representatives approved a sanctions package against Turkey due to its purchase of [S-400 missile system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S-400_missile_system) from Russia. The Trump administration said the president would veto the bill. Trump had earlier worked to delay passing sanctions against Turkey, but he lost the 2020 United States presidential election.[^cite_note-320] On December 14, 2020, the US imposed [Countering America's Adversaries Through Sanctions Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Countering_America's_Adversaries_Through_Sanctions_Act). The sanctions included a ban on all US export licenses and authorizations to SSB, an asset freeze, and visa restrictions on Dr. Ismail Demir, SSB's president, and other SSB officers.[^cite_note-321]

Subsequently, several international policy analysts raised doubts that military sanctions on the [NATO](21133.md) ally would weaken the alliance, effectively reducing Turkey's ability to obtain American technology for regional defense. For this reason, the incoming [Biden administration](65732174.md) would likely hold off on sanctions to normalize relations.[^cite_note-322][^cite_note-323][^cite_note-324]

[^cite_note-327]

On May 22, 2022, after the US embassy issued a warning that police might respond violently to an opposition gathering in Istanbul, Turkey's foreign relations ministry summoned Ambassador Jeff Flake.[^cite_note-328]

In October 2021, in the wake of the appeal for the release of Turkish activist [Osman Kavala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osman_Kavala) signed by 10 Western countries, Turkish president [Recep Tayyip Erdoğan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recep_Tayyip_Erdo%C4%9Fan) ordered his foreign minister to declare the US ambassador *[persona non grata](521241.md)*, alongside the other 9 ambassadors.[^cite_note-329] However, the ambassadors did not receive any formal notice to leave the country and Erdoğan eventually stepped back.[^cite_note-330]

#### Russia (Russo-Ukrainian War, Grain Deal, Truce)

In the [Russo-Ukrainian War](42085878.md), the US supported Ukraine and sanctioned Russia; however, the conflict brought challenges to Turkey in balancing its relations with both Ukraine and Russia, with implications for US-Turkey ties.&#x20;

[Russo-Ukrainian War](42085878.md) and [peace negotiations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peace_negotiations_in_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine)

NATO summit

[Erdogan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recep_Tayyip_Erdo%C4%9Fan), [Zelenskyy](59210523.md), [Sunak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rishi_Sunak) and [Biden](145422.md)

A vector graphic showing the countries involved and the principles of the agreement

[Black Sea Grain Initiative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_Sea_Grain_Initiative)



[Erdogan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recep_Tayyip_Erdo%C4%9Fan), [Zelenskyy](59210523.md), [Fidan](39996069.md)



The Istanbul Communique: The Talks That Could Have Ended the War

[^cite_note-331]



Complex dynamics: "Emergency Meeting" with NATO in [G20 Bali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_G20_Bali_summit). Erdogan was left uninformed/snub/forgotten in the hotel room.

[^cite_note-332]

Turkey in addition to denouncing Russia's invasion, and closing the Straits to belligerent warships (including US warships) supplied Ukraine with various types of military equipment—including armed drone aircraft and mine-resistant ambush-resistant (MRAP) vehicles—as well as humanitarian assistance.[^cite_note-crs-3.gov-333] Turkey-Ukraine close ties was a response to mutual interests in countering Russian influence (they are also part of [Organization of the Black Sea Economic Cooperation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organization_of_the_Black_Sea_Economic_Cooperation)) in the [Black Sea](3386.md) region and in sharing military technology to expand and increase the self-sufficiency of their respective defense industries.[^cite_note-crs-3.gov-333] "[Bayraktar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bayraktar_\(song\))" is the Ukrainian patriotic military propaganda song which parodies both the Russian Armed Forces and the invasion itself.

Turkey's basis for continued engagement with Russia, and its desire to help mediate the conflict, was to minimize spillover effects on Turkey's national security and economy.[^cite_note-crs-3.gov-333] On March 5, 2022, the Turkish Foreign Ministry stated after discussions with NATO partners' deputy foreign ministers that Turkey and the US will continue to work in "tight coordination" to find a diplomatic solution to Russia's invasion of Ukraine.[^cite_note-334] On the other hand, Turkey rejected the US economic sanctions against Russia and did not close its airspace to Russian civilian flights.[^cite_note-crs-4.gov-335] In April 2022, Russia and Ukraine were on the verge of signing a peace agreement in Istanbul, with the Turkish Government acting as mediator. March 2022 British Prime Minister Boris Johnson forced Ukraine not to sign the peace deal in Istanbul. October 2022, Erdoğan said Putin "is now more open to possible peace talks" and Ukraine "was not rejecting such peace talks"[^cite_note-336]

President Biden expressed consent for Turkey and the United Nations to develop parallel agreements with Russia and Ukraine to provide a Black Sea corridor for Ukrainian grain exports to address global supply concerns. The [Black Sea Grain Initiative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_Sea_Grain_Initiative) was achieved by Turkey as it regulates access to the [Turkish Straits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_Straits) through the [Montreux Convention Regarding the Regime of the Straits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montreux_Convention_Regarding_the_Regime_of_the_Straits), so by virtue of its strategic position, it has the power to mediate between the parties on various issues of contention.[^cite_note-crs-4.gov-335]

#### Greece (Aegean dispute, Cyprus–Turkey maritime zones dispute)

Cyprus–Turkey



[Cyprus-Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyprus%E2%80%93Turkey_maritime_zones_dispute)



[CRS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congressional_Research_Service) Report & US Position

[Devlet Bahçeli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Devlet_Bah%C3%A7eli) claimed US bases pose "a threat to our security." "America is using the Greek side as a pawn ... The subject of 12 islands is our wound that has not yet healed. They have been unjustly usurped from Turkey by foot tricks", Bahçeli reiterated.[^cite_note-337] Erdoğan suspended dialogue with Greek Prime Minister Kyriakos Mitsotakis after Mitsotakis appeared to raise concern about the possible sale of 40 new F-16Vs to Turkey, while addressing a May 2022 joint session of the U.S. Congress.[^cite_note-crs-4.gov-335]

The [Cyprus–Turkey maritime zones dispute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyprus%E2%80%93Turkey_maritime_zones_dispute) is a major contention in the region. For the U.S., Israel, the Republic of Cyprus and Greece, the claimed [EEZ of Greece](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exclusive_economic_zone_of_Greece), which extends to the East Mediterranean thanks to the small island of [Kastellorizo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kastellorizo) near [Anatolia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anatolia), is promoted as ensuring stability, economic development, and regional integration, especially regarding Israel's interests and concerns.[^cite_note-3plus1-338] The multilateral “3+1” initiative consisting of Israel, Cyprus and Greece, and supported by the United States, aims to diversify Europe's energy supplies through the [Leviathan gas field](30257584.md) off the coast of Israel in the East Mediterranean. However, it ruled out Turkey's position regarding its own EEZ in the region, which conflicts with the claim by Greece.[^cite_note-3plus1-338] In Turkey's view, the Greek islands close to Anatolia do not provide a [contiguous maritime border](496983.md#Contiguous_zone) between Greece and Turkey, especially in the area between [Rhodes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rhodes) and [Kastellorizo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kastellorizo), which are [78 miles apart](496983.md#Contiguous_zone), when the maximum internationally recognized limit for a contiguous maritime border claim is 12 miles (the 1923 [Treaty of Lausanne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Lausanne) was signed in a period when national [territorial waters](496983.md) were limited to 3 miles). Turkey responded to its exclusion from the "3+1" initiative by signing the [Libya (GNA)–Turkey maritime deal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Libya_\(GNA\)%E2%80%93Turkey_maritime_deal).

#### Palestine (Hamas, Peace talks)

[DoS](31975.md) on Erdogan



May, 2021: DoS: "We condemned Erdogan’s anti-Semitic comments"

[^cite_note-DOSErdogan-339]

&#x20;Erdogan, via spokesperson, "Enabling an apartheid regime in its repression of innocent people in occupied territories and then turning around and blaming others who call it out is the height of hypocrisy.”

[^cite_note-340]

On May 18, 2021, the United States is deeply committed to combating anti-Semitism in all of its forms, and must always counter lies with facts and answer crimes of hate with justice condemned Erdogan’s anti-Semitic comments.[^cite_note-DOSErdogan-339] The official response was made by the Turkish foreign office, not by Erdogan's office. The clarification was as follows: "The concept of anti-Semitism should not be exploited in order to exempt the current Israeli government from criticism, or provide it with impunity from systematic ethnic, religious and cultural cleansing policies as well as attacks against the Palestinian people by the current Israeli Administration in the occupied Palestinian territories."[^cite_note-341]

On October 7, 2023, [Turkish support for Hamas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_support_for_Hamas) changed position following the [October 7 attacks](74998317.md). The Turkish government asked the members of Hamas to leave the country, after pictures and videos of the terrorist attacks began to be published and broadcast by the international media.[^cite_note-342] Hamas leader [Ismail Haniyeh](41207463.md) and other top officials were “politely sent away.”[^cite_note-343] On October 9, 2023, Blinken posted on X: "I encouraged Türkiye's advocacy for a cease-fire and the release of all hostages held by Hamas immediately."[^cite_note-344] After protests from Israel, Blinken decided to delete his tweet which called for a cease-fire in the 2023 [Gaza war](74998519.md).[^cite_note-345] On October 25, 2023, Turkey changed position once again following reports of the "humanitarian tragedy in the Gaza Strip", and in Erdoğan's words, Hamas was now "protecting its land and people".[^cite_note-346]

In 2024, Erdoğan and the AKP government in Turkey promoted the position that Hamas needs to be part of the political process.[^cite_note-347] On November 18, 2024, the United States warned Turkey against harboring Hamas leaders.[^cite_note-348]

#### Israel (Normalization, Gaza War, NATO)

After the [previous rupture in 2010](#Palestine_\(Davos_incident_–_Gaza_flotilla_raid\)), multiple U.S. administrations worked to put the pieces of the relationship back on track between two key allies. A hot conflict between these two U.S. allies would almost certainly take the Israel-Turkey relationship across the rubicon of reparability, which in turn could trigger unresolvable problems for U.S.-Turkey relations. Erdoğan and Netanyahu met for the first time in person at the United Nations headquarters in [New York City](645042.md) on September 20, 2023. They were slowly improving ties strained by disputes over Israel's policies toward the Palestinians.[^cite_note-Golden-349] Following the handshake of the leaders, Israel's media reported an upcoming visit by Erdoğan to Israel, with a pilgrimage to the [Al-Aqsa Mosque](65759.md).[^cite_note-Golden-349] The promise of cooperation on energy was the main topic. Israeli gas would be supplied to Europe through Turkey.[^cite_note-Golden-349] If achieved, the pipeline would also enable Israel to become a transit route for additional supplies of natural gas to Europe from Egypt, Saudi Arabia and the United Arab Emirates.[^cite_note-Golden-349]

Turkey blocked cooperation between NATO and Israel by opposing to the Western \[U.S.] position, stating that the alliance should not support Israel's [Gaza war](74998519.md), which would be a violation of NATO's founding principles.[^cite_note-reuters.com-350] Turkey stated that the cooperation between NATO and Israel could continue only after an end to the conflict.[^cite_note-reuters.com-350]

On September 6, 2024, Turkish-American human rights activist [Ayşenur Ezgi Eygi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ay%C5%9Fenur_Ezgi_Eygi) was shot in the head by an [Israel Defense Forces](39237.md) (IDF) sniper during a protest against illegal [Israeli settlements](15123.md) in [Beita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beita,_Nablus), [Nablus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nablus_Governorate), in the [West Bank](33209.md). Turkish President Recep Tayyip Erdogan described the killing by Israel as "barbaric".[^cite_note-351] Relations between Turkey and Israel, a major ally of the United States, continued to deteriorate.[^cite_note-352]

A [Pew Research](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pew_Research_Center) poll in 2024 showed that 85% of Turks were dissatisfied with the way the U.S. president, Joe Biden, handled the [Israel's war in Gaza](74998519.md).[^cite_note-353]

#### Armenia (Genocide Recognition, Nagorno-Karabakh conflict, Normalization)

Presidential candidate and former Vice President [Joe Biden](145422.md) demanded that Turkey "stay out" of the [Nagorno-Karabakh conflict](4695860.md) between [Armenia](10918072.md) and [Azerbaijan](746.md), in which Turkey has supported the Azerbaijanis.[^cite_note-354]

On December 15, 2021, U.S. Secretary of State [Antony Blinken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antony_Blinken) announced that the U.S. supports the normalization process between Armenia and Turkey.[^cite_note-355]

#### Genocide (Armenian, Uygur, Gaza)

On October 27, 2020, the [U.S. Senate](24909346.md) designated the [persecution of Uyghurs in China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persecution_of_Uyghurs_in_China)'s [Xinjiang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xinjiang) [autonomous region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Autonomous_regions_of_China) as genocide.[^cite_note-fore_Mene-356] On January 19, 2021, incoming U.S. president Joe Biden's [secretary of state](32293.md) nominee [Antony Blinken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antony_Blinken) was asked, and he contended, "That would be my judgment as well."[^cite_note-357]

On April 24, 2021, [Armenian Genocide Remembrance Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenian_Genocide_Remembrance_Day), President [Joe Biden](145422.md) referred to the massacre of the Armenians during the Ottoman Empire in 1915 as "genocide" in a statement released by the White House.[^cite_note-358] Turkey has long practiced a policy of denial against the Armenian Genocide, and Biden's move was refuted by President Erdogan as "groundless" and opening a "deep wound" in U.S.-Turkey relations.[^cite_note-359]

On February 9, 2024, [Turkish](11125639.md) Foreign Minister [Hakan Fidan](39996069.md) said that the international community's silence on [Israel](9282173.md)'s actions in [Gaza](12047.md) were "complicity in [genocide](75668427.md)".[^cite_note-360]
