#### Economic Development (Iraq Development Road, Zangezur corridor)

Trade Corridors

IMEC and its connections

[India-Middle East-Europe Economic Corridor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India-Middle_East-Europe_Economic_Corridor)

Iraq Development Road

[Iraq Development Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraq_Development_Road)

President Joe Biden and his allies announced a plan to build a rail and shipping corridor.[^cite_note-361] The [India-Middle East-Europe Economic Corridor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India-Middle_East-Europe_Economic_Corridor) is a planned economic corridor that aims to bolster economic development by fostering connectivity between Asia, the Persian Gulf and Europe. The [Iraq Development Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraq_Development_Road) is an undergoing project aiming to connect Asia with Europe.[^cite_note-362] In September 2023, IMEEC criticised by Turkey as the existing but underdeveleoped Iraq-Turkey connection already present viable-established but underutilized corridor which could reach its potential with the [Iraq Development Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraq_Development_Road). The project was envisaged to bolster the current connection through the Persian Gulf with Europe through a railway and highway via ports in the [United Arab Emirates](69328.md), [Qatar](8238258.md), and [Iraq](7515928.md), including the under-construction [Grand Faw Port](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Faw_Port).[^cite_note-363]

|  | [Biden economic corridor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India%E2%80%93Middle_East%E2%80%93Europe_Economic_Corridor) | [Iraq Development Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraq_Development_Road) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Time table | September 2023 (Momerandum) | April 2024 (Momerandum) |
| States involved | Asia → United Arab Emirates → Saudi Arabia → Israel → Greece → Europe | Asia → Iraq → Turkey → Europe |

The [Zangezur corridor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zangezur_corridor) became a flash point since the end of the [Second Nagorno-Karabakh War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Nagorno-Karabakh_War). Azerbaijan and Turkey have been promoting this trade route to connect Azerbaijan to [Nakhchivan](385358.md) through [Armenia](10918072.md)'s [Syunik Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syunik_Province). Armenia steadily objected to this connection. The contention between Azerbaijan and Armenia extent to the terminology, the potential routes, and the modes of transport. After Armenian forces blocked the Zangezur corridor in 2023, [American Enterprise Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Enterprise_Institute) scholar [Michael Rubin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Rubin_\(historian\)) called Biden to act,[^cite_note-American_Enterprise_Institute-70] and to start talks with Armenia to establish a military base in the Zangezur corridor.[^cite_note-Azer_news-71] On September 11, 2023, a small contingent of U.S. special forces trained with Armenian soldiers during the "Eagle Partner" exercise in Armenia.[^cite_note-aljazeera.com-364]

#### Alliances (Enlargement of NATO, BRICS)

The [enlargement of NATO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enlargement_of_NATO) continued with two new members during President Biden's term, [Finland and Sweden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enlargement_of_NATO#Finland_and_Sweden). Turkey initially insisted on keeping its veto power on their membership, citing concerns over "combating terrorism" to be extended specifically to include "the [PKK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PKK), [PYD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Union_Party_\(Syria\)), [YPG](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/YPG), and [FETÖ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G%C3%BClen_movement)" in the tripartite memorandum between Finland, Sweden, and Turkey during the NATO summit in Madrid in June 2022. For well over a year, Turkey used this power to seek concessions from the Western bloc, which did not recognize the PYD, YPG, or FETÖ as "terrorist entities" but recognizes the PKK[^cite_note-Tr-US-PKK-40] as such. [Finland-Turkey negotiations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Finland%E2%80%93NATO_relations#Finland_June_2022_%E2%80%93_April_2023:_Ratification) ended with these issues resolved. Turkey also saw Sweden's difficult domestic membership application issues (e.g., [2023 Quran burnings in Sweden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Quran_burnings_in_Sweden)) as a moment of leverage not just for terrorism, but also for obtaining 40 new F-16Vs from the United States, as it [blocked the negotiations between the parties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sweden%E2%80%93NATO_relations#Obstacles_in_Turkey's_process).[^cite_note-365] On May 18, 2022, the U.S. brought the issue of listing the PYD and YPG[^cite_note-Tr-US-PKK-40], defined as "allies" by the United States in the war against ISIS, but viewed by Turkey as affiliates of the PKK, and the Gülen movement as "terrorist organizations" to the table for clarification.[^cite_note-USseeksclarity-366] Regarding the aforementioned US-Turkey discussions, President Erdoğan stated that "neither country has an open, clear stance against \[the mentioned] terrorist organizations. We cannot say 'yes' to those who impose sanctions on Turkey \[during our fight against our enemy], on joining NATO, which is \[essentially] a security organization."[^cite_note-USseeksclarity-366] On June 29, 2022, U.S. President [Joe Biden](145422.md) thanked Turkish President [Recep Tayyip Erdoğan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recep_Tayyip_Erdo%C4%9Fan) for paving the way for Finland and Sweden's membership at the [NATO summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Madrid_summit) in [Madrid](41188263.md).[^cite_note-367]

According to Russian media reports (never officially confirmed by Ankara) Turkey requested [BRICS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BRICS) membership. If admitted, Turkey would become a part of the first significant counterweight (navigating to multipolarity) to the U.S.-led global order.[^cite_note-368]

#### Air Defense (S400, F-35, F-16C)

During the first Trump administration, on October 2, 2020, House majority-minority leaders and Senate Foreign Relations Committee ranking member [Bob Menendez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bob_Menendez), along with other senators, suspended all sales and transfers of advanced military equipment to Ankara.[^cite_note-The_Armenian_Weekly-294] The U.S. Congress used its power to block sales and began slowrolling for the proposed sale of 40 new F-16Vs and 79 upgrade kits to Turkey, as well as the sale of [LHTEC T800](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LHTEC_T800) engines for [TAI/AgustaWestland T129 ATAK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TAI/AgustaWestland_T129_ATAK) attack helicopters produced by Turkey, which could not be exported to any third country without obtaining an export license for these engines from the Congress.[^cite_note-blocking-369] As a result, Turkey faced issues in [exporting the helicopters to Pakistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TAI/AgustaWestland_T129_ATAK#Pakistan). The hold-up had also involved Ankara's overflight disputes with Athens over the Aegean Sea. This dispute was resolved with the approval for the sale of F-35A fighter jets to Greece. In December, when the final version of the FY2023 NDAA (P.L.117-263) was passed, it excluded a House-passed condition on F-16 sales to Turkey related to potential overflights above Greek islands in the Aegean Sea. Regarding the slowrolling of the issue by the Congress, in January 2024, Chairman of the Senate Foreign Relations Committee, Democrat [Ben Cardin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ben_Cardin), stated that the approval for the sale of 40 new F-16V fighter jets and 79 upgrade kits to Turkey was contingent on Turkey's approval for [Sweden](5058739.md)'s [accession to NATO](#Alliances_\(Enlargement_of_NATO,_BRICS\)).[^cite_note-370] A letter to the Congress at the same time stated that if unable to upgrade its F-16 fleet, Turkey might consider purchasing [Eurofighter Typhoon](167667.md) aircraft. However, Erdoğan seized the moment to give his presidential approval for Sweden's NATO membership on January 25, 2024.

Senator [Bob Menendez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bob_Menendez), who had earlier expressed his opposition to the sale of F-16Vs to Turkey, stated that he still maintained this view despite the Sweden-Turkey deal over the former's NATO membership, brokered by President Biden.[^cite_note-371] On March 21, 2024, Bob Menendez announced that due to federal corruption charges (including bribery, extortion, honest services fraud, obstruction of justice, and conspiracy) he would not run in the Democratic primary. He later resigned from the Senate and was subsequently sentenced to 11 years in prison following his conviction on bribery and corruption charges on January 29, 2025.[^cite_note-372]

On June 6, 2024, the Turkish government signed a letter of acceptance (LOA) for the acquisition of 40 new F-16V aircraft.[^cite_note-Janes_10.06.2024-373] On November 26, 2024, the Turkish government announced that it intends to move forward with the acquisition of 40 new F-16V aircraft, but has decided to cancel the purchase of upgrade kits to bring 79 of its existing F-16 Block 40 and Block 50 aircraft to the Block 70 "Viper" standard,[^cite_note-FlightGlobal_28.11.2024-374] opting instead to upgrade all of its Block 30, Block 40 and Block 50 aircraft with the domestically produced kits that were developed under the F-16 "Özgür" and "Özgür-2" upgrade programs.[^cite_note-FlightGlobal_28.11.2024-374][^cite_note-375]

On November 27, 2024, the Turkish government announced that the Turkish Air Force also intends to order 40 new F-35A aircraft, if a deal can be reached with the United States regarding the crisis caused by Turkey's purchase of the S-400 air defense system from Russia.[^cite_note-376] The United States has so far presented two solutions to Turkey: On March 19, 2022, the U.S. proposed Turkey to deliver its Russian-made S-400 missile defence systems to Ukraine, in order to assist it in fighting the invading Russian forces.[^cite_note-377] Two years later, during a visit to Turkey on July 1–2, 2024, [Celeste Wallander](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Celeste_Wallander), the Assistant Secretary of Defense for International Security Affairs, and [Michael R. Carpenter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_R._Carpenter), special advisor to the President and Senior Director for Europe at the [U.S. National Security Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._National_Security_Council), proposed stationing the S-400 missile systems at the U.S.-controlled sector of [Incirlik Air Base](1597543.md), in exchange for reinstating Turkey in the F-35 fighter jet program.[^cite_note-378] Both of these proposals have so far been rejected by the Turkish government.[^cite_note-379][^cite_note-380]

### Second Trump administration (2025–present)

#### Iran

President Donald Trump said on 14 January 2026 that countries that do business with Iran will face a new 25% tariff. This includes Turkey which imports 16% of Iran's goods to as of 2023.[^cite_note-381]

#### Syria (territorial integrity, return of refugees)

At the first phone call between the two leaders, Turkey stressed the importance of lifting restrictions on Syria to start the reconstruction work for the [return of refugees of the Syrian civil war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Return_of_refugees_of_the_Syrian_civil_war).[^cite_note-2025Truce-382]

Syrian YPG (Syrian-Kurdish general commander of the SDF, Mazloum Abdi) expects continued military aid and defense umbrella from the Trump administration.[^cite_note-2025kurds-383] Secretary of State Marco Rubio, during his Senate confirmation hearing, stated support for the idea of Syrian Kurdish forces.[^cite_note-2025kurds-383] On January 25, 2025, Turkey clarified its position to EU at the joint news conference with visiting EU foreign policy chief [Kaja Kallas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaja_Kallas) in Ankara. Foreign Minister Hakan Fidan's call for resolution was: Turkey awaits US action.[^cite_note-384]

> The fight against terrorism in Syria, including the PKK and ISIS, is now the responsibility of the new administration. We are ready to provide our support. We are also ready to support the new administration in the management of the camps ([Al-Hawl refugee camp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Hawl_refugee_camp)) in northeastern Syria. In this regard, our rightful expectation from all our partners is to end their relations with the PKK's affiliates \[YPG] in Syria.

— [Hakan Fidan](39996069.md), Türkiye awaits US to address bilateral issues from the ex-administration, [^cite_note-FidanCATSAA-385]

#### Israel (Truce, Gaza Peace Summit, Abu al-Duhur)

Israel and [Lech-Lecha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lech-Lecha)



[Huckabee](301237.md): "it would be fine if they took it all." ("[river of Egypt to the great river, the Euphrates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lech-Lecha#Sixth_reading%E2%80%94Genesis_15\:7%E2%80%9317\:6)")

[^cite_note-386]



Biblical reference at Genesis 15\:7–17\:6, [Greater Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Israel).



[Fidan](39996069.md): "security as a pretext to acquire 'more land'"

[^cite_note-387]



[2025 Gaza Peace Summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Gaza_Peace_Summit)



[Barrack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tom_Barrack): Israel's Syria strike risked military confrontation with Turkey

[^cite_note-388]



Trump: I can mediate Turkey-Israel over Syria. Netanyahu: "We've had neighborly relations with Turkey that have deteriorated, and we don't want to see Syria being used by anyone, including Turkey, as a base for attack in Israel."

[^cite_note-389]

The [2025 Israel–Hamas war ceasefire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/January_2025_Gaza_war_ceasefire) was signed with [President-elect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President-elect_of_the_United_States) [Trump](4848272.md)'s active involvement, along with Egypt and Qatar. The first phase took effect on January 19, 2025, a day before President Trump's [inauguration](4848272.md#Second_presidency_\(2025%E2%80%93present\)). President Erdoğan called on the international community to fulfill its responsibilities toward ending the suffering of innocent civilians.[^cite_note-2025Truce-382] On October 13, 2025, the [2025 Gaza Peace Summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Gaza_Peace_Summit) following an agreement to implement the first phase of the [Gaza peace plan](81215079.md). Egyptian President Abdel Fattah el-Sisi, Donald Trump, Qatari Emir Sheik [Tamim bin Hamad Al Thani](6146151.md), and Recep Tayyip Erdoğan all signed a joint declaration known as the "Trump Declaration for Enduring Peace and Prosperity." Representatives for Israel and Hamas were absent. It did not have a binding effect on the State of Israel.&#x20;

Erdoğan and Netanyahu reached a kinetic level because of Gaza. On November 7, 2025, the Istanbul High Criminal Court initiated proceedings against Netanyahu and 36 other senior Israeli officials over actions in Gaza and maritime intercepts.[^cite_note-390] Formal Indictment presented in April 2026. Concurrently, Netanyahu portrayed Turkey as a major threat. Turkey argued that Benjamin Netanyahu's increasing rhetoric toward Turkey was driven by domestic political pressures and the genocide case against him, rather than strategic realities between the two countries.[^cite_note-391] The finalized indictment included charges over [2025 Global Sumud Flotilla](80704925.md). April 28, 2026, [Isaac Herzog](4645588.md) took an eight-hour detour to reach Kazakhstan, avoiding Turkish airspace.[^cite_note-392] The warrants froze lukewarm diplomatic ties and heightened long-term regional friction. The post-war security arrangements in Gaza became more complicated. The court issued an Interpol Red Notice request in July 2026. Filed under No. 2026/108, the case involves 35 defendants, including Netanyahu and Afek Moskovitch.[^cite_note-393] August 9, 2026, Netanyahu rejected Trump's 15-point plan for Gaza.[^cite_note-394] Turkey, alongside Egypt, Qatar, Saudi Arabia, the UAE, Jordan, Pakistan, and Indonesia, issued a joint statement holding Israel fully responsible for derailing.&#x20;

August 17, 2026, Netanyahu’s said Israel had [2026 Abu al-Duhur Air Base airstrikes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Abu_al-Duhur_Air_Base_airstrikes) because Syria had been “on the verge of breaching” an agreed-upon “status quo in security matters” by allowing Turkish troops to deploy there. Turkey said its Syrian assistance was provided at the request of the Syrian government and is intended to reinforce the country’s unity, territorial integrity and ability to combat terrorism, and Israel was informed by the U.S. that Turkey's Syrian support covers training, consultancy, technical assistance and reciprocal visits aimed at strengthening Syria’s defense capacity against ISIS.[^cite_note-395] The Foreign Ministry of Turkey condemned the attack. [United States Presidential Envoy for Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_ambassadors_of_the_United_States_to_Syria) [Tom Barrack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tom_Barrack) stated that Syria neither “adopted a predatory posture nor maintained proxy forces” but has repeatedly indicated a preference for de-escalation with Israel.[^cite_note-396] White House condemned 'unnecessary escalation'.[^cite_note-397]

U.S. Special Envoy Tom Barrack gave an interview on how U.S. deconflicting Israel and Turkey.[^cite_note-398] Amb Tom Barak clarified that The U.S. government does not believe the Istanbul papers quoting "[Blueprint for the Middle East](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yinon_Plan#Blueprint_for_the_Middle_East)" and parallels on Israel's current national security policies. However, [Hakan Fidan](39996069.md) told at the [Antalya Diplomacy Forum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antalya_Diplomacy_Forum): "Israel has created an illusion internationally, claiming that Israel is in favor of its own security, but it has become very clear, especially in the recent years ... it is more than that, ... from the Palestinian lands, Gaza, the West Bank, East Jerusalem, and now Lebanon and Syria, it was an onward occupation and expansionism in the region."[^cite_note-399] The attack occurred on the day SDF’s integration with Damascus was complete.[^cite_note-400]

#### Palestine (Öztürk)

In 2025, while the U.S. government did not release an exact country-by-country breakdown of statistics, during [democratic backsliding in the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_backsliding_in_the_United_States) via [visa and deportation controversies in the second Trump administration](79561481.md) part of [deportation in the second Trump administration](78314324.md), only publisized Turkish student involved in the controversies was [detention of Rümeysa Öztürk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detention_of_R%C3%BCmeysa_%C3%96zt%C3%BCrk) which her treatment received condemnation from elected officials and others for targeting students for their political views without due process. Rümeysa Öztürk published her article on March 26, 2024. Her detention was on March 25, 2025. [Marco Rubio](5502549.md) and [Hakan Fidan](39996069.md) met on March 25, 2025.[^cite_note-401] Turkey emphasized the importance of stability in Syria and the Balkans, while discussing efforts to end the Russia-Ukraine war and the need for a ceasefire in Gaza.[^cite_note-gaza25-402]

On March 25, 2025, in the Rubio-Fidan meeting, Turkey asked the U.S., using its influence on Israel, to address the need for increased efforts for a permanent ceasefire in the Gaza Strip, as adequate humanitarian aid is needed into the region, which is in dire condition.[^cite_note-gaza25-402] [Humanitarian aid during the Gaza war](76066918.md) became part of [Gaza genocide](75668427.md). Despite historically differing rhetoric between the two states, on Palestinians and Hamas, the Trump administration and Turkey found substantial alignment on a diplomatic resolution, which cultivated the [2025 Gaza Peace Summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Gaza_Peace_Summit). Turkey officially transitioned into a central guarantor nation for the Gaza peace process.[^cite_note-2025gaza-403] The U.S. actively tapped Turkey to help convince Hamas to adhere to the plan. U.S. envisioned a major role for Ankara in Gaza's reconstruction and the proposed [International Stabilization Force](81225292.md). This cooperation served a mutual strategic interest: Washington wanted capable regional allies to absorb the security burden. Turkish Defense Ministry stated that the country's armed forces were experienced in peacekeeping operations and ready to take on any assigned mission, as long as Israel pulled back to pre-conflict borders \[last stage of the process]. During the negotiations, Turkey managed to reach Hamas groups thought to be holding hostages, which had previously been unreachable due to internal communication breakdowns.[^cite_note-2025gaza-403] Turkey, besides the release, was also involved in locating the remains of Israeli hostages believed to have died in Hamas custody.[^cite_note-2025gaza-403] Resolution of Israeli reservations about Turkey's military presence in Gaza was U.S. responsibility. The U.S. took Israel's position.

#### Russo-Ukrainian War (Truce, Straits)

President Erdoğan has expressed his willingness to collaborate with President Trump in achieving a truce that will end the Russia-Ukraine war.[^cite_note-2025Truce-382] At a security meeting, with Ukraine and 21 other nations, Turkey reiterated the importance of Russia and Ukraine signing a peace agreement until then, it will continue to implement Montreux Convention to block the passage of military vessels into the Black Sea, as it has done since Russian occupation of Ukraine started in February 2022.[^cite_note-404]

On October 22, 2025, the United States imposed [sanctions](70323386.md) against Russia's largest oil companies [Rosneft](1313566.md) and [Lukoil](1027881.md). The U.S. also threatened [secondary sanctions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secondary_sanctions) against foreign financial institutions and companies that continue to do business with Rosneft and Lukoil,[^cite_note-405] which would affect their customers in Turkey.[^cite_note-406]

#### Alliances (Mecca, NATO)

In March 2026, during the [2026 Iran war](82533582.md), in which the U.S. was part, NATO air defense systems successfully intercepted [a ballistic missile fired from Iran](82623663.md) to Turkey.[^cite_note-407] NATO did not invoke Article 5 but declared it was ready, even though the NATO interception was proof of acceptance of belligerency.[^cite_note-408]

In August 2026, Israeli says Turkey's NATO protection (Article 5) does not apply to Turkish troops in Syria if there is a hot confrontation between Turkey and Israel. [^cite_note-409] Israel Katz warned Turkey not to test Israel's resolve, accusing Turkey of dragging itself into dangerous adventures in Syria by developing a functioning Syrian state \[with security forces].[^cite_note-410] Would NATO Allies consider whether they have an obligation to protect Turkey if Turkey covered attacks are “deemed to include” the attack to itself. Does a deliberate strike on non-territorial space constitute an attack?[^cite_note-411] For example, [K-329 Belgorod](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_submarine_Belgorod) sinks the [USS Gerald R. Ford](4050542.md) in non-territorial space of the U.S., dropped anchor at [Gölcük Naval Base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G%C3%B6lc%C3%BCk_Naval_Base), as Russia was responsing to Turkish beligerincy. Russia tested the waters by killing Turkish soldiers. In February 2020, when [Russian aircraft carried out or directed to](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Balyun_airstrikes) kill 33 Turkish soldiers in Syria’s Idlib province, Turkey invoked Article 4. When a country feels that its territorial integrity, political independence, or security is threatened, Article 4 serves as a step for Article 5. NATO condemned the strikes; no Article 5 invocation. Turkey developed the response alone through [Operation Spring Shield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Spring_Shield). Turkey tested the waters. Russia, perceived as only a sponsor, did not declare war on Turkey. Friction, including defense spending and disputes over conflicts such as the war in Iran \[the closure of the Strait of Hormuz prompted extensive diplomatic and defense discussions at NATO], has severely tested unity. It is an unknown territory for the rest of NATO \[non-US members] when Turkey was vanquished under a U.S.–Israel alliance while the rest of NATO watched. Erdogan to Netanyahu: Turkey won’t stay silent as Israel \[again] plunges region into bloodbath.[^cite_note-412]

Turkey's 21st-century foreign policy shifted from reliance on Cold War-era global bloc pacts to flexible regional pacts. Ankara has adopted a doctrine of "[strategic autonomy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strategic_autonomy)" and "multi-alignment."[^cite_note-413] Seeking toward a coherent regional strategy, as the American foreign policy aligned increasingly with Isreal rather than reginal security needs. [Mowing the grass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mowing_the_grass) strained every dimension of relations. Israel forcing a regional reordering: actions involving Syria and escalating rhetoric with Turkey have placed Israel at odds with broader regional and international consensus.

In August, the [Mecca Joint Defence Agreement](83903032.md) was a defense pact between Pakistan, Saudi Arabia, and Turkey. The pact's ratification was officially announced amidst the [2026 Iran war](82533582.md). US praised the agreement, calling it a "Big, bold, and important first step" and stating he was "very happy" to see the pact signed as a sign of regional self-reliance.[^cite_note-414]

### Military Industrial Procurement

[Since Johnson letter](#Greece_\(Cyprus,_Johnson_letter\)), the [defense industry of Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defense_industry_of_Turkey) is growing.

[The arms embargo](#Greece_\(Cyprus,_Arms_embargo\)) by the US in 1975–1978 following the Cyprus invasion necessitated Turkey developing a defense industry based on national resources.[^cite_note-416]

[During First Trump administration](#Air_Defense_\(Patriot_—_S-400,_F35,_CAATSA\)), bilateral relations between Turkey and the United States further deteriorated after the passage of the [National Defense Authorization Act for Fiscal Year 2019](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Defense_Authorization_Act_for_Fiscal_Year_2019), with an amendment added by Senator [John McCain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_McCain) requiring the Trump administration to submit a detailed report to Congress on the status of US–Turkey relations. The Department of Defense (DOD) submitted a mostly classified report to Congress in November 2018 followed by H.R. 648 which required the DOD report on the issue in 2019.[^cite_note-417] On December 14, 2020, the U.S. government decided to apply sanctions on Turkey, a NATO member, for disregarding the [CAATSA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Countering_America's_Adversaries_Through_Sanctions_Act) law of 2017, following Ankara's decision to purchase the [S-400 missile system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S-400_missile_system) from Russia. Turkey was also excluded from the [F-35 Joint Strike Fighter](11812.md) program on July 17, 2019, citing risks associated with the connection of a potential Russian intelligence gathering platform with advanced radars and sensors to NATO's networks.[^cite_note-DefenseOne_17.07.2019-418][^cite_note-CNBC_14.12.2020-419] The U.S. also imposed restrictions against the [Turkish Defence Industry Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defence_Industry_Agency).[^cite_note-420][^cite_note-421]

F-16D / F35 Production in Turkey





Turkish Air Force F-16D

#### F-16 Sale & Production

Turkey's 240 Lockheed Martin [General Dynamics F-16 Fighting Falcons](11642.md) were co-produced in Turkey by one of [Turkish Aerospace Industries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_Aerospace_Industries)' predecessors (TAI). The United States and Turkey signed an FMS contract in 2009 for 30 F-16 Block 50s to be co-produced by TAI.[^cite_note-Procurement,_Turkey_2010-422]

Alleged cable leaks highlighted Turkish concerns that upgrades to [General Dynamics F-16 Fighting Falcons](11642.md) had "precluded Turkish access to computer systems and software modification previously allowed".[^cite_note-423]

#### F-35 Sale & Production

Turkey was a Level 3 partner in the [F-35](11812.md) [Joint Strike Fighter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint_Strike_Fighter) program until 2019, when it was removed following its purchase of the [S-400 missile system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S-400_missile_system) from Russia, despite the [CAATSA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CAATSA) law of 2017. The [Turkish Air Force](1799517.md) and [Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_Navy) were to acquire the [F-35A](11812.md#F-35A) and [F-35B](11812.md#F-35B) variants.

[Turkey paid \$1.4 billion for procuring F-35A aircraft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lockheed_Martin_F-35_Lightning_II_procurement#Turkey), with six being delivered to [Luke Air Force Base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luke_Air_Force_Base) in Arizona, where Turkish pilots received training until the country's removal from the F-35 program in 2019.[^cite_note-424][^cite_note-425][^cite_note-Luke_AFB_1-426][^cite_note-Luke_AFB_2-427] The six F-35A aircraft built for Turkey were formally added to the inventory of the [Turkish Air Force](1799517.md) with code numbers 18–0001 to 18–0006,[^cite_note-Luke_AFB_1-426][^cite_note-Mavi_Savunma-428][^cite_note-Luke_AFB_2-427] but they were never allowed to leave the United States and are still kept inside hangars, for which the U.S. government demands rent payment from Turkey.[^cite_note-Mavi_Savunma-428][^cite_note-429]

Turkey, a NATO member, was one of eight countries—along with the United Kingdom, Canada, Netherlands, Italy, Denmark, Norway, and Australia—partnering with the United States in the [F-35](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F-35) [Joint Strike Fighter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint_Strike_Fighter) program.[^cite_note-430] Turkey planned to purchase up to 116 F-35s (100 F-35As for the [Turkish Air Force](1799517.md) and 16 F-35Bs for the [Turkish Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_Navy)), with 90 to be delivered over an estimated 10-year period (2014–2023) and to be jointly assembled and/or developed by firms from the various JSF partners. The cost was estimated to be at least \$11 billion and reportedly could have exceeded \$15 billion, given continued cost inflation which kept increasing the unit price of the aircraft. [The Pentagon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Pentagon) decided to end Turkey's partnership in the F-35 program on July 17, 2019, due to the latter's purchase of the [S-400 missile system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S-400_missile_system) from [Rosoboronexport](3094695.md), a Russian state agency in the [CAATSA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CAATSA) sanctions list.[^cite_note-CNBC_14.12.2020-419] The Pentagon also cited risks associated with the connection of a potential Russian intelligence gathering platform with advanced radars and sensors to NATO's networks, and the possibility that it could be secretly used by Russia to obtain information about the F-35's [stealth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stealth_technology) characteristics such as its [radar signature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radar_signature).[^cite_note-DefenseOne_17.07.2019-418]

#### Surveillance (Drone)

Turkey reportedly wanted to purchase drone aircraft from the United States to assist in its counterterrorism efforts against the [PKK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PKK) before its request was denied.[^cite_note-Procurement,_Turkey_2010-422] Turkey produced [Bayraktar Tactical UAS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bayraktar_Tactical_UAS).

### Military Operations



The Turkish [Provincial Reconstruction Team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Provincial_Reconstruction_Team) (PRT) in the [Wardak Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wardak_Province) of [Afghanistan](737.md) is part of the [U.S. led](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_armed_forces)–[International Security Assistance Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Security_Assistance_Force) (ISAF).

Turkey participated with the United States in the [Korean War](16772.md) in 1950–53 and in missions in Somalia, Kosovo, and [Bosnia and Herzegovina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NATO_intervention_in_Bosnia_and_Herzegovina) in 1992–2004.[^cite_note-431]

Turkey has commanded the [International Security Assistance Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Security_Assistance_Force) (ISAF) in Afghanistan twice since its inception.[^cite_note-Jim_Zanotti_2011,_page_17-432] 2,000 [Mehmetçik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mehmet%C3%A7ik) concentrated on training Afghan military and security forces and provided security at ISAF's Regional Command-Capital stationed in Kabul.[^cite_note-Jim_Zanotti_2011,_page_17-432] An undisclosed number of Mehmetçik were deployed to the Wardak and Jawzjan provinces to give ground support to USA Air Force Operations.[^cite_note-Jim_Zanotti_2011,_page_17-432]

During the [Iraq War](5043324.md), Turkey established the NATO Training Mission in 2005 and sponsored specialized training for hundreds of Iraqi security personnel in a secret facility in Turkey.[^cite_note-433]

#### Joint Exercises

For the Anatolian Falcon 2012 joint exercises, the United States sent the [480th Fighter Squadron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/480th_Fighter_Squadron) to train with Turkish pilots in the operation [Suppression of Enemy Air Defenses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suppression_of_Enemy_Air_Defenses).[^cite_note-434]

#### Operation Gladio

[Operation Gladio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Gladio) is the codename for a clandestine "[stay-behind](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stay-behind)" operation of armed resistance that was planned by the [Western Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Union_\(alliance\)) (WU) (and subsequently by [NATO](21133.md)) for a potential [Warsaw Pact](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warsaw_Pact) invasion and conquest in Europe.[^cite_note-Haberman_Times_1990-435]

[Counter-Guerrilla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Counter-Guerrilla) is the branch of the operation. The operation's founding goal was to erect a [guerrilla force](12720.md) capable of countering a possible [Soviet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Union) invasion. The goal was soon expanded to subverting [communism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communism) in Turkey. Counter-Guerrilla initially operated out of the [Turkish Armed Forces](30205.md)' [Tactical Mobilization Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tactical_Mobilization_Group) (STK). In 1967, it was renamed to the [Special Warfare Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Warfare_Department) before becoming [Special Forces Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Forces_\(Turkish_Armed_Forces\)). Counter-Guerrilla's existence in Turkey was revealed in 1973 by then-prime minister [Bülent Ecevit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B%C3%BClent_Ecevit).[^cite_note-436]

### Military Cooperation

The United States and Turkey share membership in [NATO](21133.md), the [Organization for Security and Co-operation in Europe](22796.md) (OSCE), and continue to cooperate in important projects, such as the [Joint Strike Fighter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint_Strike_Fighter) program.

Since 1954, Turkey has hosted the [Incirlik Air Base](1597543.md), an important operations base of the United States Air Force, which has played a critical role during the [Cold War](325329.md), the [Gulf War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_War), and the [Iraq War](5043324.md). Turkey routinely hosts the United States for Anatolian Falcon and (with Israel, before their relationship worsened) Anatolian Eagle exercises held at its Konya airbase.[^cite_note-437]

Turkish bases and transport corridors have been used heavily for military operations in Iraq, Afghanistan, and Libya as of 2011.[^cite_note-crs.gov-24]

In the [2016 Turkish coup d'état attempt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Turkish_coup_d'%C3%A9tat_attempt), some of the planes used at the operation and a fueling carrier took off from Incirlik base; in response, the Turkish government arrested several high-ranking Turkish military officers at Incirlik and cut power to the base for nearly a week.[^cite_note-Arms_Control_TODAY-438]

#### Nuclear warheads

Turkey hosts U.S. controlled nuclear weapons as part of [nuclear sharing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_sharing) policy. Its current arsenal is [B61 nuclear bomb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B61_nuclear_bomb), while it formerly held [MGR-1 Honest John](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MGR-1_Honest_John), [MIM-14 Nike Hercules](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MIM-14_Nike_Hercules), [PGM-19 Jupiter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PGM-19_Jupiter), [W33](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/W33_\(nuclear_warhead\)) and [W48](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/W48) artillery shells.

Turkey does not have dedicated nuclear-capable fighter aircraft that can deliver the weapons and does not train its pilots to fly nuclear missions.[^cite_note-Arms_Control_TODAY-438]

#### Radar and signal analysis

To have the [Terminal High Altitude Area Defense](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terminal_High_Altitude_Area_Defense) be approved, Turkey received two conditions:[^cite_note-439] Iran or Syria should not be named as a threat to Turkey, and Turkey's territory was to be protected by the system (as a national defense requirement). According to U.S. officials, the [AN/TPY-2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AN/TPY-2) radar was deployed at Turkey's [Kürecik Air Force base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K%C3%BCrecik_Radar_Station) and activated in January 2012.[^cite_note-440][^cite_note-441]

### Military Milestones

- 1932: General [Douglas MacArthur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Douglas_MacArthur), then-[Chief of Staff of the U.S. Army](485507.md), visited Turkey and met with [Atatürk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mustafa_Kemal_Atat%C3%BCrk).

  [^cite_note-443]

- 1943: The [Second Cairo Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Cairo_Conference) of December 4–6, 1943 addressed Turkey's possible contribution to the [Allies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allies_of_World_War_II) in [World War II](32927.md). The meeting was attended by President [Franklin D. Roosevelt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franklin_D._Roosevelt) of the [United States](3434750.md), Prime Minister [Winston Churchill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Winston_Churchill) of the [United Kingdom](31717.md), and President [İsmet İnönü](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C4%B0smet_%C4%B0n%C3%B6n%C3%BC) of the [Republic of Turkey](11125639.md).

- 1950: The [Turkish Brigade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_Brigade), codenamed *North Star*, was a military formation from [Turkey](11125639.md) that served under the [United Nations Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Command) during the [Korean War](16772.md). The brigade's first 5,000 Turkish troops arrived on October 19, 1950, and remained in varying strengths until the summer of 1954. It was attached to the [25th Infantry Division](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/25th_Infantry_Division_\(United_States\)) of the United States.

- 1952: Turkey joined [NATO](21133.md) on February 18, 1952.

- 1954: The United States and Turkey signed the first status of forces agreement.

  [^cite_note-Jim_Zanotti_2011,_page_37-444]

- 1980: U.S.–Turkey Defense and Economic Cooperation Agreement.

  [^cite_note-Jim_Zanotti_2011,_page_37-444]

- 1987: [Turkish Aerospace Industries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_Aerospace_Industries) began producing [F-16C and F-16D fighter aircraft](11642.md) under license.

- 1992: [TCG *Muavenet*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TCG_Muavenet_\(DM_357\)) was hit by two [Sea Sparrow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sea_Sparrow) missiles fired from the [aircraft carrier](2219.md) [USS *Saratoga*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USS_Saratoga_\(CV-60\)) during NATO's "Exercise Display Determination 1992" in [Saros Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saros_Bay), [Turkey](11125639.md), on October 2, 1992, resulting in five deaths and 22 injuries among its crew.

- 1995: The [Turkish Air Force](1799517.md) took part in NATO's [Operation Deliberate Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Deliberate_Force).

- 1999: The Turkish Air Force took part in NATO's [Operation Allied Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Allied_Force).

- 2003: The [Turkish Parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_Parliament) denies permission for the ground invasion of Iraq from Turkey, but permits the use of Turkish air bases for overflight.

  [^cite_note-Jim_Zanotti_2011,_page_37-444]

- 2003: U.S. forces detained Turkish special forces troops in Suleimaniyah, Iraq.

  [^cite_note-Jim_Zanotti_2011,_page_37-444]

- 2011: The Turkish Air Force and [Turkish Naval Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_Naval_Forces) took part in NATO's [Operation Unified Protector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Unified_Protector).

  [^cite_note-Jim_Zanotti_2011,_page_37-444]

## Economic relations

See also: [Economy of Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Turkey) and [Economy of the United States](32022.md)



U.S. trade deficit (in billions, goods only) by country in 2014



[Istanbul](3391396.md) is the [largest city in Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_largest_cities_in_Europe)

[^cite_note-446]

&#x20;and the chief financial and economical center of Turkey. Istanbul ([Constantinople](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constantinople)) was the capital of the [Roman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Empire) (330–395), [Byzantine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Byzantine_Empire) (395–1204 and 1261–1453), [Latin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin_Empire) (1204–1261) and [Ottoman](22278.md) (1453–1922) Empires.



[New York](645042.md) is the [largest city in the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_United_States_cities_by_population) and the world's principal [financial center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Financial_center).

The United States and Turkey are both members in the [Organisation for Economic Co-operation and Development](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organisation_for_Economic_Co-operation_and_Development) (OECD) and the [G-20](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G-20_major_economies). The US and Turkey have had a Joint Economic Commission and a Trade and Investment Framework Agreement for several years. In 2002, the two countries indicated their joint intent to upgrade bilateral economic relations by launching an Economic Partnership Commission.

Turkey is currently the 32nd-largest goods trading partner with \$20.5 billion in total (\$10.2 billion; imports \$10.3 billion) goods trade during 2018. US' goods and services trade with Turkey totaled an estimated \$24.0 billion (exports: \$12.7 billion; imports: \$11.2 billion) in 2017.[^cite_note-trade-447] The trade deficit was \$143 million in 2018.[^cite_note-trade-447]

The US exports of goods and services to Turkey involved 68,000 jobs in 2015.[^cite_note-trade-447]

## Culture & Media

### Midnight Express (1978 film)

The 1978 American semi-biographical film [*Midnight Express*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midnight_Express_\(film\)) was [banned in Turkey](9790999.md) under [Article 301 of the Turkish Penal Code](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Article_301_\(Turkish_Penal_Code\)), which caused a strain on U.S.–Turkish relations. The movie was widely perceived in Turkey as a revenge for the [Turkish invasion of Cyprus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_invasion_of_Cyprus) in 1974 and the establishment of the self-declared [Turkish Federated State of Cyprus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_Federated_State_of_Cyprus) in 1975, recognized only by Turkey. It was filmed almost entirely at [Fort Saint Elmo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Saint_Elmo) in [Valletta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valletta), [Malta](19137.md), which had played an important role during the [Great Siege of Malta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Siege_of_Malta) (1565) by the [Ottoman Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottoman_Navy). The selection of this symbolic location, despite the existence of more economical alternatives closer to or within the United States, led to the perception that it conveyed a veiled warning message to Turkey regarding the fate of Cyprus.[^cite_note-448] Most of the [vilified Turkish characters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midnight_Express_\(film\)#Allegations_of_Turkophobia) in the movie were portrayed by Greek and Armenian actors. Both within Turkey and Iran, the film generated comparisons with the anti-Iranian movie *[Not Without My Daughter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Not_Without_My_Daughter_\(film\))*. However, many creators associated with *Midnight Express* later apologized the film's wholesale vilification of Turkish people.[^cite_note-449]

### Valley of the Wolves (2003 TV series)

[Valley of the Wolves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valley_of_the_Wolves:_Iraq) sparked intense interest to the point that the US ambassador to Ankara and Turkey's foreign minister exchanged positions on the fictional TV show. Both state officials were anxious to appear conciliatory, however they shared their opinions and perspectives on massacres of civilians at a wedding party, and multiple summary executions, and most controversial was on the depiction of the Abu Ghraib prison.[^cite_note-450] Abu Ghraib prison became famous by [Abu Ghraib torture and prisoner abuse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abu_Ghraib_torture_and_prisoner_abuse).

### Turks renowned for their accomplishments in the United States









Turks renowned for their accomplishments in the United States include [Ahmet Ertegun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmet_Ertegun) (1923–2006), the founding chairman of [Atlantic Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantic_Records) between 1947 and 2006. He discovered and championed many leading [rhythm and blues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rhythm_and_blues) and [rock](25423.md) musicians. Ertegun also wrote classic [blues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blues) and [pop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Popular_music) songs. He has been described as "one of the most significant figures in the modern recording industry."[^cite_note-451] Ertegun served as the chairman of the [Rock and Roll Hall of Fame](58902.md) and museum, located in [Cleveland, Ohio](5951.md). In 2017 he was inducted into the [Rhythm and Blues Music Hall of Fame](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rhythm_and_Blues_Music_Hall_of_Fame) in recognition of his work in the music business. Ertegun helped foster ties between the United States and Turkey, his birthplace. He served as the chairman of the [American Turkish Society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Turkish_Society) for over 20 years until his death.[^cite_note-452] He also co-founded the [New York Cosmos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Cosmos_\(1971%E2%80%931985\)) [soccer](10568.md) team of the [original North American Soccer League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_American_Soccer_League_\(1968%E2%80%931984\)).

[Mehmet Oz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mehmet_Oz) (born June 11, 1960), also known as *Dr. Oz*, is a Turkish-American [television presenter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Television_presenter), physician, author, professor emeritus of [cardiothoracic surgery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cardiothoracic_surgery) at [Columbia University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Columbia_University), former political candidate, and President [Donald Trump](4848272.md)'s nominee to serve as administrator of the [Centers for Medicare & Medicaid Services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centers_for_Medicare_&_Medicaid_Services) during his [second presidency](78298108.md). He was previously a member of the [President's Council on Sports, Fitness, and Nutrition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President's_Council_on_Sports,_Fitness,_and_Nutrition) between 2018 and 2022. The son of Turkish immigrants, Oz was raised in [Wilmington, Delaware](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilmington,_Delaware), and graduated from [Harvard University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harvard_University) and the [University of Pennsylvania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Pennsylvania). He subsequently began his residency in surgery at [Columbia University Irving Medical Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Columbia_University_Irving_Medical_Center) in 1986. In 2001, Oz became a professor of surgery at [Columbia University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Columbia_University), and later retired to professor emeritus in 2018. In 2003, [Oprah Winfrey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oprah_Winfrey) was the first guest on the [Discovery Channel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Discovery_Channel) series *Second Opinion with Dr. Oz*, and he was a regular guest on *[The Oprah Winfrey Show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Oprah_Winfrey_Show)*, making more than sixty appearances. In 2009, *[The Dr. Oz Show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Dr._Oz_Show)*, a daily television program about medical matters and health, was launched by Winfrey's [Harpo Productions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harpo_Productions) and [Sony Pictures Television](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sony_Pictures_Television), running for 13 seasons. Oz ran in the [2022 U.S. Senate election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_United_States_Senate_election_in_Pennsylvania) in [Pennsylvania](23332.md) as a conservative [Republican](32070.md), the [first Muslim candidate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Muslim_members_of_the_United_States_Congress) for Senate to be nominated by either major party. Oz lost the election to the [Democratic](5043544.md) nominee [John Fetterman](5303368.md).

[Aziz Sancar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aziz_Sancar) (born September 8, 1946) is a Turkish [molecular biologist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Molecular_biologist) specializing in [DNA repair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DNA_repair), [cell cycle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cell_cycle) checkpoints, and [circadian clock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circadian_clock).[^cite_note-453][^cite_note-454] In 2015, he was awarded the [Nobel Prize in Chemistry](25416893.md) along with [Tomas Lindahl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tomas_Lindahl) and [Paul L. Modrich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_L._Modrich) for their mechanistic studies of DNA repair.[^cite_note-NYT-20151007-wjb-455][^cite_note-NP-20151007-456] He has made contributions on [photolyase](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Photolyase) and [nucleotide excision repair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nucleotide_excision_repair) in bacteria that have changed his field. Sancar is currently the [Sarah Graham Kenan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarah_Graham_Kenan) Professor of Biochemistry and Biophysics at the [University of North Carolina School of Medicine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_North_Carolina_School_of_Medicine) and a member of the [UNC Lineberger Comprehensive Cancer Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNC_Lineberger_Comprehensive_Cancer_Center).[^cite_note-457] He is the co-founder of the Aziz & Gwen Sancar Foundation, which is a non-profit organization to promote [Turkish culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_culture) and to support Turkish students in the United States.[^cite_note-CarolinaTürkEvi-458][^cite_note-459]

[Şafak Pavey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%9Eafak_Pavey) (born July 10, 1976)[^cite_note-ohchr.bio-460] is a Turkish diplomat, columnist and politician. She was a member of the [Turkish Grand National Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_Grand_National_Assembly) from the main opposition [Republican People's Party](404748.md) (CHP) representing [Istanbul Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Istanbul_Province).[^cite_note-HDN2-461] She is the first disabled woman ever elected to the Turkish parliament, and is a member of the [United Nations](31769.md) [Committee on the Rights of Persons with Disabilities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Committee_on_the_Rights_of_Persons_with_Disabilities).[^cite_note-USSD1-462][^cite_note-USSD2-463] In 1996, before she turned 20, her left arm and left leg were [amputated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amputation) after a train accident in [Switzerland](26748.md). One year later, she went to [London](17867.md), [United Kingdom](31717.md), to pursue her education. She studied international relations at the [University of Westminster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Westminster) and completed her post-graduate studies at the [London School of Economics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/London_School_of_Economics).[^cite_note-3QD-464] In 2012, Pavey was honored by the [United States Department of State](31975.md) with the [International Women of Courage Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Women_of_Courage_Award).[^cite_note-USSD2-463][^cite_note-HDN1-465]

## Public relations



[Turkish House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_House) in [Manhattan](45470.md), [NY](645042.md)



Founded in 1863, [Robert College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_College) in [Istanbul](3391396.md) is the oldest continuously operating American school outside the US

### Turkish House

The [Turkish House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_House) (also called *Türkevi Center*) is a 561-foot-high (171 m), 36-floor [skyscraper](29485.md) located at 821 United Nations Plaza ([First Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Avenue_\(Manhattan\))) in [Turtle Bay, Manhattan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turtle_Bay,_Manhattan), [New York City](645042.md), United States, across from the [headquarters of the United Nations](188955.md).[^cite_note-466] Turkish House serves as the headquarters of multiple [Turkish diplomatic missions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_diplomatic_missions_of_Turkey) in New York City, as well as a center of Turkish cultural activity.

Designed by [Perkins Eastman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Perkins_Eastman), it has 36 stories and reaches a height of 561 feet (171 m) from the ground to the roof.[^cite_note-New_York_YIMBY_2021-467][^cite_note-The_Architect's_Newspaper_2022-468] The building contains about 220,000 square feet (20,000 m<sup>2</sup>) of usable space, of which 180,000 square feet (17,000 m<sup>2</sup>) is used by the [Permanent Mission of Turkey to the United Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Permanent_Mission_of_Turkey_to_the_United_Nations) and the Consulate General of Turkey in New York City; the rest is residential space. The curved facade is an allusion to the [crescent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Star_and_crescent) on the [flag of Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flag_of_Turkey), while the top of the skyscraper is shaped like a [tulip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tulip), the [national flower of Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_symbols_of_Turkey#National_flower).[^cite_note-The_Architect's_Newspaper_2022-468] Construction works commenced in September 2017[^cite_note-Warerkar_2017-469][^cite_note-The_Architect's_Newspaper_2022-468] and the building was largely completed by May 2021.[^cite_note-New_York_YIMBY_2021-467]

### American schools in Turkey

Established in 1863 by [Christopher Robert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christopher_Robert), a wealthy American philanthropist, and [Cyrus Hamlin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyrus_Hamlin_\(missionary\)), a [missionary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Missionary) devoted to education, [Robert College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_College) in [Istanbul](3391396.md) is the oldest continuously operating American school outside the [United States](3434750.md).[^cite_note-RobertCollege-470][^cite_note-library.columbia.edu-471] Six years after its foundation, with the permission ([Ottoman Turkish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottoman_Turkish_language): *[irade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irade-i_seniyye)*) of the [Ottoman Sultan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottoman_Sultan), the first campus (currently housing [Boğaziçi University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bo%C4%9Fazi%C3%A7i_University)) was built in [Bebek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bebek,_Istanbul) at the ridge of the [Rumeli Castle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rumelihisar%C4%B1). Hamlin, who became the first president of Robert College, was preoccupied with the construction of the campus such that [George Washburn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Washburn_\(educator\)) acted as the *de facto* head of the college from 1871 onwards. In 1877, he was officially named president by the trustees. During his tenure between 1877 and 1903, Washburn "gradually assembled a faculty of distinguished scholars who firmly established the college's academic reputation."[^cite_note-ColumbiaRobert-472][^cite_note-473]

[Christopher Robert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christopher_Robert) died in 1878, leaving a significant portion of his wealth to the college.[^cite_note-news2-474] In that same year, a college catalog was compiled, providing general information and an outline of the courses of study.[^cite_note-ColumbiaRobert-472] Defining the aims of the college, the catalog stated: "The object of the College is to give to its students, without distinction of race or religion, a thorough educational equal in all respects to that obtained at a first-class American college and based upon the same general principles."

After [George Washburn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Washburn_\(educator\)), Robert College was administrated by [Caleb Frank Gates Sr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caleb_Frank_Gates#Early_life_and_education) (1903–1932). The school adopted a strictly secular educational model in accordance with the republican principles of Turkey and [Atatürk's reforms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atat%C3%BCrk's_reforms) since 1923. Robert College, at various points of its existence, had [junior high school](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Junior_high_school), [high school](554992.md), and [university](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University) sections under the names *Robert Academy*, *Robert Yüksek* and *American College for Girls*. Since 1971, the present-day Robert College functions only as a [high school](554992.md)[^cite_note-475] on its [Arnavutköy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arnavutk%C3%B6y) campus (formerly the campus of *American College for Girls*), yet it retains the title of "College". The [Bebek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bebek,_Istanbul) campus was turned over to the [Republic of Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_Turkey) for use as a public university named [Boğaziçi University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bo%C4%9Fazi%C3%A7i_University), the renamed continuation of Robert College's university section.

### Lobbying and think tanks



4th Annual Turkic American Convention

The [Turkish lobby in the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_lobby_in_the_United_States) is a lobby that works on behalf of the Turkish government to promote the nation's interests with the US government. The [Turkish Coalition of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_Coalition_of_America) (TCA) is an educational, congressional advocacy, and charitable organization that was incorporated in February 2007.

The [Office of Defense Cooperation Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Office_of_Defense_Cooperation_Turkey) is a United States Security Assistance Organization working on issues related to Turkey.

### Opinion Surveys

According to a survey conducted in the spring of 2017 and released in August, 72% of Turks see the United States as a threat to Turkey's security. Furthermore, the US was perceived as a greater threat to security than Russia or China.[^cite_note-478] According to PBS, opinions of the US dropped steadily from 1999/2000 (52% in Turkey in 1999/2000) and in 2006, favorable opinions dropped significantly in predominantly Muslim countries, which ranged from 12% in Turkey to 30% in Indonesia and Egypt.[^cite_note-479] A 2019 survey conducted by the [Pew Research Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pew_Research_Center) showed 73% of Turks had a [negative view of the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-American_sentiment_in_Turkey), with only 20% having a positive view, the lowest among countries polled.[^cite_note-Global_Indicators_Database-480] The same study also showed only 11% of Turks had confidence in the US leader at the time of the survey, President [Donald Trump](4848272.md), with 84% having no confidence in him.[^cite_note-Global_Indicators_Database-480] A 2024 survey conducted by the Pew Research Center showed only 8% of Turks had confidence in US President [Joe Biden](145422.md).[^cite_note-481] According to a 2026 [Pew Research Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pew_Research_Center) survey, 13% of Turks had a favorable view of the United States, while 84% had a negative view.[^cite_note-\:62-482]

The following histogram shows the percentage of Turks that viewed the United States favorably according to the PEW Global Attitudes Survey:[^cite_note-Global_Indicators_Database-480]

#### 1999 Clinton visit

President Bill Clinton visited Ankara, İzmit, Ephesus and Istanbul on November 15–19, 1999. It was an official state visit during which he also attended the [Organization for Security and Cooperation in Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organization_for_Security_and_Cooperation_in_Europe)'s (OSCE) summit meeting.

#### 2009 Obama visit





Relations between Turkey and the United States markedly improved during the [Obama administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Obama_administration)'s first term, but the two countries were nevertheless unable to reach their ambitious goals.[^cite_note-486] Obama made his first official visit to Turkey at [Ankara](802.md) and [Istanbul](3391396.md) April 6–7, 2009. There US critics who claimed that Turkey should not be rewarded by an early presidential visit as its government had been systematically reorienting foreign policy onto an [Islamist](15012.md) axis. Former [U.S. Ambassador to Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Ambassador_to_Turkey) Mark Parris remarked: "Whatever the merits of this argument, the Obama administration, by scheduling the visit, have decisively rejected it."[^cite_note-TZ090502a-487]

Turkish President Gül later referred to the visit as "evidence of a vital partnership between Turkey and the US," whilst [Turkish Foreign Minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_Foreign_Minister) [Ahmet Davutoğlu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmet_Davuto%C4%9Flu) pointed out that they were "changing the psychological atmosphere" of what was before "seen as a military relationship".[^cite_note-TZ090603b-488] Obama clarified: "We are not solely strategic partners, we are also model partners." With this change in terminology, "The President wanted to stress the uniqueness of this relationship. This is not an ordinary relationship, it's a prototype and unique relationship."[^cite_note-TZ090604-489] A [US House Committee on Foreign Affairs](1267657.md) hearing, *The United States and Turkey: A Model Partnership*, chaired by Head of the Subcommittee on Europe [Robert Wexler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Wexler) was convened after "the historic visit that Obama paid to Turkey", and concluded that "this cooperation is vital for both of the two states in an environment in which we face serious security issues in Afghanistan, Iraq, Iran, the Balkans, Black Sea, Caucuses and the Middle East, besides a global financial crisis".[^cite_note-TZ090516-490]

After Obama's visit, Turkish Prime Minister [Recep Tayyip Erdoğan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recep_Tayyip_Erdo%C4%9Fan) and [Chief of the Turkish General Staff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_of_the_Turkish_General_Staff) [İlker Başbuğ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C4%B0lker_Ba%C5%9Fbu%C4%9F) hosted US [Chairman of the Joint Chiefs of Staff](364458.md) Admiral [Mike Mullen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Mullen) in Ankara. During the closed-door meeting, they discussed the pledging of further Turkish support troops to [Afghanistan](737.md) and Pakistan where Turkish authorities have influence, the secure transport of troops and equipment from the port of [İskenderun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C4%B0skenderun) during the [withdrawal of U.S. troops from Iraq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Withdrawal_of_U.S._troops_from_Iraq_\(2007%E2%80%932011\)), and the pro-Kurdish terrorists operating in south-eastern Turkey and northern Iraq.[^cite_note-TZ090501-491]

#### 2013 Erdoğan visit

Vice President Joe Biden and Secretary of State John Kerry participate in a lunch at the State Department honoring Prime Minister Erdogan of Turkey.

In May 2013, Erdoğan visited the White House and met with Obama, who said the visit was an opportunity "to return the extraordinary hospitality that the Prime Minister and the Turkish people showed \[him] on \[his] visit to Turkey four years ago".[^cite_note-\:0-492] During their joint press conference, both Obama and Erdoğan stressed the importance of achieving stability in Syria. Erdoğan said that during his time with Obama, "Syria was at the top of \[their] agenda" and Obama repeated the United States plan to support the [Assad](364813.md)-opposition while applying "steady international pressure".[^cite_note-\:0-492] When they were not discussing national security threats, Obama and Erdoğan discussed expanding economic relations between the two countries; Turkey had received over \$50 billion in foreign investments, \$20 billion of which came from the United States.[^cite_note-CNN2013-493] In 2003, there was only \$8 billion in U.S. investment in Turkey; both Erdoğan and Obama praised this recent increase and agreed to continue expanding the trade and investment agreements between the two countries.[^cite_note-\:0-492][^cite_note-CNN2013-493] Erdoğan's visit culminated with talks of stability in the region. Obama stressed the importance of normalizing relations between Turkey and [Israel](9282173.md) and praised the steps Erdoğan had taken in that process. The process normalizing the Turkish-Israeli relationship had begun and Erdoğan stated that he would continue this process: "We don't need any other problems, issues in the region."[^cite_note-\:0-492][^cite_note-494]

#### 2019 Erdoğan visit

In November 2019, Erdogan visited the White House and held meetings with U.S. President [Donald Trump](4848272.md).[^cite_note-495]

#### 2025 Erdoğan visit

On September 25, 2025, during a meeting in the [Oval Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oval_Office), [Donald Trump](4848272.md) praised Turkish President [Recep Tayyip Erdoğan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recep_Tayyip_Erdo%C4%9Fan), stating that Erdoğan "knows about rigged elections better than anybody." Referring to his own time out of office as "exile," Trump claimed they had remained friends throughout, despite what he called a "rigged" 2020 U.S. election. He described Erdoğan as "highly opinionated," adding, "Usually, I don’t like opinionated people, but I always like this one." The comments drew notice due to Erdoğan's leadership style, which has frequently been described by critics as increasingly autocratic. During the meeting, Trump hinted at lifting the ban on sale of F-35 to Turkey.[^cite_note-496][^cite_note-497][^cite_note-498]

### Extradition

The extradition relationship between the United States and Turkey is governed by a bilateral treaty signed in Ankara on June 7, 1979, which entered into force on January 1, 1981. This treaty expanded upon an earlier 1923 agreement, broadening the scope of extraditable offenses to include crimes such as narcotics trafficking, hijacking, bribery, and obstruction of justice.[^cite_note-499][^cite_note-500]

#### Legal framework and process

Under the 1979 treaty, both the U.S. and Turkey must look at extradition requests if the act in question is considered a crime in both countries. But there are protections in place—like the political offense rule—which lets a country refuse extradition if the crime is political. In the U.S., courts also review each request to make sure there is enough evidence to justify the extradition.[^cite_note-501]

#### Notable cases

| Name | Reason |
| --- | --- |
| Eylem Tok and Timur Cihantimur | Turkish novelist Eylam Tok and her 17-year-old son Timusr Cihantimur have fled from Turkey after Timur allegedly caused an accident, on March 1, 2024, resulting in one death and multiple injuries. They were arrested in the US and Turkey requested their extradition, charging Cihantimur with reckless killing and Tok with aiding his escape. In February 2025 a US magistrate judge ruled that the charges were covered under the extradition treaty, allowing the process to proceed.[^cite_note-502][^cite_note-503][^cite_note-504] |
| Fethullah Gülen | Fethullah Gülen is a Turkish cleric that lived in [Pennsylvania](23332.md). He was a major source of tension between the U.S. and Turkey. Turkey blamed him for the failed coup in 2016 and asked the U.S. many times to send him back. But U.S. officials refused, saying there was not enough evidence and that the charges seemed political. Gülen died in October 2024, bringing this issue to an end.[^cite_note-505][^cite_note-506] |
| Sezgin Baran Korkmaz | Sezgin Baran Korkmaz is a Turkish businessman who was arrested in Austria in June 2021. He was accused of helping to hide over \$133 million from a large fraud scheme involving fake U.S. biodiesel tax credits. Both the U.S. and Turkey wanted him extradited, but Austria chose to send him to the United States. In July 2022, he was sent to Utah to face charges of money laundering, wire fraud, and interfering with a legal process.[^cite_note-507][^cite_note-508][^cite_note-509] |

Since the PKK was listed [as a terrorist organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurdistan_Workers_Party/States_Listed_as_Terrorist) by the U.S. in 1997 and Öcalan was [captured with the CIA's](#Syria_\(Terrorism,_Adana_Agreement\)) help in 1999, the PKK splintered and unified under [Kurdistan Communities Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurdistan_Communities_Union) (KCK). Israel had openly voiced support for these non-PKK groups, like the U.S.<sup>[\[37\]](#cite_note-37)</sup> The relationship was based on the [alliance of the periphery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alliance_of_the_periphery). It targeted non-dominant, non-Arab minority groups such as the Kurdish population to counter or direct opposition to Israel's security challenges. Turkey registers them as unconventional and proxy warfare groups exploiting internal fault lines to weaken Turkey from within by diverting their resources, attention, and stability. Since 1984, more than 40,000 people have lost their lives just in Turkey.<sup>[\[38\]](#cite_note-38)</sup> The 40-year conflict has cost Turkey's economy an estimated \$1.8 trillion to \$2 trillion since 1984.<sup>[\[39\]](#cite_note-39)</sup> [History of the Kurdistan Workers' Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Kurdistan_Workers'_Party) showed that it evolved from an anti-imperialist and anti-American movement to eventually serving as an intermediary for U.S. regional objectives.
